A Contact Homotopy Type
Soham Chanda, Amanda Hirschi

Abstract
. Adapting the construction of global Kuranishi charts to the contact setting, we associate to any non-degenerate closed contact manifold a flow category based on Reeb orbits and moduli spaces of pseudo-holomorphic buildings. The construction is natural in the sense that to any exact symplectic cobordism we can associate a flow bimodule between the flow categories of its ends.



1.
Introduction

1.1.
Context

Contact manifolds are smooth manifolds equipped with a maximally integral distribution, their contact structure. Although such manifolds appear in the literature as early as
[
Lie72
]
, the systematic study of contact manifolds is younger than that of symplectic manifolds. While any contact manifold
(Y,\xi)
is odd-dimensional, given a contact form
\lambda
we can define the symplectization of
(Y,\lambda)
to be the product
(\mathbb{R}\times Y,d(e^{s}\lambda))
. While the symplectization is not a complete invariant of contact manifolds,
[
Cou14
]
, it allows many tools from symplectic geometry to be imported into contact topology, in particular, the theory of pseudo-holomorphic curves.
Early applications were constructions of symplectic capacities,
[
EH87
]
, and the Weinstein conjecture in dimension
3
,
[
Hof93
]
.

Symplectic field theory is the ambitious vision of
[
EGH00
]
, proposing an intricate algebraic framework based on moduli spaces of punctured pseudo-holomorphic curves. We refer to
[
HS24a
]
for a survey on the history and possible application of SFT; we could not do them justice here.
The main problem in realizing the SFT framework is the lack of transversality of the relevant moduli spaces. The basic theory and estimates were worked out in
[
HWZ96
,
HWZ95
]
, while compactness was established in
[
BEH+03
,
CM05
]
. The goal of a uniform theory to deal with the transversality issues motivated the development of polyfolds,
[
HWZ21
,
FFGW16
,
FH18
]
. A construction of Kuranishi charts for all genera was given by
[
Ish18
]
, but utilizing them to obtain algebraic invariants is yet to be done.

In genus zero, considering curves with one positive puncture, SFT postulates the existence of a Floer homology theory, called
contact homology
, with generators given by Reeb orbits. In
[
BH18
]
and
[
BH23
]
, Bao–Honda give constructions that are suitable for computations, cf.
[
Avd23
]
, while
[
Par19
]
gives a more abstract construction of contact homology using the framework developed in
[
Par16
]
. However, as in Hamiltonian Floer theory, contact homology uses only the information of rigid moduli spaces of curves, i.e., those of dimension zero, and of the existence of suitable moduli spaces in dimension one. Floer homotopy theory has the goal of extracting invariants also from higher-dimensional moduli spaces. It was proposed by
[
CJS95
]
in 1995 and reworked in the Morse–Bott context by
[
Zho24
,
CK23
,
Bon24
]
. Both
[
LT18
]
and
[
AB24
]
give different approaches, respectively, the latter placing greater emphasis on bordism theories. Flow categories and the associated homotopical structures have been constructed in Hamiltonian Floer theory,
[
BX22
,
Rez22
]
, symplectic cohomology,
[
Rez24
,
CK23
]
, Lagrangian Floer theory,
[
Lar21
,
PS24
,
BB25
]
as well as
[
LS14
]
in a somewhat different context. Apart from
[
TT25
]
, which uses generating families instead of pseudo-holomorphic curves, no Floer homotopy theoretic constructions have been given in the context of contact topology.

1.2.
Main results

The construction of our flow category relies fundamentally on the global Kuranishi charts for moduli spaces of punctured pseudo-holomorphic curves that we build in §
3
. To keep the notation light here, we summarize the background on contact topology in §
2.1
and define our pseudo-holomorphic curves and their degenerations in §
2.2
. For this introduction, let us simply say that we consider the SFT compactifications
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}_{\text{SFT}}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)
of moduli spaces of genus-zero
J
-holomorphic curves of relative homology class
\beta
in the symplectisation of a contact manifold
(Y,\lambda)
that are asymptotic at their positive and negative punctures to Reeb orbits in
\Gamma^{+}
and
\Gamma^{-}
respectively.

Theorem A

(Theorem
3.40
)
.

Let
(Y,\xi)
be a closed contact manifold equipped with a non-degenerate contact form
\lambda
. Suppose
J
is a
\lambda
-adapted almost complex structure on
(Y,\xi)
and
\Gamma^{\pm}
are finite sequences of Reeb orbits.

(1)

The moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}_{\text{SFT}}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)
admits a global Kuranishi chart with corners
\mathcal{K}
of the correct virtual dimension.

(2)

If
\Gamma^{+}
and
\Gamma^{-}
consist of good Reeb orbits, then the orientation line
\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{K}}
of
\mathcal{K}
is canonically isomorphic to
\mathfrak{o}(\mathbb{R})^{\vee}\otimes\bigotimes\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}\mathfrak{o}_{\gamma}\otimes\bigotimes\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{-}}\mathfrak{o}^{\vee}_{\gamma}
.

While much of the construction is similar to previous constructions of global Kuranishi charts, the crucial first step is different: the construction of very ample line bundles on the closed domain curves. The original idea in
[
AMS21
]
, somewhat rephrased in
[
HS24b
]
and
[
AMS24
]
, is to construct a very ample line bundle on the domain of pseudo-holomorphic curve by pulling back a sufficiently positive differential form, respectively, line bundle on the target manifold. In the contact case, where
Y
could have vanishing
H^{2}
and such a line bundle could only be pulled back to the punctured domain, this is not an option. However, in genus zero, all that is needed is the assignment of a positive degree to each irreducible component of a domain so that these degrees add correctly when a node is smoothed. This is carried out in §
3.2.1
.

Remark 1.1

(Technical remark)
.

The proof of Theorem
A
passes through the moduli spaces considered in
[
Par19
]
. In contrast to the compactifications usually considered in the SFT literature, these curves do not come with levels, that is, one quotients the maps on each irreducible component separately by the translation action on the target. In the process, we give a geometric way of recovering the usual moduli spaces considered in SFT from those used by
[
Par19
]
, which may be of independent interest.

Theorem
A
does not assume
\Gamma^{+}
to be a single Reeb orbit. Hence, our construction yields the foundations necessary for rational SFT as outlined in
[
Lat22
]
and used in
[
Sie19
,
MZ20
,
MZ23
]
. We do not pursue this direction and instead establish that (at least genus zero) SFT moduli spaces fit into the flow category framework of
[
AB24
]
. However, we have to generalize their definition of flow category in two ways. First, we allow the objects to be orbifolds and replace the cartesian product (in the definition of the compositions) by a form of fiber product. Secondly, the flow category comes with symmetric actions on the objects and morphism spaces. The details can be found in §
4.1
. Restricting to the case of points and trivial symmetric actions recovers the classical definition. Tangential structures such as stable complex structures or framings can be defined for this definition of flow categories exactly as in
[
AB24
]
and are discussed in §
4.1.2
. In particular, we show that these flow categories are the objects of a stable
\infty
-category (Theorem
4.18
), extending one of the main results of
[
AB24
]
to our setting. We expect that most of the flow category constructions in the literature, such as
[
PS25
]
, can be adapted.

Remark 1.2
.

Replacing the objects of the flow category by orbifolds was proposed by Mohammed Abouzaid. It differs from the definition of a Morse–Bott flow category in
[
Zho24
,
CK23
,
Bon24
]
in the way the composition maps are defined and because the group action depends on the object. This new definition appears naturally in our setup because we equip our curves with asymptotic markers at each puncture but do not constrain the markers to be mapped to a fixed base point on the Reeb orbit. Curiously enough, not choosing a base point is almost forced on us by the global Kuranishi chart construction.

Given a contact manifold
(Y,\xi)
with non-degenerate contact form
\lambda
, define
\mathcal{P}
to be the set of finite sequences of Reeb orbits. Given
L>0
, we let
\mathcal{P}_{\leq L}\subset\mathcal{P}
be the subset of sequences where each element has action
\leq L
. We identify a Reeb orbit
\gamma
with the associated orbifold
B\gamma
given by the manifold

E\gamma=\{\widetilde{\gamma}\mid\widetilde{\gamma}\text{ a constant-speed parametrization of }\gamma\}

equipped with the
S^{1}
-action that rotates the domain. This orbifold is equivalent to
[*/\mathbb{Z}_{m}]
, where
m
is the multiplicity of the Reeb orbit. To a sequence
\Gamma=(\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{k})
of Reeb orbits, we associate the product
B\Gamma=B\gamma_{1}\times\dots\times B\gamma_{k}
.

Theorem B
.

For any choice of
\lambda
-adapted almost complex structure
J
there exists a stably complex rel–
C^{1}
flow category
\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}
with objects given by finite sequences of Reeb orbits and morphism spaces based on moduli spaces of punctured
J
-holomorphic curves.

We emphasize that we obtain a stable complex structure on
\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}
despite including bad Reeb orbits in the objects of our symmetric flow category. The non-orientability of these Reeb orbits is compensated for by certain index bundles that are part of the stable complex structure; see §
4.4.1
. Theorem
B
follows from a telescope construction using Theorem
4.20
and Proposition
4.47
. Concretely, in Theorem
4.20
and Theorem
4.36
, we construct a symmetric flow category
\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{\leq L}
and its stably complex lift with objects given by
\mathcal{P}_{\leq L}
. Proposition
4.47
then asserts that for
L<L^{\prime}
, we can find a stably complex symmetric bimodule from
\mathscr{M}_{\leq L}^{Y,\lambda}
to
\mathscr{M}_{\leq L}^{Y,\lambda}
.

The flow category we construct should be regarded as an enhancement of contact homology after forgetting the natural dga structure on contact homology. A sketch of the relation is given in §
4.6
. We expect that the dga structure can be encoded by utilizing the natural symmetric monoidal structure of concatenation on the objects
\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{\leq L}
but do not pursue it in this paper.

Remark 1.3
.

Instead of the flow category constructed here, one could use Theorem
A
to build a contact flow multi-category, where the objects are Reeb orbits, while moduli spaces of curves with one positive puncture and multiple negative punctures constitute the multi-morphisms. Since the foundational aspects of flow multi-categories are still under development, we chose to pursue a bar-construction-style flow category for contact manifolds.

Remark 1.4
.

The global Kuranishi chart of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J}_{\text{SFT}}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)
depends on a choice of
perturbation datum
, see Definition
3.28
, similar to other constructions of global Kuranishi charts,
[
AMS21
,
HS24b
,
BX22
,
Rez22
]
. One key step of Theorem
B
is an inductive construction of such perturbation data, following
[
BX22
]
. However, by carefully choosing these data and not using smoothing theory, we can avoid some of the additional steps.

Restricting to cylinders, we can drop the restriction on the action in geometrically nice cases.

Corollary 1.5
.

Suppose
(Y,\lambda)
has no contractible Reeb orbits. Then, there exists a
cylindrical contact flow category

\mathscr{M}^{cyl}
with objects given by
all
Reeb orbits of
\lambda
and morphism spaces given by compactifications of moduli spaces of cylinders.

Let now
(\widehat{X},d\lambda)
be an exact symplectic cobordism from
(Y^{-},\lambda^{-})
to
(Y^{+},\lambda^{+})
so that the primitive
\lambda
is of the form
e^{\pm s}\lambda^{\pm}
near the respective end of
\widehat{X}
. Let
J
be an
\omega
-compatible almost complex structure on the completion of
X
so that
J=J^{\pm}
over the completed ends for some cylindrical almost complex structure.

Theorem C

(Theorem
4.34
)
.

Given an action bound
L
, any exact symplectic cobordism induces a rel–
C^{1}
flow bimodule
\mathscr{N}^{\widehat{X}}
from
\mathscr{M}^{Y^{-},\hskip-0.2pt\lambda^{-}}_{\leq L}
to
\mathscr{M}^{Y^{+},\hskip-0.2pt\lambda^{+}}_{\leq L}
.

Corollary 1.6

(Lemma
4.35
)
.

If
(\widehat{X},\omega)
is the trivial cobordism
(\widehat{Y},d(e^{s}\lambda))
, then
\mathscr{N}^{\widehat{X}}
is equivalent to the diagonal bimodule given suitable choices of auxiliary data.

The diagonal bimodule should be thought of as the identity morphism in
\infty
-category
\text{Flow}^{\Sigma}
. Thus, the corollary can be seen as a (weak) naturality statement of our construction.

To prove invariance up to equivalence of the choice of contact structure and almost complex structure of the flow category
\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}
, one would have to further prove the flow-categorical analogs of the composition of chain homotopies and invariance under deformations using flow bordisms. This is delegated to future work.

2.
Moduli spaces of pseudo-holomorphic buildings

2.1.
Background

Let
(Y^{2n-1},\xi)
be a closed contact manifold equipped with a contact form
\lambda
, i.e., a
1
-form satisfying
\ker(\lambda)=\xi
and
\lambda\wedge(d\lambda)^{n-1}>0
. Let
R
be the associated Reeb vector field, uniquely determined by the conditions

d\lambda(R,\cdot)=0\qquad\qquad\lambda(R)=1.

Its closed orbits are called Reeb orbits. We define

\mathcal{P}_{1}\coloneqq\mathcal{P}_{1}(\lambda):={\raisebox{1.99997pt}{$\left\{\gamma\in C^{\infty}(S^{1},Y)\mid\exists T>0:\dot{\gamma}=T\,R(\gamma)\right\}$}\left/\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\sim$}\right.}

to be the set of unparametrized Reeb orbits of constant speed of
\lambda
. Let
\mathcal{P}^{*}_{1}\subset\mathcal{P}_{1}
be the subset of simple Reeb orbits. Given
\gamma\in\mathcal{P}_{1}
, we denote by

•

m_{\gamma}
the multiplicity of
\gamma
,

•

\overline{\gamma}
the underlying simple orbit and identify it with
\text{im}(\gamma)
,

•

\widetilde{\gamma}
any parametrization of
\gamma
,

•

\mathcal{A}(\gamma):=\int_{S^{1}}\gamma^{*}\lambda
the
action
of
\gamma.

We assume that
\lambda
is
nondegenerate
, that is, that the return map
d\phi_{R}^{T}(\gamma(1))
of the Reeb flow has no eigenvalue
1
for a Reeb orbit
\gamma
of action
T
. Equivalently, the set of simple unparametrized Reeb orbits is discrete in
Y
.

An almost complex structure
J
on the
symplectization

(\widehat{Y},\omega)\coloneqq(\mathbb{R}\times Y,d(e^{s}\lambda))
is
\lambda
-adapted
if
J
is invariant under the
\mathbb{R}
- translation action,
J(\frac{\partial}{\partial t})=R
and
J|_{\xi}
tames
d\lambda
. Any
d\lambda
-compatible almost complex structure
J_{\xi}
on
\xi
determines uniquely a
\lambda
-adapted almost complex structure
J
on the symplectisation
\widehat{Y}
.

A smooth map

u\colon\dot{C}\to\mathbb{R}\times Y
on a punctured Riemann surface
(\dot{C},\mathfrak{j})
is
J
-holomorphic
if it satisfies the Cauchy-Riemann equation

\bar{\partial}_{J}u\coloneq\frac{1}{2}(du+Jdu\mathfrak{j})=0.

Note that due to exactness, all
J
-holomorphic maps from a closed Riemann surface to the symplectization are constant. The simplest type of non-constant
J
-holomorphic curves is given by
trivial cylinders
. They are of the form

u_{\gamma}\colon\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}\to\mathbb{R}\times Y:(s,t)\mapsto(Ks,\widetilde{\gamma}(Kt)),

for some Reeb orbit
\gamma
and a constant-speed parametrization
\widetilde{\gamma}
of
\gamma
, where
K=\int_{\gamma}\lambda
is the action of the Reeb orbit.

Since
\widehat{Y}
is non-compact and the curves are punctured, the usual notion of energy does not make sense in this context. The analogue is the
Hofer energy
, given by

E_{H}(u)=\int_{\dot{C}}f^{*}d\lambda+\sup_{\phi\in\mathcal{C}}\int a\;da\wedge f^{*}\lambda

for map
u=(a,f)\colon\dot{C}\to\widehat{Y}
, where
\mathcal{C}
is the set of non-negative smooth functions
\phi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
with compact support and normalized
L^{1}
norm (i.e.,
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(s)ds=1.)
In the case of non-degenerate contact form
\lambda
, it follows from
[
HWZ96
]
that any
J
-holomorphic curve with finite Hofer energy asymptotically converges to a trivial cylinder over a Reeb orbit.

The compactness result
[
BEH+03
, Theorem 10.1]
(or
[
CM05
]
) proves that the moduli space of punctured curves with bounded Hofer energy and bounded topological type allows a natural compactification by adding boundary strata of
J
-holomorphic buildings. We will now discuss them in detail in genus zero.

2.2.
Buildings in symplectizations

The moduli space of genus zero buildings have a natural stratification modeled on trees. We recall some basics and develop relevant notations for moduli spaces of genus zero buildings. The dual trees underlying our buildings without levels take the following form.

Definition 2.1
.

A
decorated tree (or forest)
is a directed tree (or forest)
T
with internal and external (respectively finite and exterior) edges together with the data of

•

a class
\beta_{v}\in H_{2}(Y,\{\gamma_{e}\}_{e\in E_{v}})
for each
v\in V
,

•

\gamma\colon E(T)\to\mathcal{P}_{1}
associating to an edge a Reeb orbit,

We call a vertex
v\in V(T)

trivial
if
\beta_{v}=0
and
v
has at most two adjacent edges. A decorated tree
T
is
stable
if it has no trivial vertices.

\import

./Untitled-2.pdf_tex

Figure 1.
Morphisms of a tree

Define the category
\mathscr{S}
(and
\mathscr{S}^{\bullet}
) to have as objects decorated trees (respectively forests) as in Definition
2.1
with morphisms given by contractions
p\colon T\to T^{\prime}
so that

(1)

for each non-contracted edge
e\in E(T)
we have
\gamma^{\prime}_{p(e)}=\gamma_{e}
,

(2)

for each
v^{\prime}\in V(T^{\prime})
we have
\beta_{v^{\prime}}=\#_{p(v)=v^{\prime}}\beta_{v}
.

See Figure
1
for an example.
The composition of two such morphisms is the obvious one. The category
\mathscr{S}
is the category
\mathscr{S}_{I}
in
[
Par19
]
. We denote by
\text{Aut}(T/T^{\prime})
the group of automorphisms of
T
that leave
p
invariant. We call a tree
T

maximal
if any morphism
T\to T^{\prime}
is an isomorphism. Note for maps to a symplectization, any maximal decorated tree is a
corolla
, at tree with a unique vertex and no interior edges.

Notation
.

Given a Riemann surface
C
and a point
z\in C
, we denote by

S_{z}C\coloneqq(T_{z}C\setminus\{0\})/\mathbb{R}_{>0}

the boundary circle at infinity.

Definition 2.2
.

Let
T
be a decorated tree. A
pseudoholomorphic building of type
T
consists of the following data

(i)

for each
v\in V(T)
a closed connected, possibly nodal, genus zero Riemann surface
(C_{v},\mathfrak{j}_{v})
together with a set of pairwise distinct points
\{z_{v,e}\}_{e\in E_{v}}
;

(ii)

for each
v\in V(T)
a smooth map
u_{v}\colon\dot{C}_{v}\coloneqq C_{v}\setminus\{z_{v,e}\}\to\widehat{Y}
, which

•

is
C^{0}
-convergent to the positive trivial cylinder over
\gamma_{e}
at
z_{v,e}
for an incoming edge
e\in E_{v}^{+}
,

•

is
C^{0}
-convergent to the negative trivial cylinder over
\gamma_{e}
at
z_{e}
for an outgoing edge
e\in E_{v}^{-}

•

represents
\beta_{v}
;

•

satisfies
\bar{\partial}_{J,\mathfrak{j}_{v}}u_{v}=0
.

(iii)

for each
e\in E^{\text{ext}}_{v}
an
asymptotic marker

\widetilde{b}_{e}\in S_{z_{v,e}}C_{v}
,

(iv)

for every interior edge
e
from
v
to
v^{\prime}
, a
matching isomorphism

m_{e}\colon S_{z_{v,e}}C_{v}\to S_{z_{v^{\prime},e}}C_{v^{\prime}}
intertwining
d{u_{v}}_{Y}
and
d{u_{v^{\prime}}}_{Y}
.

We call such a building
stable
if
T
is stable.
An
isomorphism
of two such buildings consists of a collection
\{\iota_{v}\colon C_{v}\cong C^{\prime}_{v}\}
of biholomorphisms with
\iota_{v}(z_{v,e})=z^{\prime}_{v,e}
, preserving the matching isomorphisms so that
u_{v}=u^{\prime}_{v}\circ\iota_{v}
.

Definition 2.3

(Moduli spaces of buildings)
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{J}(T)
be the moduli space of maps as in Definition
2.2
up to isomorphism. It admits a free
\mathbb{R}^{V(T)}
-action given by translating the map on the corresponding sub-curve in the
\mathbb{R}
-factor of
\widehat{Y}
. The
moduli space of
J
-holomorphic buildings of type
T
is

\mathcal{M}^{J}(T)\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{J}(T)/\mathbb{R}^{V(T)}.

We define its
SFT compactification
to be

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J}(T)\coloneqq\bigsqcup\limits_{[T^{\prime}\to T]}\mathcal{M}^{J}(T^{\prime})/\text{Aut}(T^{\prime}/T)

equipped with the Gromov topology,
[
Par19
]
.

If
T
is a corolla with incoming and outgoing exterior edges labeled by
\Gamma^{+}=\{\gamma^{+}\}
and
\Gamma^{-}=\{\gamma^{-}\}
, respectively, we write

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)\coloneqq\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J}(T).

We call
T

effective
if
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J}(T)\neq\emptyset
.
By
[
BEH+03
, Theorem 10.1]
, respectively
[
Par19
, Theorem 2.27]
, the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J}(T)
is compact for any
T
and there exist only finitely many isomorphism classes of morphisms
T^{\prime}\to T
so that
T^{\prime}
is effective. Given
T
, define

(2.1)

\mathcal{P}(T)\coloneqq\bigcup\limits_{T^{\prime}\to T}{\{\gamma_{e}\mid e\in E(T^{\prime})\}},

to be the set of Reeb orbits which buildings in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J}(T)
can be asymptotic to, either at “internal” puncture or at an input or output puncture. Due to the compactness of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J}(T)
, the set
\mathcal{P}(T)
is finite. In particular, for a fixed set of input and output Reeb orbits, the energy equations in
[
BEH+03
, §5.8]
show that the Hofer energy is a constant that is linearly dependent on the action of the Reeb orbits.

2.3.
Leveled buildings

We now add level structures to our buildings by endowing the decorated trees defined in the last section with level structures. The derived orbifold of pseudo-holomorphic buildings with levels will be later used to construct the morphism spaces of the flow category in §
4
.

Definition 2.4
.

A
leveled tree

(T,\ell)
consists of a decorated tree
T
as in Definition
2.1
equipped with a
level function

\ell:V(T)\to\mathbb{N}
satisfying

•

if
\ell(v)=1
, then
v
has an incoming exterior edge,

•

\ell(w)\geq\ell(v)+1
if
(v,w)\in E(T)
.

We call
(T,\ell)

stable
if each
level

\ell^{-1}(\{j\})
is nonempty for
j\leq\max\ell
. The
size
of a leveled tree is the maximal value of the level function. A
morphism
of a leveled tree is obtained by simultaneous contraction of edges between adjacent levels.

Any leveled tree is from now on assumed to be stable.

Lemma 2.5
.

Each decorated tree
T
as in Definition
2.1
admits a unique minimal level function, its
pre-level function

p\ell:V(T)\to\mathbb{N}
given by

•

p\ell(v)=1
if
v\in V^{i}
, the set of vertices with an incoming exterior edge,

•

p\ell(v)=\max\{d(v,v_{i})\mid v_{i}\in V^{i}\}

where
d
is the (edge-)distance function, which is well defined since
T
is a tree.∎

Definition 2.6
.

A vertex
v
in a decorated tree is
trivial
if it has exactly two adjacent edges, both labeled by the same Reeb orbit, and if it carries the
0
homology class.

Remark 2.7
.

Given a leveled tree
(T,\ell)
, we can construct a tree
T_{\ell}
that contracts onto
T
by adding a chain of
\ell(w)-\ell(v)-1
trivial vertices between the vertices
v,w\in V(T)
with
(v,w)\in E(T)
and
\ell(w)>1+\ell(v)
. This recovers the usual notion of the underlying tree of SFT buildings.

If the pre-level function is injective, it is the only level function on
T
. The simplest occurrence of non-injectivity is when the tree
T
has three vertices (as in Figure
1
) with a pre-level containing two vertices. In this case, the fiber of the forgetful map

(2.2)

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\text{SFT}}^{J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)

is homeomorphic to a closed interval
[0,1]
. In particular, the interval
(0,1)
records the relative height between the curves corresponding to each of the vertices, and the boundary
\{0,1\}
of the fiber consists of the breaking of the pre-level
j
into two levels and the appearance of trivial cylinder components.

\import

./leveled_tree.pdf_tex

Figure 2.
Three different level functions for an unleveled tree. We do not draw the trivial vertices for the sake of clarity; one can recover the trivial vertices uniquely by the differences of levels between adjacent vertices.

Convention 2.8
.

We will denote the non-negative real line by
\mathbb{R}_{+}=[0,\infty).

Definition 2.9
.

A leveled tree
(T,\ell)
is
maximally leveled
if
\#\ell^{-1}(j)\leq 1
for each
j\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proposition 2.10
.

Given any decorated tree
T
, there exists a number,
N_{T}
, of maximally leveled trees
(T,\ell)
over
T
, where
N_{T}
is uniquely determined by the underlying tree.

The proof is by induction, for which we need another definition.

Definition 2.11
.

A leveled tree
(T,\ell)
is
k
-maximally leveled
if

•

\#\ell^{-1}(j)\leq 1
for
j\leq k
;

•

\ell
is injective on the set
p\ell^{-1}(\{1,\dots,k\})
.

Lemma 2.12
.

Given a
k
-maximally leveled tree
(T,\ell)
, there exists
\#\ell^{-1}(k+1)!
-many leveled trees
(T,\ell^{\prime})
that are
(k+1)
-maximally leveled and contract onto
(T,\ell)
and which are minimal with respect to this property.

Proof.

Set
N\coloneqq\#\ell^{-1}(k+1)
. Unraveling definitions, the conditions mean that we have to find
N
level functions
\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{N}\colon V(T)\to\mathbb{N}
that satisfy

(a)

\ell_{i}
is
(k+1)
-maximal;

(b)

\ell_{i}
agrees with
\ell
on
\ell^{-1}(\{1,\dots,k\})
;

(c)

for any
v,w\in V(T)
that can be connected by a (directed) path, we have

(2.3)

\ell_{i}(v)-\ell_{i}(w)=\ell(v)-\ell(w)

if
\ell(v)
,
\ell(w)\geq k+1
.

Condition (
b
) means that
\ell_{i}
is determined on
\ell^{-1}(\{1,\dots,k\})
, while Condition (
c
) implies that the values of
\ell_{i}
on
\ell^{-1}(\{m\geq k+2\})
are determined by the values of
\ell_{i}
on
\ell^{-1}(k+1)
, due to the minimality requirement. The
(k+1)
-maximality together with the requirement that
(T,\ell_{i})
be stable means that we have
N
elements that have to distributed over
N
levels. Thus, we have
N!
options for extending
\ell
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.10
.

The leveled tree
(T,p\ell)
is tautologically
0
-maximal. Now we apply Lemma
2.12
inductively until we obtain
\#V(T)
-corollas. The minimality of Lemma
2.12
ensures that each
\#V(T)
-corolla can only be obtained through a unique path of
k
-corollas for
k\leq\#V(T)
. Thus, the number of such trees is determined inductively by the values
\#\ell^{-1}(k+1)!
by Lemma
2.12
.
∎

Definition 2.13
.

Let
(T,\ell)
be a leveled tree with underlying decorated tree
T
. A
J
-holomorphic building

(u,C,b_{*},m_{*})

of type
(T,\ell)
is a
J
-holomorphic building of type
T_{\ell}
as in Definition
2.2
. An isomorphism between two such buildings is defined as before.

Remark 2.14
.

Note that for any such building
u
and any trivial vertex
v
of
T
, the map
u_{v}
is forced to be a trivial cylinder.

We write
\mathcal{M}^{J}(T,\ell)
for the space of leveled buildings of type
(T,\ell)
up to reparametrization and translation, and we define the
SFT moduli space of buildings of type at least
(T,\ell)
to be

(2.4)

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J}_{\text{SFT}}(T,\ell)\coloneqq\bigsqcup\limits_{(T^{\prime},\ell^{\prime})\to(T,\ell)}\mathcal{M}^{J}(T^{\prime},\ell^{\prime})/\text{Aut}(T^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}}/T_{\ell})

equipped with the Gromov topology as defined in
[
BEH+03
, §7.3]
.

2.4.
Buildings in exact symplectic cobordisms

We now discuss the trees, which stratify the moduli space of buildings in exact symplectic cobordisms.

Definition 2.15
.

An
exact symplectic cobordism
from
(Y_{+},\lambda_{+})
to
(Y_{-},\lambda_{-})
is an exact symplectic manifold
(\widehat{X},\omega=d\lambda)
together with embeddings

\displaystyle\Theta^{+}\colon(-N,\infty)\times Y^{+}\to\widehat{X}

\displaystyle\Theta^{-}\colon(-\infty,N)\times Y^{-}\to\widehat{X}

so that
\widehat{X}\setminus\text{im}(\Theta^{+})\cup\text{im}(\Theta^{-})
is compact and
(\Theta^{\pm})^{*}\lambda=e^{s}\lambda^{\pm}
.

An almost complex structure
J
on a symplectic cobordism
(X,\omega)
is
compatible
if it is
\omega
-compatible and
{\Theta^{\pm}}^{*}J
is translation invariant. In particular, this implies that
{\Theta^{\pm}}^{*}J
is an adapted almost complex structure.

Example 2.16
.

Suppose
(Y,\xi)
is a contact manifold with two contact forms
\lambda^{\pm}
and two adapted almost complex structures
J^{\pm}
. Since the space of contact forms is contractible, we can find a smooth path
\{\lambda_{s}\}_{s\in\mathbb{R}}
of contact structures so that
\lambda_{s}=\lambda^{-}
for
s\leq-m
and
\lambda_{s}=\lambda^{+}
for
s\geq m
. We can then find a path
\{J_{s}\}_{s\in\mathbb{R}}
that agrees with
J^{-}
on
\mathbb{R}_{\leq-m}
and
J^{+}
on
\mathbb{R}_{\geq m}
and so that
J_{s}
is
\lambda_{s}
-adapted for each
s
. By taking a
m\gg 0
and rescaling the homotopy
\{\lambda_{s}\}_{s\in\mathbb{R}}
we can ensure that
\partial_{s}\lambda_{s}
is small, whence
d(e^{s}\lambda_{s})
is symplectic. Then,
J
is a compatible almost complex structure on the symplectic cobordism
(\mathbb{R}\times Y,\omega=d(e^{s}\lambda_{s}))
.

Notation
.

Given an exact symplectic cobordism
(\widehat{X},\omega)
from
(Y^{+},\lambda^{+})
to
(Y^{-},\lambda^{-})
, we abbreviate

X^{00}:=\mathbb{R}\times Y^{+},\quad X^{01}:=X,\quad X^{11}:=\mathbb{R}\times Y^{-}.

We set
\mathcal{P}_{i}:=\mathcal{P}(X^{ii})
and
\mathcal{P}_{01}:=\mathcal{P}_{0}\sqcup\mathcal{P}_{1}
.

Definition 2.17
.

A
decorated cobordism tree (or forest)
is a tree (or forest)
T_{c}
equipped with maps

\ast\colon E(T_{c})\to\{0,1\}\qquad\qquad\ast_{\pm}\colon V(T_{c})\to\{0,1\}

so that

•

\ast(E^{\text{ext},+})=0
and
\ast(E^{\text{ext},-})=1
,

•

\ast_{+}\leq\ast_{-}
,

•

for any exterior edge
e\in E^{\text{ext},\pm}
adjacent to the vertex
v
we have
\ast(e)=\ast_{\pm}(v)
,

together with the data of

•

a Reeb orbit
\gamma_{e}\in\mathcal{P}_{\ast(e)}
for each
e\in E(T_{c})
,

•

a homology class
\beta_{v}\in H_{2}(X^{\ast_{-}(v)\ast_{+}(v)},\{\gamma_{e}\}_{e\in E_{v}})
for each
v\in V(T_{c})
.

We call
v
a
symplectisation vertex
if
\ast_{+}(v)=\ast_{-}(v)
. The cobordism tree is
stable
if none of the symplectization vertices are trivial.

Similar to the category
\mathscr{S}
, there is a category of decorated cobordism trees
\mathscr{S}^{c}
whose objects are decorated cobordism trees and whose morphisms are contractions
T^{\prime}\xrightarrow[\pi]{}T
such that
\ast_{+}(\pi(v))\leq\ast_{+}(v),\ast_{-}(\pi(v))\geq\ast_{-}(v)
and
\ast(\pi(e))=\ast(e)
for any non-contracted edge
e
.

\import

./cobtreemorph.pdf_tex

Figure 3.
Morphisms of a cobordism tree

Definition 2.18
.

A
leveled cobordism tree (or forest)

(T_{c},\ell_{c})
is a decorated cobordism tree (or forest) with a level function
\ell_{c}
such that
(T_{c},\ell_{c})
is a leveled tree as in Definition
2.4
and there is an integer
\mathfrak{c}>0
so that
\ell_{c}^{-1}(\mathfrak{c})=\ast_{+}^{-1}(0)\cap\ast_{-}^{-1}(1)
and
(\ast_{+}(v),\ast_{-}(v))=(0,0)
if and only if
\ell(v)<\mathfrak{c}
, while
(\ast_{+}(v),\ast_{-}(v))=(1,1)
if and only if
\ell(v)>\mathfrak{c}
.

Definition 2.19
.

A leveled cobordism tree is
maximally levelled
if
\ell^{-1}(j)\leq 1
for all
j\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{\mathfrak{c}\}.

The proof of Proposition
2.10
carries over to leveled cobordism trees.

Proposition 2.20
.

Given any decorated cobordism tree
T_{c}
, there exists a number
N_{T_{c}}
of maximally leveled trees
(T_{c},\ell)
over
T_{c}
, where
N_{T_{c}}
is uniquely determined by the underlying tree.∎

Definition 2.21
.

Let
T_{c}
be a decorated cobordism tree. A
pseudoholomorphic building of type
T_{c}
consists of the same data as in Definition
2.2
but with the property that the target of the map
u_{v}
is
X^{\ast_{-}(v)\ast_{+}(v)}
.
We call such a building
stable
if
T_{c}
is stable.

An
isomorphism
of two such buildings consists of a collection
\{\iota_{v}\colon C_{v}\cong C^{\prime}_{v}\}
of biholomorphisms with
\iota_{v}(z_{v,e})=z^{\prime}_{v,e}
so that
u_{v}=u^{\prime}_{v}\circ\iota_{v}
.

Definition 2.22

(Moduli space of cobordism buildings)
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{J}(T_{c})
be the moduli space of maps as in Definition
2.21
up to isomorphism. It admits a free
\mathbb{R}^{V_{s}(T)}
-action, given by translations of maps on each symplectization vertex. The
moduli space of
J
-holomorphic cobordism buildings of type
T_{c}
is

\mathcal{M}^{J}(T_{c}):=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{J}(T_{c})/\mathbb{R}^{V_{s}(T)}.

We define

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J}(T_{c})\coloneqq\bigsqcup_{[T^{\prime}_{c}\to T_{c}]}\mathcal{M}^{J}(T^{\prime}_{c})/\text{Aut}(T^{\prime}_{c}/T_{c})

equipped with the Gromov topology as in
[
BEH+03
]
.

3.
The global Kuranishi chart construction

3.1.
Base space

Fix integers
n\geq 0
and
d>0
. We write

\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}(d)\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{d},d)

for the locus of (equivalence classes of) regular embedded stable holomorphic maps
\varphi\colon C\to\mathbb{P}^{d}
of genus
0
and degree
d
. In particular,
\text{im}(\varphi)
is not contained in a complex hyperplane of
\mathbb{P}^{d}
and
\varphi
admits no nontrivial automorphisms. Thus,
\mathcal{B}
is a smooth quasi-projective variety and served as the base space of previous global Kuranishi chart constructions,
[
AMS21
,
HS24b
,
AMS24
]
. Given nonnegative integers
n^{\pm}\geq 0
, we let

\mathcal{B}_{n^{+},n^{-}}=\mathcal{B}_{n^{+},n^{-}}(d)

be the preimage of
\mathcal{B}
under the forgetful map
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,n^{+},n^{-}}(\mathbb{P}^{d},d)\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{d},d)
, where the
(n^{+}+n^{-})
-many marked points are divided into positive and negative marked points. It is an easy verification that this is again a complex manifold.

While the spaces
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{B}_{n}
capture the domain breaking of stable maps, they do not capture the degenerations of buildings, where nodes are constrained by the asymptotic conditions. Thus, we construct a real-oriented blow-up of
\mathcal{B}
that will serve as the base space for the Kuranishi chart of Pardon buildings; see §
3.1.1
. It agrees with the base space used in Hamiltonian Floer theory,
[
BX22
,
Rez22
,
AB24
]
. In §
3.1.2
, we perform a generalized corner blow-up to obtain the base space for the Kuranishi chart of leveled buildings.

3.1.1.
Without levels

We will perform real-oriented blow-ups on the space
\mathcal{B}_{n^{+},n^{-}}(d)
following
[
BX22
]
. These spaces will serve as the base spaces for the global charts of Pardon buildings in
3.3
. Assume we are given an integer
d_{i}^{\pm}\geq 1
for each marked point
z_{i}^{\pm}
so that

d=p\Bigl(\sum\limits_{i}{d^{+}_{i}}-\sum\limits_{j}{d^{-}_{j}}\Bigr)+n-2

for some uniform integer
p\geq 1
where
n
is the number of marked points.
The following definition will allow us to modify
\mathcal{B}_{n^{+},n^{-}}(d)
to obtain the right boundary stratification for curves in symplectizations.

Definition 3.1
.

Given a stable map
\varphi\colon C\to\mathbb{P}^{d}
whose domain has a unique node
x
, we say that
x
is of
type
0
if it is non-separating or if it separates
C
into irreducible components
C_{0}
and
C_{1}
of degree
d_{0}
, respectively
d_{1}
so that

(3.1)

\mathrm{d}_{x}:=(d_{0}-p\hskip-3.0pt\sum\limits_{z^{+}_{i}\in C_{0}}{d_{i}^{+}}+p\hskip-3.0pt\sum\limits_{z^{-}_{j}\in C_{0}}{d_{j}^{-}}-\deg(\omega_{C}|_{C_{0}}))-(d_{1}-p\hskip-3.0pt\sum\limits_{z^{+}_{i}\in C_{1}}{d_{i}^{+}}+p\hskip-3.0pt\sum\limits_{z^{-}_{j}\in C_{1}}{d_{j}^{-}}-\deg(\omega_{C}|_{C_{1}}))=0.

We say
x
is of
type
1
with order
|\mathrm{d}_{x}|
otherwise.

The following does not depend on the way we determine the type of the node. Given
[\varphi,C,z_{*}]\in\mathcal{B}_{n^{+},n^{-}}(d)
whose domain has two nodes
x_{1},x_{2}
, we call
x_{i}
of
type
j
if
[\varphi,C,z_{*}]
is the limit of a sequence of maps with one-nodal domains so that the node converges to
x_{i}
and each of them is of type
j
. Inductively, this yields a decomposition
N_{C}=N_{C}^{\mathrm{o}}\sqcup N_{C}^{\bullet}
of the nodes of the domain of any
[\varphi,C,z_{*}]\in\mathcal{B}
.

Definition 3.2
.

The space
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{n^{+},n^{-}}(d)
consists of elements of
\mathcal{B}_{n^{+},n^{-}}(d)
equipped with the following additional data

•

an asymptotic marker
b_{k}^{\pm}\in S_{z_{k}^{\pm}}C
at each marked point
z_{k}^{\pm}
determining a real line
\ell_{k}^{\pm}\subset T_{z_{k}^{\pm}}C
,

•

an isomorphism
m_{z}\colon S_{z}C_{v}\to S_{z}C_{v^{\prime}}
, or, equivalently, an element of
(T_{z}C_{v}\otimes T_{z}C_{v^{\prime}}\setminus\{0\})/S^{1}
, for each
z\in\mathcal{N}_{C}^{\bullet}
between the irreducible components
C_{v}
and
C_{v^{\prime}}
of
C
.
1
1

1

To see this equivalence, note that
\ell=[\xi\otimes\xi^{\prime}]
yields a map
m_{\ell}([\eta]):=[\langle{\xi,\widetilde{\eta}}\rangle\xi^{\prime}]
where
\langle{\cdot}\rangle
is any Hermitian product on
T_{z}C
, while conversely given
m
we can set
\ell_{m}:=[\widetilde{\xi}\otimes\widetilde{m}(\xi)]
where
\xi\in S_{z}C
is arbitrary and the tilde means that we take a lift in the respective tangent space.

For the next assertion, we recall the construction of the real-oriented blow-up of a complex manifold
Z
along an effective normal crossing divisor
D
from
[
Sab13
, §8.2]
. Suppose first
D
is smooth and principal, and let
S_{D}\to Z
be the
S^{1}
-bundle of the line bundle
L_{D}\to Z
associated to
D
. Let
f\colon Z\to L_{D}
be a holomorphic section. Then
f^{-1}(0)=D
, so the composition
\widetilde{f}

Z\setminus D\xrightarrow{f}L_{D}\setminus 0\to S_{D}

is well-defined. The
real-oriented blow-up

\text{Bl}_{Z}(D)
of
Z
along
D
is the closure of the image of
\widetilde{f}
and the blow-down map
\text{Bl}_{Z}(D)\to Z
is simply given by the restriction of the projection
S_{D}\to Z
.
By
[
Sab13
, Lemma 8.1]
, the space
\text{Bl}_{Z}(D)
admits a unique smooth structure with

(3.2)

\partial\text{Bl}_{Z}(D)\cong S_{D}|_{D}.

If
\{D_{i}\}_{i}
is a normal crossing divisor with finitely many irreducible components, then there exists a real blow-up of
Z
along
\{D_{i}\}_{i}
. By
[
Sab13
, Lemma 8.2]
, it is given by

(3.3)

\text{Bl}_{Z}(\{D_{i}\}_{i})=\text{Bl}_{Z}(D_{1})\times_{Z}\dots\times_{Z}\text{Bl}_{Z}(D_{k}).

which we can take as the definition for the purposes of this paper.

Remark 3.3
.

In our construction we also encounter the case of blowing up a divisor
D
, which has normal crossings self-intersections, i.e., any point of
D
admits a
holomorphic
chart
\phi\colon U\to Z
where
U
is an open neighborhood of
0
in
\mathbb{C}^{n}
and
\phi^{-1}(D)=U\cap\{z_{1}\dots z_{k}=0\}
.
We define the smooth structure on the real-oriented blow-up,
\text{Bl}_{Z}(D)
via Equation (
3.3
) in each such coordinate chart
U
. As the transition functions on the base are holomorphic, they lift to diffeomorphisms between the blow-ups.

Lemma 3.4
.

\mathcal{B}^{P}_{n^{+},n^{-}}(d)
is a smooth
\text{PGL}_{d+1}(\mathbb{C})
-manifold with corners so that the depth in the boundary of
(\varphi,C,z_{*},b_{*},m_{*})
is given by the number of elements in
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}_{C}
. Moreover, each corner stratum is invariant under the
\text{PGL}_{d+1}(\mathbb{C})
-action.

Proof.

We abbreviate
\mathcal{B}^{P}=\mathcal{B}^{P}_{n^{+},n^{-}}(d)
and
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{n^{+},n^{-}}(d)
. Recall that
\mathbb{L}_{i}\to\mathcal{B}
is the complex line bundle with fiber given by
T_{z_{i}}^{*}C
at
(\varphi,C,z_{*})
. Let

\mathcal{B}^{am}:=\bigoplus\limits_{i=1}^{k^{+}}S(\mathbb{L}^{\vee}_{i})\oplus\bigoplus\limits_{j=1}^{k^{-}}S(\mathbb{L}^{\vee}_{j})

be a direct sum of
U(1)
bundles corresponding to the line bundles
\mathbb{L}_{*}
and let
\pi_{am}\colon\mathcal{B}^{am}\to\mathcal{B}
be the forgetful map.
Then
\mathcal{B}^{am}
is clearly smooth (without corners) and
\pi_{am}
is a submersion.

Let
D_{1},\dots,D_{\ell}
be the divisors in
\mathcal{B}
given by curves with at least one node in
\mathcal{N}^{\bullet}
. They form a normal crossing divisor, so we can define
\mathcal{B}^{mi}:=\text{Bl}_{\mathcal{B}}(\{D_{i}\}_{i})
with blow-down map
\pi_{mi}\colon\mathcal{B}^{mi}\to\mathcal{B}
. We claim that

(3.4)

\mathcal{B}^{P}=\mathcal{B}^{am}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{B}^{mi}

and that the forgetful map
\mathcal{B}^{P}\to\mathcal{B}
is induced by the blow-down map
\mathcal{B}^{mi}\to\mathcal{B}
. To see this, we note that the asymptotic markers at the marked points correspond exactly to the additional data of elements of
S(\mathbb{L}^{\vee}_{j})
. Forgetting them yields the canonical map
\mathcal{B}^{P}\to\mathcal{B}^{mi}
. Meanwhile, the normal bundle of
D_{i}
has fiber
N_{\varphi}=\mathbb{L}_{z^{+}}^{\vee}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{z^{-}}^{\vee}
by
[
ACG11
, §XI.3]
. Thus an element of its sphere bundle is exactly a matching isomorphism, so the claim without the group action follows from (
3.2
).
Finally, by
[
AK10
, Theorem 5.1]
2
2

2

To be precise, the statement is for projective blow-up while we work with the spherical one. However, as mentioned in the paragraph above Remark 1.1 op. cit., their results also hold for spherical blow-ups.
, the smooth
\text{PGL}_{d+1}(\mathbb{C})
-action on
\mathcal{B}
lifts to a smooth action on
\mathcal{B}^{P}
. For the last assertion, it suffices to show that each stratum of codimension
1
is invariant under the
PGL
-action. Let
S
be such a stratum, and note that
S
is a connected component of the space
S_{1}(\mathcal{B}^{P}_{d})
of codimension-
1
points in
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{d}
. Since
S_{1}(\mathcal{B}^{P}_{d})
is preserved by the action and
\text{PGL}_{d+1}(\mathbb{C})
is connected, the orbit of
S
under this action is connected as well. Hence, it has to agree with
S
.
∎

Returning to the notation of §
2.2
, recall that
\mathscr{S}
is the category of decorated trees defined in Definition
2.1
. Let
\mathscr{S}^{\mathrm{o}}
be the category obtained from
\mathscr{S}
by forgetting the data of the Reeb orbits associated to the edges and replacing the relative homology class
\beta_{v}\in H_{2}(Y,\{\gamma_{v,e}\}_{e\in E_{v}})
by an integer
d_{v}\geq 1
, corresponding to the degree of the map
\widecheck{\varphi}_{v}\colon C_{v}\to\mathbb{P}^{d}
, where
\widecheck{\varphi}
is the image of
\varphi
under the blow-down map
\mathscr{B}^{P}\to\mathcal{B}
. We equip
\mathscr{S}^{\mathrm{o}}
with the dimension function

(3.5)

\dim(T)=2(d-3)+2(d+1)d+3(\#\Gamma^{-}+\#\Gamma^{+})-\#E^{\text{int}}(T)

Lemma 3.5
.

There exists a canonical stratification
P\colon\mathcal{B}^{P}_{n^{+},n^{-}}(d)\to\mathscr{S}^{\mathrm{o}}
which assigns the tree type of the domain to an element of
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{n^{+},n^{+}}(d)
. It is cell-like in the sense that
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{/T}:=P(\mathscr{S}^{\mathrm{o}}_{/T})
is a smooth manifold with corners of dimension
\dim(T)
with interior given by
P^{-1}(\{T\})
.

Proof.

Define the function
P^{\mathbb{R}}
by letting
T_{\widehat{\varphi}}=P^{\mathbb{R}}(\widehat{\varphi})
be the underlying dual graph of the domain of
\pi(\widehat{\varphi})
, where we have collapsed all edges corresponding to nodes of type
0
(and added the degrees of the associated vertices). Given an element in (the preimage of) a gluing chart (under
\pi
) near
\widehat{\varphi}
, we obtain a unique morphism
T_{\widehat{\varphi}}\to T_{\widehat{\phi}}
in
\mathscr{S}^{\mathrm{o}}
. Since
\mathcal{B}
is unobstructed, the induced germ of a map
\mathcal{B}^{P}\to\mathscr{S}^{\mathrm{o}}_{T_{\widehat{\varphi}}/}
near
\widehat{\varphi}
is a stratification. The last claim follows from the dimension formula for
\mathcal{B}
and the description of the corner strata of the real blow-up above. Observing that
\mathcal{B}^{P}\to\mathcal{B}^{mi}
is a torus bundle over
\mathcal{B}^{mi}
, this completes the proof.∎

3.1.2.
Base space with levels

In this subsection, we construct a generalized blow-up of
\mathcal{B}^{P}
that will serve as the base space for the global Kuranishi chart of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\text{SFT}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)
. For this, we use the generalized blow-up of
[
KM15
]
and the stratification of
\mathcal{B}^{P}
by decorated trees.

Generalized blow-up

We give a quick recap of the generalized blow-up of a manifold
X
with corners as defined in
[
KM15
]
. We denote the set of closures of connected boundary (codimension 1) components by
\mathcal{M}_{1}(X)
, assuming that no
F\in\mathcal{M}_{1}(X)
self-intersects. We can then define the set of codimension-
k
faces
\mathcal{M}_{k}(X)
to consist of intersections
F_{I}=F_{i_{1}}\cap\dots\cap F_{i_{k}}
of
k
distinct elements of
\mathcal{M}_{1}(X)
. We now associate the following combinatorial data to
X
.

•

To each face
F\in\mathcal{M}_{k}(X)
, we associate the freely generated monoid

\sigma_{F}\coloneqq\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}H\in\mathcal{M}_{1}{X},\\
F\subset H\end{subarray}}\mathbb{N}e_{H}

•

The
monoidal complex

\mathcal{P}_{X}
of
X
consists of the collection of monoids
\sigma_{F}
for every face
F
of codimension 1 and higher, together with the canonical maps
i_{GF}:\sigma_{G}\to\sigma_{F}
induced by inclusion of the faces
F\subset G
.

Definition 3.6

(
[
KM15
, Definition 2.2]
)
.

A
refinement

\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}
of a single monoid
\sigma
is a collection
\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}=\{\tau\mid\tau\subset\sigma\}
of submonoids such that

i)

if
\tau\in\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}
and
\tau^{\prime}\subset\tau
, then
\tau^{\prime}\in\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}
,

ii)

for any
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}
, the intersection
\tau_{1}\cap\tau_{2}
is a face of both
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
,

iii)

\text{span}_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(\sigma)=\bigcup\limits_{\tau\in\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}}{\text{span}_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(\tau)}
.

Note that (
i
) implies that a refinement is uniquely determined by the maximal monoids it contains. A classical example of a refinement of a monoid is given by subdivison. We refer to
[
KM15
, §2]
for more details and examples.

The more general notion of the
refinement of monoidal complex

\mathcal{R}_{Q}
amounts to a collection
\mathcal{R}_{Q}=\{\mathcal{R}_{\sigma}\mid\sigma\in Q\}
of refinements of the monoids of
Q
together with compatibilities between these refinements. We refer the reader to
[
KM15
, Definition 4.7]
for the precise definition.

The main construction in
[
KM15
]
can be paraphrased as follows.

Theorem 3.7

(
[
KM15
, Theorem A]
)
.

For any smooth refinement
\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{P}_{X}
, there is a manifold
Y=[X;\mathcal{R}]
with corners with a blow-down map
b:Y\to X
such that
\mathcal{P}_{Y}=\mathcal{R}
and the blow-down map induces the refinement
\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{P}_{X}
.

We refer the reader to the excellently written
[
KM15
]
for the proof and just add some description of the exceptional divisors, which is missing from
[
KM15
]
. This will be useful for understanding the difference between real-oriented blow-ups and generalized blow-ups as well as§
3.4
, which shows that the generalized blow-up yields the ‘correct’ base space.

Recall that for the real-oriented blow
\text{Bl}_{D}(X)
of a smooth quasi-projective variety along a normal crossing divisor
D=\{D_{i}\}_{i}
in
X
, we intuitively replace
D_{i}
by the

spherical projectivization

\mathbb{P}_{>0}(N_{D_{i}/X})\coloneqq(N_{D_{i}/X}\setminus 0)/\mathbb{R}_{>0}.

Given now a smooth manifold
Y
with corners and two embedded codimension-
1
boundary strata
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
intersecting in
Z_{12}
, the generalized blow-up of
Y
along
Z_{12}
replaces
Z_{12}
by the
positive part

(3.6)

\mathbb{P}_{+}(N_{Z_{12}/Y})=\left\{(y,[v])\in\mathbb{P}_{>0}(N_{Z_{12}/X})\mid v=v_{1}\oplus v_{2}\text{ with }v_{i}\in N_{Z_{i}/Y}\text{ inward pointing}\right\}.

of the spherical projectivization, where we use that
N_{Z_{12}/Y}
splits as the direct sum
N_{Z_{1}/Y}\oplus N_{Z_{2}/Y}
. In the picture below we show the simplest case.

\import

./blo2woT.pdf_tex

Figure 4.
Corner blowup of
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
.

Remark 3.8
.

Given a Riemannian metric
g
on
Y
, we can identify
\mathbb{P}_{+}(N_{Z_{i}/Y})
with a subset of the sphere normal bundle
SN_{Z_{i}/Y}
of
Z_{i}
and extend this isomorphism to an isomorphism

[0,\epsilon)\times\mathbb{P}_{+}(N_{Z_{12}/Y})\cong U\subset\widetilde{Y},

where
\widetilde{Y}
is the blow-up. Then, the blow-down map
\beta
becomes the map

[0,\epsilon)\times\mathbb{P}_{+}(N_{Z_{12}/Y})\to Y:(t,y,[v])\mapsto\exp_{y}(t\widetilde{v}),

where
\widetilde{v}
is the unique lift of
[v]
to the sphere normal bundle.

Suppose
D
is a normal crossing divisor in
X
with two irreducible smooth components
D_{1},D_{2}
. Applying the above observation to the case of
Y=\text{Bl}_{D}(X)
with
Z_{i}
the preimage of
D_{i}
under the blow-down map, we have that the normal bundle of (the interior of)
Z_{i}
is canonically isomorphic to the hyperplane line bundle

(3.7)

L_{i}\,\coloneqq\,\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{>0}(N_{D/X})}(1)\coloneqq\mathbb{P}_{>0}(N_{D/X})\times_{D}N_{D/X}.

On the other hand, the (interior of the) intersection
Z_{12}
is canonically identified with

(3.8)

Z_{12}\,\cong\,\Bigl(\mathbb{P}_{+}(N_{Z_{1}/X})\times\mathbb{P}_{+}(N_{D_{2}/X})\Bigr)|_{D_{12}}

with normal bundle corresponding to

N_{Z_{12}/Y}\;\cong\;L_{1}|_{Z_{12}}\,\oplus\,L_{2}|_{Z_{12}}

under the identification (
3.8
). The generalized blow-up of
Y
along
Z_{12}
now replaces
Z_{12}
with
\mathbb{P}_{+}(L_{1}|_{Z_{12}}\oplus L_{2}|_{Z_{12}})
. Hence, a point
p
in
\beta^{-1}(Z_{12})
corresponds to a tuple

(3.9)

p=\Bigl(y,[v_{1}],[v_{2}],[v^{\prime}_{1}\oplus v^{\prime}_{2}]\Bigr),

where
y\in D_{ij}
and
v_{i},v^{\prime}_{i}\in(N_{D_{i}/X})_{y}
with
[v_{i}^{\prime}]=[v_{i}]
. The brackets denote the equivalence class under the
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-action on the respective bundle. The key point of (
3.9
) is that the “added data” of
[v^{\prime}_{1}\oplus v^{\prime}_{2}]
yields a ratio obtained by choosing a Riemannian metric on
X
and lifting
v_{i}
and
v_{1}^{\prime}\oplus v_{2}^{\prime}
to unit vectors with respect to that metric. Another consequence is that the normal bundle of the embedding
\beta^{-1}(Z_{12})\hookrightarrow\beta^{-1}(Z_{1})
is exactly the pullback of
L_{2}|_{Z_{12}}
.

3.1.3.
Blow-up of
\mathcal{B}^{P}

We now return to our situation at hand, the real-oriented blow-up
\mathcal{B}^{P}\coloneqq\mathcal{B}^{P}_{n^{+},n^{-}}(d)
of
\mathcal{B}_{n^{+},n^{-}}(d)
. The construction of the generalized blow-up of
[
KM15
]
as written assumes that the codimension one boundary components are embedded. In our situation we do not have this property due to the fact that the contraction maps
T^{\prime}\to T
of trees in
\mathscr{S}
can be fixed by a nontrivial automorphism of
T^{\prime}
, see e.g.,
[
Par19
, Figure 8]
. We therefore split up the construction into two steps.

•

Fix a neighborhood
U
of the closure of
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{2}
, the union of all codimension 2 strata in
\mathcal{B}^{P}
, such that every boundary (closure of codimension
1
points) component in
U
is embedded.

•

Replace
U
with a corner blow-up
[U;\mathcal{R}]
corresponding to a suitable refinement of the monoidal complex
\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{P}_{U}
.

The first step, the choice of
U
we can do immediately. Let us now define the refinement
\mathcal{R}
, recalling that the strata of
\mathcal{B}^{P}
are indexed by decorated trees.

Definition 3.9
.

Given a decorated tree
T
and an enumeration
E(T)=\{e_{1},\dots,e_{n}\}
with associated monoid
\sigma_{T}=\mathbb{N}\langle{e_{1},\dots,e_{n}}\rangle
and a maximal level function
\ell
on
T
, the associated monoid is

(3.10)

\sigma_{T,\ell}=\mathbb{N}\langle{e^{\prime}_{1},\dots,e^{\prime}_{n}}\rangle,

where

(3.11)

e^{\prime}_{i}\coloneqq e_{i}+\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\ell(e_{j})\leq p\ell(e_{i})\\
e\ell(e_{j})<e\ell(e_{i})\end{subarray}}{e_{j}},

with
e\ell((v,w))=\ell(w)
for any edge
e=(v,w)
.

Definition 3.10
.

Given a decorated tree
T
, we let
L_{T}
be the set of maximally leveled level functions on
T
. The
refinement

\mathcal{R}(\sigma_{T})
of
\sigma_{T}
is the refinement generated by
\left\{\sigma_{T,\ell}\mid\ell\in L_{T}\right\}
.

Lemma 3.11
.

\mathcal{R}(\sigma_{T})
is a smooth refinement, and the refinements
\mathcal{R}(\sigma_{T})
form a refinement
\mathcal{R}
of the monoidal complex
\mathcal{P}_{U}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that each
\sigma_{T,\ell}
is a smooth monoid in the sense of
[
KM15
]
. Since
\sigma_{T}
is smooth, it suffices to show that
e^{\prime}_{1},\dots,e^{\prime}_{n}
are linearly independent in
\mathbb{R}\langle{e_{1},\dots,e_{n}}\rangle
. This follows from the definition of a level function. The second claim is a direct consequence of the construction.
∎

Definition 3.12
.

We define the
leveled base space
to be

(3.12)

\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}=\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{n^{+},n^{-}}(d)\coloneqq\frac{[U;\mathcal{R}]\sqcup(\mathcal{B}^{P}\setminus\mathcal{B}^{P}_{2})}{\sim}

where we identify the interior of
[U;\mathcal{R}]
with
U\setminus\mathcal{B}^{P}_{2}
via the blow-down map
[U;\mathcal{R}]\to U
.

Lemma 3.13
.

The space
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{n^{+},n^{-}}(d)
is a smooth oriented manifold with corners whose codimension-
k
boundary strata correspond to
(k+1)
-leveled trees.

Proof.

The proof will be based on
[
KM15
, Proposition 3.2]
which states that the dimension of a monoid
\tau
in a refinement is equal to the codimension of a face
F_{\tau}
corresponding to the monoid in the blow-up. We know that in the complement of
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{2}
, the codimension-
1
strata correspond to trees with exactly two vertices, and there are no strata of higher codimension. Thus the result follows easily in
\mathcal{B}^{P}\setminus\mathcal{B}^{P}_{2}.
Definition
3.12
shows that it remains to consider
[U;\mathcal{R}]
.
It follows from Definition
3.9
that every monoid of dimension
k
in the smooth refinement corresponds to a leveled tree with
(k+1)
-many levels. The result then follows directly from
[
KM15
, Proposition 3.2]
.
∎

Example 3.14
.

We briefly describe the preimage of a (simple) corner stratum of
\mathcal{B}^{P}
under the blow-down map. Let
T_{k}
be the tree with a unique vertex, one input and
k
outputs, and let
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{T_{k}}
be the stratum corresponding to
T_{k}
. For the standard simplex
\Delta^{n}
, we define the
maximally blown-up simplex

\widetilde{\Delta}^{n}
by iteratively blowing up the faces of the simplex, starting with the zero-dimensional faces and ending at a
(n-2)
-dimensional face. Thus, we obtain a sequence

(3.13)

\Delta^{n}\xleftarrow[\text{ blow up vertices}]{\pi_{1}}\Delta^{n}_{1}\xleftarrow[\text{ blow up edges }]{\pi_{2}}\Delta^{n}_{2}\xleftarrow[\text{ blow up $2$-faces}]{\pi_{3}}\dots\xleftarrow[\text{ blow up }(n-2)\text{-faces}]{\pi_{n-2}}\widetilde{\Delta}^{n}

of real-oriented and generalized blow-ups. We write
\widetilde{\pi}\colon\widetilde{\Delta}^{n}\to\Delta^{n}

for the composition of the maps in (
3.13
).
Now, a small enough neighborhood of
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{T_{k}}
is diffeomorphic to
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{T_{k}}\times[0,1)^{k}
. The corner blow-up replaces
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{T_{k}}\times[0,1)^{k}
with
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{T_{k}}\times\widetilde{\Delta}^{k-1}\times[0,1).
Explicitly, in the case of the tree
T_{2}
we have

\import

./blo2.pdf_tex

Figure 5.
Corner blowup of
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
corresponding to the tree with 3 vertices.

3.1.4.
Base space for disconnected domains

We now construct the base space for leveled buildings with disconnected domains, where each component has a unique incoming vertex. While the base space for disconnected Pardon buildings is simply given by the product of base space for (connected) Pardon buildings, the construction of
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}
is more subtle because the level structure is defined for the whole forest, not each tree separately. The first lemma reduces this to the connected setting.

Lemma 3.15
.

There exists a functor
\Phi
from the category
\mathscr{S}^{\bullet}
of forests with
k
components, each with one incoming edge, to the full subcategory
\mathscr{S}_{k,\gamma_{0}}\subset\mathscr{S}
of decorated trees with one incoming edge labeled by a fixed Reeb orbit
\gamma_{0}
, so that the root vertex has energy zero and
k
outgoing edges.

Proof.

The category
\mathscr{S}^{\bullet}
splits into a disjoint union of full subcategories, indexed by the Reeb orbits at the incoming exterior edges. Fix one such subcategory
\mathscr{S}^{\bullet}_{\underline{\gamma}}
, labeled by Reeb orbits
\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{k}
. Let
T_{0}
be the decorated corolla with zero energy, one incoming exterior edge, labeled by an arbitrary Reeb orbit
\gamma_{0}
, and
k
outgoing exterior edges, labeled by
\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{k}
. Then, the functor

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}^{\bullet}_{\underline{\gamma}}\;\to\;\mathscr{S}_{k,\gamma_{0}}

\displaystyle T=T_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup T_{k}\;\mapsto\;T_{0}\#_{i}T_{i}

is well-defined and an isomorphism by inspection.
∎

Intuitively, one can think of the isomorphism by introducing a
ghost vertex
to which all incoming exterior edges of the components of the forest are connected. We write
e_{j}
for the edge connecting
T_{j}
to the ghost vertex in the construction of Lemma
3.15
.
The functor
\Phi
can be upgraded to a functor

(3.14)

P\colon\mathscr{S}^{\bullet}_{k}\times(\{1,\dots,k\},\supseteq)\to\mathscr{S}

by letting
P(T,I)
be the tree obtained from
\Phi(T)
by contracting all edges
e_{j}
with
j\notin I
.

To make the blow-up construction concrete, fix Reeb orbits
\Gamma^{+}
and
\Gamma^{-}
and let
\Lambda\colon\Gamma^{-}\to\Gamma^{+}
be a function. It induces a partition of
\Gamma^{-}
into the sets
\Lambda_{\gamma}:=\Lambda^{-1}(\{\gamma\})
. Fix for each
\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}
a corolla
T_{\gamma}
with a unique positive edge labeled by
\gamma
and negative edges labeled by
\Lambda_{\gamma}
. Suppose the objects in the category
\mathscr{S}_{/T_{\gamma}}
come equipped with a function
d_{\gamma}\colon V(T^{\prime}_{\gamma})\to\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
that is additive under contractions of edges. Set
d_{\gamma}\coloneqq d_{\gamma}(T_{\gamma})
. Then, the product

\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\Lambda}\coloneq\prod\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}(d_{\gamma})}

is going to be the base space for the construction of the global Kuranishi chart for Pardon buildings with disconnected domains stratified by forests that contract onto the forest
\sqcup_{\gamma}T_{\gamma}
. Define the category
\widetilde{\mathscr{S}}
of leveled decorated forests similarly.

Construction 3.16
.

Fix sequences
\Gamma^{\pm}
of Reeb orbits and a partition
\Lambda\colon\Gamma^{+}\to\Gamma^{-}
. Then, we define the base space
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}
as follows. Equip
[0,1)^{|\Gamma^{+}|}\times\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\Lambda}
with the product stratification. Let
U\subset\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\Lambda}
be a neighborhood of the strata of codimension at least
2
, so that each hyperplane in
U
is embedded. The strata of
U
are indexed by a decorated forest
T
and a subset
I\subset\{1,\dots,k\}
, where
k=|\Gamma^{+}|
. Then, the monoid
\sigma_{T,I}
associated to the stratum
S_{T,I}
of
U
is given by

\sigma_{T,I}\coloneqq\bigoplus_{e\in E(T)}\mathbb{N}e\;\oplus\;\bigoplus\limits_{i\in I}\,\mathbb{N}e_{i}\;=\bigoplus_{e\in E(P(T,I))}\mathbb{N}e.

The second equality holds by the definition of
P(T,I)
. We may now apply the algorithm of Proposition
2.10
along with Definition
3.9
for
\sigma_{T,I}=\sigma_{P(T,I)}
to obtain a refinement
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}
of the monoidal complex of
[0,1)^{|\Gamma^{+}|}\times U
. Then, we define
\widetilde{U}
to be the pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U\setminus S_{2}(\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\underline{d}}))\times[0,1)^{|\Gamma^{+}|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}U;\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}{]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[0,1)^{|\Gamma^{+}|}}\times\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\Lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{U}}

where the upper horizontal map is the inverse of the blow-down map, restricted to the complement of the blown-up locus. This admits a canonical smooth structure. Moreover,
\widetilde{U}
is equipped with a canonical smooth map
\widetilde{\beta}\colon\widetilde{U}\to[0,1)^{|\Gamma^{+}|}
. We define the
base space of disconnected buildings

(3.15)

\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(\{0\}^{|\Gamma^{+}|})

to be the preimage of the stratum of highest codimension in
[0,1)^{|\Gamma^{+}|}
.

Lemma 3.17
.

\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}
is a smooth manifold with corners of dimension
\dim(\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\Lambda})+|\Gamma^{+}|-1
.

Proof.

By the universal property of the generalized blow-up, we can do the blow-up iteratively. Another way to see this is that the refinement we obtain can be realized as a sequence of iterated star subdivisions; then it follows from
[
KM15
, Corollary 7.3]
. By Construction
3.16
, the first step is to blow-up
[0,1)^{|\Gamma^{+}|}
to obtain a space
Y
, while the second step is to blow-up
Y\times\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\Lambda}
according to the refinement described above. Using the explicit description in
[
KM15
, §3]
, we see that the preimage
Z\subset Y
of
\{0\}^{|\Gamma^{+}|}\subset[0,1)^{|\Gamma^{+}|}
under the blow-down map is a codimension-
1
face of
Y
and thus a manifold with corners. Then,
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\underline{d}}
is the preimage of
Z
under the generalized blow-up, or equivalently, the blow-up of
Z\times\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\Lambda}
according to the given refinements. Thus, it is a smooth manifold with corners by
[
KM15
, Theorem A]
and has dimension
\dim(\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\Lambda})+|\Gamma^{+}|-1
.
∎

3.2.
Families of buildings

Let
(Y,\lambda)
be a nondegenerate closed contact manifold, and fix a
\lambda
-adapted almost complex structure
J
on the symplectization
\widehat{Y}
of
Y
. We now explain how to obtain
framings
of our punctured domain curves. This will lead to the definition of the (infinite-dimensional) family of buildings, Definition
3.20
, out of which we cut our thickening by a perturbed Cauchy–Riemann equation.

3.2.1.
Framings of buildings in symplectizations

Definition 3.18
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a finite set of Reeb orbits. We call
\widetilde{\lambda}\in\Omega^{1}(Y)
an
\mathcal{F}
-integral approximation
of
\lambda
if

(i)

\widetilde{\lambda}\wedge(d\widetilde{\lambda})^{n}>0
,

(ii)

\int_{S^{1}}\widetilde{\gamma}^{*}\widetilde{\lambda}\in\mathbb{N}
for any
\gamma\in\mathcal{F}
,

(iii)

For any subsets
\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-}\subset\mathcal{F}
, we have

(3.16)

\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\lambda}(\gamma)-\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{-}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\lambda}(\gamma)>0\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(\gamma)-\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{-}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(\gamma)>0

Lemma 3.19
.

There exists an
\mathcal{F}
-integral approximation of
\lambda
for any finite set
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{P}(\lambda)
.

Proof.

Due to the finiteness of
\mathcal{F}
and compactness of
S^{1}
, all conditions are open save for the requirement that
\int_{S^{1}}\widetilde{\gamma}^{*}\widetilde{\lambda}\in\mathbb{N}
. Thus there exists
\lambda^{\prime}
satisfying the other conditions and with
\int_{S^{1}}\widetilde{\gamma}^{*}\lambda^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Q}
for any
\gamma\in\mathcal{F}
. We can ensure this by modifying
\lambda
separately in disjoint neighborhoods of all the orbits
\gamma\in\mathcal{F}
. Multiplying
\lambda^{\prime}
with a sufficiently high positive integer, we obtain the desired
\mathcal{F}
-integral approximation
\widetilde{\lambda}
of
\lambda
.
∎

Given a decorated tree
T
we can thus fix a
\mathcal{P}(T)
-integral approximation
\widetilde{\lambda}
of
\lambda
, where
\mathcal{P}(T)
was defined in Equation (
2.1
), and an integer
p\geq 3
. Given a
J
-holomorphic building
(u,C,z_{*},m_{*})
with underlying dual graph
T_{u}
that contracts to
T
, we will now construct the line bundle that will serve as our “reference line bundle” later on.
For
v\in V(T_{u})
, set

(3.17)

L_{u,v}\coloneqq\mathcal{O}_{C_{v}}\Bigl(\sum\limits_{e\in E_{v}^{+}}{\int_{S^{1}}\gamma_{e}^{*}\widetilde{\lambda}\;z_{v,e}}-\sum\limits_{e\in E_{v}^{-}}{\int_{S^{1}}\gamma_{e}^{*}\widetilde{\lambda}\;z_{v,e}}\Bigr)^{\otimes p}.

By Stokes’ Theorem,

(3.18)

\deg(\omega_{C_{v}}(D_{v})\otimes L_{v})=-2+|D_{v}|+p\int_{\dot{C}_{v}}u_{v}^{*}d\widetilde{\lambda}\;>0

In particular, we obtain a very ample holomorphic line bundle

(3.19)

\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}_{u}:=\omega_{\widetilde{C}}(D)\,\otimes\widetilde{L}_{u}

on the normalization
\widetilde{C}
of
C
, where
D
is the divisor of special points on
\widetilde{C}
. Since
C
has genus
0
, the bundle
\widetilde{L}_{u}
descends to a unique (up to holomorphic isomorphism) holomorphic line bundle
\mathfrak{L}_{u}
on
C
. A basis of the global holomorphic sections of
\mathfrak{L}_{u}
defines an automorphism-free stable map
\iota\colon C\to\mathbb{P}^{d}
so that
H^{1}(C,\iota^{*}\mathcal{O}(1))=0
.
In particular, if the positive/negative exterior edges of
T_{u}
are labeled by
\Gamma^{\pm}
, then
[\iota]\in\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-}}(d)
, as defined in §
3.1
.

Fix now sequences
\Gamma^{\pm}
of Reeb orbits and a relative homology class
\beta\in H_{2}(Y,\Gamma^{+}\sqcup\Gamma^{-})
. Let
T
be the corolla with degree
\beta
and positive/negative exterior edges labeled by
\Gamma^{\pm}
. Fix
L>\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}(\Gamma^{+}),\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}(\Gamma^{-})
. Then, choose a
\mathcal{P}(T)
-integral approximation
\widetilde{\lambda}
and let
d
be the integer of Definition
3.28
. Let
\mathcal{B}^{P}=\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\Gamma^{+}\sqcup\Gamma^{-}}(d)
be the space defined in §
3.1
. Recall that it is a principal torus bundle over the real-oriented blow-up of
\mathcal{B}_{0,\Gamma^{+}\sqcup\Gamma^{-}}(d)
. Let
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{B}^{P}
be the pullback of the universal family of
\mathcal{B}_{0,\Gamma^{+}\sqcup\Gamma^{-}}(d)
.
Due to the action bound, we may fix
\kappa_{L}>0
sufficiently small so that non-trivial cylinders have
\lambda
-energy at least
2\kappa_{L}
.

Definition 3.20
.

We define
\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{Z}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(T)
be the space of tuples
(\varphi,u)
where

i)

\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{P}
lies in the stratum associated to a tree
T_{\varphi}
admitting a contraction
T_{\varphi}\to T
,

ii)

u=([u_{v}])_{v\in V(T_{\varphi})}
is a collection of equivalence classes of smooth maps
u_{v}\colon\dot{C}_{v}\to\widehat{Y}
up to translation, where

•

u_{v}
is
J
-holomorphic near the punctures of
\dot{C}_{v}
,

•

if
x\in C_{v}
is a positive/negative puncture (mapping to a node) of type
1
, then
u_{v}
is positively/negatively asymptotic to the Reeb orbit
\gamma_{e}
, where
e\in E(T^{\prime})
is the associated edge;

•

the matching isomorphism of
\varphi
at the edge
e=(v,v^{\prime})
intertwines
(\widehat{u}_{v})_{z_{v,e}}
and
(\widehat{u}_{v^{\prime}})_{z_{v^{\prime},e}}
,

•

\int_{C_{v}}{u_{v}}^{*}_{Y}d\lambda\geq 0
,

•

if
C_{v}
is unstable, then
\int_{C_{v}}u^{*}_{Y}d\lambda\geq\kappa_{L}
.

We assume
\mathcal{B}^{P}
and
\mathcal{C}
are equipped with
G
-invariant metrics
d_{\mathcal{B}}
and
d_{\mathcal{C}}
respectively, the choice of which is irrelevant. Define

(3.20)

\mathcal{C}_{\geq\epsilon}\coloneqq\{\zeta\in\mathcal{C}\mid d_{\mathcal{C}}(\zeta,\text{Crit}(\pi))\geq\epsilon\}

for
\epsilon>0
.
We equip
\mathcal{Z}
with the topology generated by the following
\epsilon
-neighborhoods for
\epsilon>0
. Given
(\varphi,u)\in\mathcal{Z}
, define
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}(\varphi,u)
to be the subset of points
(\varphi^{\prime},u^{\prime})
such that

•

d_{\mathcal{B}}(\varphi,\varphi^{\prime})<\epsilon
;

•

the (orbits of the) graphs satisfy

d_{\text{H}}\Bigl(\mathbb{R}^{V(T_{\varphi})}\cdot\text{graph}(\varphi,u)|_{\mathcal{C}_{\geq\epsilon}},\mathbb{R}^{V(T_{\varphi^{\prime}})}\cdot\text{graph}(\varphi^{\prime},u^{\prime})|_{\mathcal{C}_{\geq\epsilon}}\Bigr)<\epsilon

in the Gromov-Hausdorff metric, where
T_{\varphi}
is the dual graph of the fiber
\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}
and we choose any representatives of the classes
[u_{v}]
and
[u^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}}]
;

•

for
e\in E(T_{\varphi})
with associated Reeb orbit
\gamma_{e}
and corresponding node
x_{e}\in\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}
we have

d_{Y}(u^{\prime}_{Y}(z),\gamma_{e})<\epsilon

for any
z\in\mathcal{C}_{\varphi^{\prime}}
with
d_{\mathcal{C}}(z,x_{e})\leq\epsilon
.

3.2.2.
Determining unitary framings

Abusing notation, we also denote by
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{Z}
the pullback of the universal family
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma^{+}\sqcup\Gamma^{-}}(d)
. The
\mathcal{G}
-action on
\mathcal{B}^{P}
lifts to a
\mathcal{G}
-action on
\mathcal{Z}
. In contrast to the action on
\mathcal{B}^{P}
, which is not proper, the additional data of the building means that the lifted action is proper, which will be crucial to reduce to the action of the compact group
G
later on.

Lemma 3.21
.

The
\mathcal{G}
-action on
\mathcal{Z}
, given by

g\cdot(s,u)=(g\cdot s,u\circ g^{-1}),

is proper in the sense of Palais,
[
Pal61
]
.

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}
be the real blowup at the images of the sections and the nodes of the fibers of
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{V}
. Fix a smooth metric on
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}
. This restricts to a smooth metric on
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{o}}
. Given now
(s,u)\in\mathcal{Z}
, we see that the projection
u_{Y}
of
u
to
Y
extends to a continuous function
\bar{u}_{Y}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{s}\to Y
. Given any Riemannian metric on
Y
, we obtain an inequality of Lipschitz numbers

L(u_{Y})\leq L(\bar{u}_{Y}).

The Lipschitz number of
\bar{u}_{Y}
is finite since its differential is bounded away from the “puncture circles” and we know its behavior near the “puncture circles”. Thus, we may use the same argument as in
[
AMS24
, Lemma 4.13]
to conclude.
∎

Lemma 3.22
.

Writing
\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{\partial}}\subset\mathcal{Z}
for the closed subspace of
J
-holomorphic building, the quotient map

(3.21)

\psi\colon\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{\partial}}/\mathcal{G}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}(T)

is an isomorphism of orbi-spaces.

Proof.

The surjectivity of (
3.21
) follows from the construction of the very ample line bundles in §
3.1.1
, while injectivity (including the statement about isotropy groups) can be shown as in
[
HS24b
, Discussion 3.16]
. Continuity follows from the definition of the metric on
\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{\partial}}
. Thus, it remains to show that (
3.21
) is open or, equivalently, closed.

This can be checked locally on the target. To this end, let
[u,C]\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}(T)
be arbitrary.
By
[
Par19
, Proposition 3.26]
, we can find a divisor
D\subset Y
so that
u_{Y}\,\pitchfork\,D
and adding
u_{Y}^{-1}(D)
as marked points stabilizes the domain
C
of
u
, and so that this is the minimal number of marked points required to stabilize the domain.
Then, there exists a neighborhood
U\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}(T)
of
[u,C]
so that for any
[u^{\prime},C^{\prime}]\in U
, the map
u^{\prime}_{Y}
intersects
D
transversely and the added points
{u^{\prime}}^{-1}(D)
stabilize
C^{\prime}
.
This yields a continuous map

\mathfrak{f}\colon U\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,\#\Gamma^{-}+\#\Gamma^{+}+m}/S_{m},

where
m=\#u^{-1}(D)
and the symmetric action permutes the last
m
marked points.
Let
\rho_{1},\dots,\rho(d)
be local sections of the universal family

\overline{\mathcal{C}}:=\mathcal{C}_{0,\#\Gamma^{-}+\#\Gamma^{+}+m}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,\#\Gamma^{-}+\#\Gamma^{+}+m}/S_{m}

near
p=\mathfrak{f}([u,C])
, whose images do not meet the nodal points of the fibers and so that for each irreducible component
C_{v}
of
C
we have

(3.22)

\#\{i\mid\rho_{i}(p)\in C_{v}\}=\deg(\mathfrak{L}_{u}|_{C_{v}}^{\otimes p}).

Shrinking
U
if necessary, we may assume the equality in (
3.22
) holds for any point in
U
. Define the holomorphic line bundle
\overline{\mathcal{L}}\coloneqq\mathcal{O}_{\overline{\mathcal{C}}}(\rho_{1}+\dots+\rho(d))
. It pulls back to a complex orbi-line bundle over the universal family of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}(T)
, and has the same multi-degree as
\mathfrak{L}_{u}^{\otimes p}
when restricted to the fiber over
[u,C]
. Therefore, as in
[
HS24b
, Lemma 4.8]
, the forgetful map
\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{\partial}}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}(T)
is locally the projectivization of a continuous orbi-bundle. In particular, the map is closed.
∎

Remark 3.23
.

The above proof strongly relies on the fact that our curves have genus zero.

By
[
HS24b
, Lemma 4.4]
, the
\text{PGL}_{d+1}(\mathbb{C})
-action on
\mathcal{B}_{n}(d)
is proper when restricted to the locus
\mathcal{B}^{\,\text{st}}_{n}(d)\subset\mathcal{B}_{n+\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-}}(d)
of curves with stable domain (for any
n\geq 0
). Therefore,
[
HS24b
, Corollary 4.6]
asserts the existence of a
\text{PGL}_{d+1}(\mathbb{C})
-invariant map

(3.23)

\zeta\colon\mathcal{B}^{\,\text{st}}_{3d^{\prime}}(d)\to\text{PGL}_{d+1}(\mathbb{C})/\text{PU}(d+1)\cong\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{d+1}.

where the isomorphism is induced by the polar decomposition,
[
HS24b
, Lemma 3.8(iii)]
. By averaging, we can choose
\zeta
to be invariant under the
S_{3d^{\prime}}
-action on
\mathcal{B}_{3d^{\prime}+\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-}}^{\,\text{st}}(d)
given by permuting the marked points labeled by
\{1,\dots,3d^{\prime}\}
.
Lemma
3.22
and
[
Par19
, Proposition 3.26]
, refined as in the proof of
[
HS24b
, Lemma 4.3]
, imply that we can find a finite set
\{D_{i},r_{i}\}
of compact codimension-
2
submanifolds with boundary
D_{i}\subset Y
so that the sets

U_{i}:=\left\{(\varphi,u)\in\mathcal{Z}\mid u\,\pitchfork\,D_{i},\,\forall C^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{C}|_{\varphi}:\#C^{\prime}\cap u^{-1}(D_{i})=3\deg(L_{u}|_{C^{\prime}}),\,(\mathcal{C}|_{\varphi},u^{-1}(D_{i}))\text{ is stable}\right\}

form an open cover of
\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{\partial}}
. We replace
\mathcal{Z}
with
\bigcup\limits_{i}{U_{i}}
without further mention. Since
\mathcal{Z}
is metrizable, there exists a
\mathcal{G}
-invariant partition of unity
\{\chi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
subordinate to
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
by Lemma
3.21
and
[
AMS24
, Corollary 4.12]
. Let

\Phi_{i}\colon U_{i}\to\mathcal{B}_{3d^{\prime}}^{\,\text{st}}(d)/S_{3d^{\prime}}

be given by forgetting the marked points labeled by
\Gamma^{\pm}
, adding the intersections as marked points, mapping to
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{3d^{\prime}}(d)/S_{3d^{\prime}}
and then applying the blow-down map. Finally, define

(3.24)

\zeta_{\mathcal{U}}\colon\mathcal{Z}\,\to\,\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{d+1}

by

(3.25)

\zeta_{\mathcal{U}}(\varphi,u)=\sum\limits_{i}{\chi_{i}(\varphi,u)\,\zeta(\Phi_{i}(\varphi,u))}.

In
[
HS24b
]
, the collection
\{(U_{i},D_{i},\chi_{i})\}_{i}
was called a good covering; see Definition 3.10 op. cit. While the above discussion shows that we can do the same in our setting, this definition is too restrictive for the inductive construction in §
4.2
.
A prototype of the issue one faces is that once we fix divisors for one-leveled buildings, we need to find new divisors for two-leveled buildings such that the restriction to the one leveled components has the same intersection combinatorics with the already chosen divisors.
Thus, we replace it with the following variation.

Definition 3.24
.

A
good covering
of
\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{\partial}}
consists of

i)

a finite collection
\{U_{i}\}_{i}
of
\text{PGL}_{d+1}(\mathbb{C})
-invariant open subsets of
\mathcal{Z}
that cover
\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{\partial}}
,

ii)

for each
i
smooth
\mathcal{G}
-equivariant sections
\sigma_{i,j}\colon U_{i}\to\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{o}}|_{U_{i}}
for
1\leq j\leq d+2
together with divisors
D_{ij}\subset Y
so that

•

\sigma_{i,j}
and
\sigma_{i,j^{\prime}}
have disjoint images for
j\neq j^{\prime}

•

for each
j
we have
u(\sigma_{i,j}(\varphi,u))\in D_{i,j}
and
u\,\pitchfork\,D_{i,j}
near
\sigma_{i,j}(\varphi,u)
for any
(\varphi,u)\in U_{i}
,

iii)

a continuous
\mathcal{G}
-invariant function
\chi_{i}\colon\mathcal{Z}\to[0,1]
with support in
U_{i}

so that
\sum\limits_{i}{\chi_{i}}
is positive on
\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{\partial}}
.

Remark 3.25
.

Note that the divisors need not be distinct, i.e., in the construction above, we can take
D_{i,j}=D_{i}
for any
j
. Thus the existence of a good covering follows from the discussion above Definition
3.24
.

The discussion before Definition
3.24
carries over to the definition of good coverings and yields the following statement.

Lemma 3.26
.

A good covering
\mathcal{U}
together with the map in (
3.23
) determines a
\mathcal{G}
-invariant map
\zeta_{\mathcal{U}}\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{d+1}
.∎

3.3.
Kuranishi charts for buildings in symplectizations

We restate our main theorems more precisely here and prove them in the following subsections. Let
\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-}
be finite collections of Reeb orbits of action
\leq L
, and let
\beta\in H_{2}(Y,\Gamma^{+}\sqcup\Gamma^{-})
be a relative homology class. We define
T
to be the decorated corolla as in Definition
2.1
with positive/negative exterior edges labeled by
\Gamma^{+}
and
\Gamma^{-}
, respectively, and with degree
\beta
. We write
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}(T)=\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)
.

Definition 3.27
.

A
pre-perturbation datum

\mathfrak{D}=(\widetilde{\lambda},\nabla,p)
for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J}(T)
consists of

•

a
\mathcal{P}(T)
-integral approximation
\widetilde{\lambda}
of
\lambda
as in Definition
3.18
;

•

a translation-invariant
J
-linear connection
\nabla
on
T\widehat{Y}
;

•

an integer
p\gg 1
.

To such a pre-perturbation datum, we can associate the following spaces. Set

(3.26)

d^{\prime}\coloneqq p\Bigl(\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(\gamma)-\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{-}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(\gamma)\Bigr)

and
d\coloneqq d^{\prime}-2
. Let
\mathcal{B}^{P}\coloneqq\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-}}(d)
be the smooth manifold with corners defined in §
3.1.1
and define the groups

(3.27)

G\coloneqq\text{PU}(d+1)\qquad\qquad\mathcal{G}\coloneqq\text{PGL}_{d+1}(\mathbb{C}).

We let
\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{Z}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(T)
be the family of curves over
\mathcal{B}^{P}
defined in Definition
3.20
. As before, let
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{Z}
be the pullback of the universal family of
\mathcal{B}^{P}
.

Definition 3.28
.

A
perturbation datum

\alpha=(\mathfrak{D},\mathcal{U},\zeta,E,\mu)
extending
\mathfrak{D}
is the data of

•

a good covering
\mathcal{U}=\{(U_{i},\sigma_{i},\chi_{i})\}_{i\in I}
on a subset of
\mathcal{Z}
and a
\mathcal{G}
-equivariant map

\zeta\colon\mathcal{B}^{\text{st}}_{3d^{\prime}}(d)/S_{3d^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{G}/G;

•

a finite-dimensional
G
-representation
E
equipped with an equivariant linear map
\mu\colon E\to\mathcal{V}
, where

(3.28)

\mathcal{V}\coloneqq\left\{\eta\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}^{\circ}\times\widehat{Y},\overline{\text{Hom}}_{\mathbb{C}}(\text{pr}_{1}^{*}T_{\mathcal{C}^{\circ}/\mathcal{B}^{P}},\text{pr}_{2}^{*}T\widehat{Y}))^{\mathbb{R}}\mid\text{ supp}(\eta)/\mathbb{R}\text{ is compact}\right\},

\mathcal{C}^{{}^{\mathrm{o}}}
denoting the complement of the special points of the fibers, so that for any
(\varphi,u)\in\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{\partial}}
with
\zeta_{\mathcal{U}}(\varphi,u)=0
, the Cauchy–Riemann operator

D\bar{\partial}_{J}(u)+\mu(\cdot)|_{\text{graph}(\varphi,u)}\colon C^{\infty}_{c}(\dot{C},u^{*}T\widehat{Y})^{\mathbb{R}}\oplus E_{k}\to\Omega^{0,1}_{c}(\dot{C},u^{*}T\widehat{Y})

is surjective, where
\zeta_{\mathcal{U}}
is the map given by Lemma
3.26
.

Theorem 3.29
.

Let
T
be a decorated tree as at the beginning of the subsection.

(1)

Any pre-perturbation datum
\mathfrak{D}
can be completed to a perturbation datum
\alpha=(\mathfrak{D},\mathcal{U},\zeta,E.\mu)
for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}(T)
as in Definition
3.28
.

(2)

If
\alpha
is a perturbation datum, then Construction
3.30
and Definition
3.31
yield a rel–
C^{1}
global Kuranishi chart with corners for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J}(T)
.

(3)

If
\Gamma^{\pm}
consists of good Reeb orbits, there exists a canonical isomorphism

(3.29)
