\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}}\,\cong\,\mathfrak{o}(\mathbb{R})^{\vee}\otimes\bigotimes\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}\mathfrak{o}_{\gamma}\otimes\bigotimes\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{-}}\mathfrak{o}^{\vee}_{\gamma}

of orientation lines, where
\mathfrak{o}_{\gamma}
is the orientation line of the Reeb orbit, defined in §
3.6
.

Before giving the construction of the thickening, Construction
3.30
, we have to briefly discuss Cauchy-Riemann operators on punctured Riemann surfaces. More details can be found in §
A
or
[
Wen16
, §7]
. Fix
k\geq 4
and
0<\delta<1
so that

(3.30)

\delta<\min\limits_{\gamma\in\mathcal{P}(T)}{\inf|\sigma(A_{\gamma})|}

This is well-defined since
\mathcal{P}(T)
is finite and
\lambda
is nondegenerate. For our purposes here, it suffices to consider the linearized Cauchy–Riemann equation without variation of the domain. Fix thus a possibly nodal Riemann surface
(C,\mathfrak{j})
with underlying graph contracting to
T
. Choose also a complex linear translation-invariant connection
\nabla
on
T\widehat{Y}
and a Riemannian metric on
\widehat{Y}
. Then, we can associate to any smooth map
u\colon\dot{C}\to\widehat{Y}
, which is
J
-holomorphic and asymptotic to a trivial cylinder near the punctures, the operator

(3.31)

D^{\nabla}_{u}\colon W^{k,2,\delta}(C,u^{*}T\widehat{Y})\to W^{k-1,2,\delta}(\widetilde{C},\Omega^{0,1}_{\widetilde{C}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}u^{*}T\widehat{Y})

given by the derivative of

(3.32)

\mathscr{F}_{u}(\xi)=\Phi_{\exp_{u}(\xi)\to u}(d\exp_{u}(\xi)^{0,1}_{J,\mathfrak{j}}).

where
\Phi
is the parallel transport along sufficiently short geodesics. The operator
D_{u}
is independent of the choice of connection and metric if
u
is
J
-holomorphic. Since the exponential weight
\delta
satisfies (
3.30
), we have that
D_{u}
is Fredholm by
[
Wen16
, Lemma 7.10]

with index

(3.33)

\text{ind}(D_{u}^{\nabla})=n\chi(C)-n(|\Gamma^{+}|+|\Gamma^{-}|)+2c_{1}^{\tau}(u^{*}T\widehat{Y})+\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\mu^{\tau}_{\text{CZ}}(\gamma)}-\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{-}}{\mu^{\tau}_{\text{CZ}}(\gamma)}.

Construction 3.30

(Thickening)
.

The thickening
\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}
consists of tuples
(\varphi,u,w)\in\mathcal{Z}\times E_{k}
where

i)

the image
\widecheck{\varphi}
of
\varphi
under the blow-down map
\mathcal{B}^{P}\to\mathcal{B}
satisfies

(3.34)

d_{v}\coloneqq\deg(\widecheck{\varphi}_{v})=|D_{v}|-2+p\,\Bigl(\sum\limits_{e^{\prime}\in E^{\text{int},+}_{v}}{\int\gamma_{v,e^{\prime}}^{*}\widetilde{\lambda}}-\sum\limits_{e^{\prime}\in E^{\text{int},+}_{v}}{\int\gamma_{v,e^{\prime}}^{*}\widetilde{\lambda}}\Bigr);,

for each
v\in V(T_{\varphi})
, where
D_{v}
is the divisor of special points on
C_{v}
and
\gamma_{v,e}
is the Reeb orbit to which
u_{v}
converges at the puncture
z_{v,e}
;

ii)

the matching isomorphism
m_{e}
associated to
\varphi
at the edge
e=(v,v^{\prime})
intertwines
u_{v}
and
u_{v^{\prime}}
in the sense that
\widehat{u}_{v^{\prime}}\circ m_{(v,v^{\prime})}=\widehat{u}_{v}
;

iii)

the perturbation
w
satisfies

(3.35)

\overline{\partial}_{J}\,u_{v}+\mu_{k}(w)|_{\text{graph}(\varphi_{v},u_{v})}=0

for each
v\in V(T_{\varphi})
3
3

3

By the assumption that elements of
\mathcal{V}
are invariant under translation, this equation is well-defined, i.e., does not depend on the choice of representative
u_{v}
.
, and the map

(3.36)

E\to\text{coker}(D_{u}^{\nabla}):w\mapsto[\mu_{k}(w)|_{\text{graph}(\varphi,u)}]

is surjective.

We take the covering group to be

(3.37)

\widehat{G}:=G\times\prod\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}\sqcup\Gamma^{-}}{S}^{1},

where the torus acts by rotating the respective asymptotic marker. The thickening
\mathcal{T}
admits a continuous
\widehat{G}
-equivariant map
\Pi\colon\mathcal{T}\to\mathcal{B}^{P}
. In general,
\mathcal{T}
is not compact and
\Pi
is not surjective.

Definition 3.31

(Obstruction bundle and section)
.

We define the obstruction bundle
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}
to be the trivial bundle

(3.38)

\mathcal{E}\coloneqq E\,\oplus\,\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}

and define the (pre-)obstruction section
\mathfrak{s}_{pre}\colon\mathcal{T}\to\mathcal{E}
by

(3.39)

\ \mathfrak{s}_{pre}(\varphi,u,w)=(w,\zeta_{\mathcal{U}}(\varphi,u)).

Remark 3.32
.

The projection of
\mathfrak{s}_{pre}
to
\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}
is only continuous. We will replace this part of the obstruction section by an equivariant section with the same zero locus that is of class rel–
C^{1}
, see Lemma
3.39
.

Proof of Theorem
3.29
(
1
).

Given the work done in §
3
, it remains to find a perturbation space
(E,\mu)
. Let us summarize why. By Lemma
3.19
, we can find a
\mathcal{P}(T)
-integral approximation
\widetilde{\lambda}
, while the existence of a translation-invariant
J
-linear connection
\widehat{\nabla}
on
T\widehat{Y}
is immediate. Since our curves have genus zero, any integer
p\gg 3
is sufficiently large for
\mathfrak{L}_{u}:=\eqref{eq:linebundle-construction}
to be very ample for any
(\varphi,u)\in\mathcal{Z}
close to
\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{\partial}=0}
and
H^{1}(C,\mathfrak{L}_{u})=0
. Good coverings, as in Definition
3.24
, were constructed in §
3.2.2
. To obtain the existence of
(E,\mu)
, we recall from
[
AMS24
, Definition 4.1]
that a
finite-dimensional approximation scheme
of a smooth
G
-vector bundle
V\to B
over a smooth
G
-manifold is a sequence
(E_{k},\iota_{k})
of finite-dimensional
G
-representations with
G
-equivariant linear maps
\iota_{k}\colon E_{k}\to C^{\infty}_{c}(B,V)
so that

•

E_{k}\subset E_{k+1}
is a sub-representation with
\iota_{k+1}|_{E_{k}}=\iota_{k}
,

•

\bigcup\limits_{k\geq 1}{\text{im}(\iota_{k})}
is dense in
C^{\infty}_{c}(B,V)
in the
C^{\infty}_{\text{loc}}
-topology.

Lemma 3.33
.

Finite-dimensional approximation schemes of
\mathcal{V}
exist.

Proof.

Apply
[
AMS24
, Lemma 4.2]
to the manifold
B\coloneqq\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{o}}\times\widehat{Y}/\mathbb{R}
and the vector bundle
V\coloneqq\overline{\text{Hom}}_{\mathbb{C}}(\text{pr}_{1}^{*}T_{\mathcal{C}^{\circ}/\mathcal{B}^{P}},\mathbb{R}\oplus\text{pr}_{2}^{*}T(\widehat{Y}/\mathbb{R}))
.
∎

Lemma 3.34
.

Let
y=(\varphi,u)\in\mathcal{Z}
be arbitrary. Then there exists
k_{y}\geq 0
so that

(3.40)

E_{k_{y}}\to\text{coker}(D_{u}):w\mapsto[\mu_{k}(w)|_{\text{graph}(\varphi,u)}]

is surjective.

Proof.

This follows from the facts that
D_{u}
is Fredholm and that an element in the cokernel is identically zero if it vanishes on an open subset of
C
. See also
[
Par19
, Proposition 3.26]
.
∎

Lemma 3.35

(Openness of transversality)
.

Given
y\in\mathcal{Z}_{\alpha}
and
k_{y}
as in Lemma
3.34
, there exists a neighborhood
W_{y}\subset\mathcal{Z}
of
y
so that for any
y^{\prime}=(\varphi^{\prime},u^{\prime},w^{\prime})\in W_{y}
the map

(3.41)

E_{k_{y}}\to\text{coker}(D_{u^{\prime}}):w\mapsto[\mu_{k}(w^{\prime})|_{\text{graph}(\varphi^{\prime},u^{\prime})}]

is surjective.

Proof.

Over a given stratum, this follows from the fact that regularity is an open condition. To see that it is also an open condition under gluing, refer to
[
Par19
, Lemma 5.8]
and the discussion loc. cit.
∎

Define the function
\widetilde{k}\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{N}
by

\widetilde{k}(\varphi,u)=\inf\{\ell\mid E_{\ell}\to\text{coker}(D_{u})\text{ is surjective}\}.

By Lemma
3.34
, the function
\widetilde{k}
is well-defined and, by Lemma
3.35
, it is upper semi-continuous. Thus it achieves a maximum
k
on the compact set
\left\{(\varphi,u)\in\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{\partial}}\mid\zeta_{\mathcal{U}}(\varphi,u)=0\right\}
. Setting
E=E_{k}
, we obtain the desired perturbation space.∎

We can now prove Theorem
3.29
(
2
). Let
\alpha
be a perturbation datum. We use the same notation as above. Write
\beta\colon\mathcal{B}^{P}\to\mathcal{B}
for the forgetful map.

Proposition 3.36
.

The forgetful map
\mathcal{T}\to\mathcal{B}^{P}
is a
G
-equivariant
C^{1}_{loc}
-fiber bundle. In particular, it is a topological submersion whose fibers carry a canonical smooth structure.

Proof.

This follows from Theorem
A.1
and Proposition
A.2
.
∎

Lemma 3.37
.

The action
G\times\mathcal{T}/G\times\mathcal{B}^{P}\to\mathcal{T}/\mathcal{B}^{P}
is fiberwise locally linear and of class rel–
C^{1}
.

Proof.

The action map is continuous by the definition of the topology on
\mathcal{T}
. The fact that it is of class rel–
C^{1}
follows from the gluing result in §
A
, while the fiberwise local linearity is a consequence of relative
C^{1}
-regularity and the same argument as in
[
HS24b
, Lemma 5.9]
.
∎

Lemma 3.38
.

The forgetful map
\mathfrak{s}_{\text{pre}}^{-1}(0)\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J}(T)
descends to an isomorphism

\mathfrak{s}_{\text{pre}}^{-1}(0)/G\,\cong\,\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J}(T)

of orbispaces.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.22
, it suffices to show that
\mathfrak{s}_{\text{pre}}^{-1}(0)/G\,\cong\,\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{\partial}}/\mathcal{G}
. This follows from the
\mathcal{G}
-equivariance of
\zeta_{\mathcal{U}}
.
∎

The obstruction bundle
\mathcal{E}
is trivially of class rel–
C^{1}
; however, the obstruction section
\mathfrak{s}_{\text{pre}}
is only continuous since the cut-off functions we construct in §
3.2.2
are only continuous.

Lemma 3.39
.

We can replace
\mathfrak{s}_{\text{pre}}
by a
\widehat{G}
-equivariant obstruction section
\mathfrak{s}
of class rel–
C^{1}
with

\mathfrak{s}_{\text{pre}}^{-1}(0)=\mathfrak{s}^{-1}(0).

Proof.

We only have to replace the projection of
\mathfrak{s}_{\text{pre}}
to the second summand. This follows from a mollification argument as in
[
AMS24
, Lemma 4.55]
.
∎

3.4.
Leveled buildings

For the flow category, it is important to use leveled buildings and not Pardon buildings. Thus, we prove here that our global Kuranishi charts constructed in §
3.3
also yield global Kuranishi charts for the buildings classically used in SFT.

Theorem 3.40
.

Suppose
\Gamma^{\pm}
are collections of Reeb orbits of total action
<L
,
\beta
is a relative homology class, and
\alpha
is a perturbation datum for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)
. Then

\mathcal{K}^{\mathbb{R}}\coloneqq\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{P}}\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}

is a global Kuranishi chart with corners for the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\text{SFT}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)
of leveled SFT buildings.

The proof relies heavily on our adaptation of the gluing theorem in
[
Par19
]
, developed in §
A
. We first start with a discussion of local charts in the corner blow-up
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}
of
\mathcal{B}^{P}
and then prove Proposition
3.45
which is the key ingredient for Theorem
3.40
.

3.4.1.
Local charts for
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{P}
and by abuse of notation let
\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\varphi}}
denote a small neighborhood of
\varphi
in the stratum corresponding to the tree type
T_{\varphi}
. Recall that the gluing results
A.1.3
prove that for a small enough
\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\varphi}}
, there is a local chart

g_{b}\colon G_{T_{\varphi}}\times\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\varphi}}\to\mathcal{B}^{P}.

Recall from the discussion below Theorem
3.7
that points in
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}
correspond to points in
\mathcal{B}
along with additional data coming from the normal bundles to the corner strata that are blown up. Concretely, an element
p\in\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}
lying over the intersection
D_{I}=\cap_{i\in I}D_{i}
of divisors is given by

(3.42)

p=(y,[v_{1}],\dots,[v_{I}],[v^{\prime}_{1,1}\oplus\dots\oplus v^{\prime}_{1,k_{1}}],,\dots,[v^{\prime}_{\ell,1}\dots\dots\oplus v^{\prime}_{\ell,k_{\ell}}])

where
y\in D_{I}
and
v_{i},v_{i}^{\prime}\in(N_{D_{i}/\mathcal{B}})_{y}
are related by a positive scaling. We thus can make the following observation.

Lemma 3.41
.

Given
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{P}
with underlying decorated tree
T
, a choice of tangent vector
\{s_{v,e}\}_{v\in V(T),e\in E^{\text{int}}_{v}}
at each marked point determines a lift
\widetilde{\varphi}\in\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}
of
\varphi
.∎

Let
(T_{\varphi},\ell)
be the underlying leveled tree of
\widetilde{\varphi}
. Then, a sufficiently small neighborhood of
\widetilde{\varphi}
in the fiber
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}|_{\varphi}
of the blow-down map is diffeomorphic to
\prod_{j=1}^{\max\ell}(\Delta^{\#\ell^{-1}(j)-1})^{\text{int}}
by the natural isomorphism

(3.43)

(\Delta^{k})^{\text{int}}\,\cong\,(\mathbb{R}_{>0})^{k+1}/\mathbb{R}_{>0}.

The stratum carries a natural action of
(\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{R}^{e\ell^{-1}(i)}_{>0},\times)
, given by scaling the
v_{*}^{\prime}
terms in (
3.42
). We will show that a local chart
g_{b}\colon G_{T_{\varphi}}\times\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\varphi}}\to\mathcal{B}^{P}
centered at
\varphi
can be lifted to give a local chart centered at
\widetilde{\varphi}
in the leveled base space
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}
. To this end, let
k=\max\ell-1
be the number of ‘gaps’ between levels. The level function induces a function
el\colon E(T_{\varphi})\to\mathbb{N}
by the relation

e\ell(e)=\ell(v^{\prime})

when
e=(v,v^{\prime})
with
\ell(v^{\prime})>\ell(v)
.
There is a natural embedding,

\varDelta\colon[0,1)^{k}\times\prod_{j=1}^{\max\ell}(\Delta^{\#\ell^{-1}(j)-1})^{\text{int}}\hookrightarrow G_{T_{\varphi}/}

defined by

\varDelta(t_{1},\dots,t_{k},w_{1},\dots,w_{k})=(\Delta^{w_{1}}_{e\ell^{-1}(1)}(t_{1}),\Delta^{w_{2}}_{e\ell^{-1}(2)}(t_{2}),\dots,\Delta^{w_{k}}_{e\ell^{-1}(k)}(t_{k}))

where
\Delta^{w_{i}}_{e\ell^{-1}(i)}(x)
is the linear embedding of the ray
[0,1)\mapsto[0,1)^{e\ell^{-1}(i)}
corresponding to the point
w_{i}
in the simplex
\Delta^{\#\ell^{-1}(i)-1}
. Here we use the extension of (
3.43
) to an isomorphism

\mathbb{R}_{+}^{k}/\mathbb{R}_{>0}\cong\Delta^{k-1}.

See Figure
5
for a pictorial description.

Lemma 3.42
.

There exists a diffeomorphism
lg_{b}
making

(3.44)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}0,1)^{k}\times\prod_{j=1}^{\max\ell}(\Delta^{\#\ell^{-1}(j)-1})^{\text{int}}\times\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\varphi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\mathbb{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{T_{\varphi}/}\times\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\varphi}}\quad}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces lg_{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varDelta\times\text{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{b}}

into a Cartesian square, up to shrinking
\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\varphi}}
.

Proof.

This follows from the explicit local description of the generalized blow-up in
[
KM15
, §3]
and our identification of the fiber
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}|_{\varphi}
with the product of interiors of simplices.
∎

3.4.2.
Leveled thickening

Fix two collections
\Gamma^{\pm}
of Reeb orbits of total action
<L
and let
\alpha
be a perturbation datum for the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)
of Pardon buildings of degree
\beta
. Let
\mathcal{K}=\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}
be the associated global Kuranishi chart constructed in §
3.3
and recall that its base space
\mathcal{B}^{P}
is a torus bundle over the blow-up of a subset of regular stable maps in some complex projective space. By §
3.1.2
, there exists a further generalized blow-up
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathcal{B}^{P}
based on refining decorated trees to decorated trees with levels.
We now use
\alpha
to construct a ‘leveled thickening’
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbb{R}}
and will show that it is homeomorphic to the pullback of
\mathcal{T}
along
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathcal{B}^{P}
, see Corollary
3.46
.

Definition 3.43
.

The
leveled thickening

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbb{R}}\coloneq\overline{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathbb{R}}(T)
is given set-wise by the disjoint union

(3.45)

\mathcal{T}^{\mathbb{R}}\coloneqq\bigsqcup\limits_{T^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}}\to T_{\ell}}{\raisebox{1.99997pt}{$\mathcal{T}^{\mathbb{R}}(T^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}})$}\left/\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\text{Aut}(T^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}}/T_{\ell})$}\right.}

where
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbb{R}}(T^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}})
is the quotient
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbb{R}}(T^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}})=\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathbb{R}}(T^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}})/\mathbb{R}^{\max\ell^{\prime}}
of the space
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathbb{R}}(T^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}})=\{(\varphi,u,w)\}
, where

•

\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T^{\prime}}
,

•

u=(u_{v})_{v\in V(T^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}})}
is a sequence of maps, where

–

for
v\in V_{nt}(T^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}})=V(T^{\prime})

u_{v}\colon(\dot{\mathcal{C}}|_{\varphi})_{v}\to\mathbb{R}\times Y

is a smooth map representing
\beta_{v}
, which is positively/negatively asymptotic near the puncture
z_{v,e}\in(\mathcal{C}|_{\varphi})_{v}
to the trivial cylinder over
\gamma_{e}
, while

–

for
v\in V_{t}(T^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}})
,
u_{v}
is the trivial cylinder over the associated Reeb orbit;

•

w\in V_{r}
is a vector so that for any
v\in V_{nt}(T^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}})
we have

(3.46)

\bar{\partial}_{J}\,u_{v}\ +\ \mu_{k}(w)|_{\text{graph}(\check{\varphi}_{v},u_{v})}\ =\ 0.

We require that
(\varphi,u,w)
satisfy the regularity assumption (
3.36
) and equip
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbb{R}}
with the Gromov topology.

Remark 3.44
.

An unbranched trivial cylinder is regular by
[
Par19
, Lemma 2.40]
, so it is not an issue that we do not perturb them.

Proposition 3.45
.

Fix a point
(\varphi,u_{0},w)\in\mathcal{T}^{\mathbb{R}}
. Let
g\colon G_{T_{\varphi}/}\times\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\varphi}}\times N\hookrightarrow\mathcal{T}
denote the gluing map as constructed in the proof of Theorem
A.1
. Then, there is a lift
lg
of the map
g
to a gluing map for the leveled thickening
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbb{R}}
such that the following diagram is Cartesian,

(3.47)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}0,1)^{k}\times\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\varphi}}\times\prod_{j=1}^{\max\ell}(\Delta^{\#\ell^{-1}(j)-1})^{\text{int}}\times N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{\mathbb{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{T_{\varphi}/}\times\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\varphi}}\times N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces lg}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

where
U^{\mathbb{R}}
and
U
are neighborhoods of the points
(\varphi,u_{0},w)
and
(\varphi,[u_{0}],w)
in
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbb{R}}
and
\mathcal{T}
respectively.
The vertical arrow on the right is the natural projection map induced by forgetting the level data. The left vertical arrow
p
is defined by

\rho(t,b,x,u,w)=(\varDelta(t,x),b,\overline{u},w),

where the map
u\mapsto\overline{u}
forgets the trivial cylinders in
u
and quotients by more translations.

Proof.

We first choose the lift
\widetilde{\varphi}
of
\varphi
, using the leveled building
u_{0}
. The key observation is that a cylindrical chart at a puncture
p_{v,e}
determines a tangent vector in
T_{p_{v,e}}C_{v}
. Indeed, a cylindrical chart can be compactified to obtain a disc chart
\vartheta\colon\mathbb{D}^{2}_{r}\to C_{v}
so that
\vartheta(0)=p_{v,e}
and the associated tangent vector is
d\vartheta(0)1\in T_{p_{v,e}}C_{v}
. Using these tangent vectors, Lemma
3.41
gives us a lift
\widetilde{\varphi}
of
\varphi
in
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}
. We will see that the leveled building determines cylindrical charts up to first order and thus a lift. We first fix a representative of
u_{0}
.
As in §
A.1.3
, choose sections
q^{\prime}_{v}
of the smooth locus of the universal family
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{B}^{P}
near
\varphi
. This determined a unique representative
\overline{u}_{0}\colon\mathcal{C}^{o}_{\varphi}\to\mathbb{R}\times Y
by requiring that
\overline{u}_{0}(q^{\prime}_{v})\in\{0\}\times Y
for each
v
. We can similarly add marked points
q^{\prime}_{v}
to get a representative
\widetilde{u}_{0}
of the leveled building
u_{0}
such that
\widetilde{u}_{0}(q^{\prime}_{v})=0
. Fix cylindrical charts near each puncture
p\in\mathcal{C}|_{\varphi}
such that
u_{0}
is given by

u_{0}(s,t)=(L_{\gamma}s,\widetilde{\gamma}(t))+O(e^{-ks})

in these local coordinates, where
\widetilde{\gamma}
is a parametrization of
\gamma
given by the base point (which is non-unique if
\gamma
is multiply-covered). We call such a chart a
normalizing end
and use them together with the above observation to obtain a lift
\widetilde{\varphi}
of
\varphi
by Lemma
3.41
. A direct computation shows that
\widetilde{\varphi}
does not depend on the choice of representative of the leveled building
u_{0}
or the sections
q^{\prime}_{v}
. Using this lift
\widetilde{\varphi}
, we get a local chart
g_{b}\colon G_{T_{\varphi}}\times\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\varphi}}\to\mathcal{B}^{P}
and its lift
lg_{b}
as in (
3.44
).

We first construct
lg
on
\{0\}^{k}\times\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\varphi}}\times\prod_{j=1}^{\max\ell}(\Delta^{\#\ell^{-1}(j)-1})^{\text{int}}\times N
, we can extend it to the whole space by a similar construction as in Theorem
A.1
.
We define
{lg}
on
\{0\}^{k}\times\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\varphi}}\times\prod_{j=1}^{\max\ell}(\Delta^{\#\ell^{-1}(j)-1})^{\text{int}}\times N
as

{lg}\colon\{0\}^{k}\times\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\varphi}}\times\prod_{j=1}^{\max\ell}(\Delta^{\#\ell^{-1}(j)-1})^{\text{int}}\times N\to U^{\mathbb{R}}|_{(T_{\varphi},\ell)}

given by the relation

{lg}\Bigl(lg_{b}^{-1}(\widetilde{\varphi}),[u],w^{\prime}\Bigr):=(\varphi,u,w^{\prime}),

where
(\varphi,u,w^{\prime})
lies in a small enough neighborhood
U^{\mathbb{R}}|_{(T_{\varphi},\ell)}\subset\mathcal{T}^{\mathbb{R}}|_{(T_{n}u,\ell)}
in the restriction of
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbb{R}}
to the strata of leveled tree type
(T_{\varphi},\ell).

Finally, we extend
{lg}
to obtain the required lift
lg
of
g
as stated in the diagram (
3.47
) by a construction similar to that of
\Glue
in the proof of Theorem
A.1
. The construction of the diagonal map
\Delta
ensures that while performing the gluing of the target as in §
A.1.2
, we glue cylinders of the same length between two levels. The rest of the gluing construction can be followed verbatim to obtain the leveled gluing map
lg
.
∎

We can now prove the key result that will allow us to show that the global Kuranishi chart for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}(T)
pulled back along
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathcal{B}^{P}
yields a global Kuranishi for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}_{\text{SFT}}(T_{\ell})
.

Corollary 3.46
.

There exists a canonical map
\pi^{\mathbb{R}}\colon\mathcal{T}^{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}
so that the following square

(3.48)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}^{\mathbb{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\mathbb{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

is a pullback square.

Proof.

In order to construct
\pi^{\mathbb{R}}
, fix open covers
\{U_{i}^{\mathbb{R}}\}_{i\in I^{\mathbb{R}}}
of
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbb{R}}
and
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
\mathcal{T}
with maps
i\mapsto i^{\prime}
so that there exist local charts
lg_{i}
and
g_{i^{\prime}}
as in (
3.47
) and so that we have diffeomorphisms
lg_{b,i}
and
g_{b,i^{\prime}}
onto
V_{i}^{\mathbb{R}}\subset\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}
for
i\in I^{\mathbb{R}}
and
V_{i^{\prime}}\subset\mathcal{B}^{P}
for
i^{\prime}\in I
as in (
3.44
), with
\pi(U_{i^{\prime}})\subset V_{i^{\prime}}
for each
i^{\prime}
. Moreover, choose the
U_{i}^{\mathbb{R}}
so that

\Pi^{-1}(U_{i^{\prime}})\,=\,\bigcup\limits_{i\in I^{\mathbb{R}}_{i^{\prime}}}{U_{i}^{\mathbb{R}}}.

for each
j\in I
and, shrinking the sets
V_{i}^{\mathbb{R}}
, that
V_{i}^{\mathbb{R}}\neq V_{j}^{\mathbb{R}}
for
i\neq j
with
i^{\prime}\neq j^{\prime}
.
Increasing the cover
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
trivially, we may assume
I^{\mathbb{R}}=I
. Thus, we may define
\pi^{\mathbb{R}}_{i}\colon U_{i}^{\mathbb{R}}\to V_{i}^{\mathbb{R}}
to be the composition

(3.49)

U_{i}^{\mathbb{R}}\,\xrightarrow{lg_{i}^{-1}}\,[0,1)^{k_{i}}\times\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{i}}\times\prod_{j=1}^{\max\ell_{i}}(\Delta^{\#\ell_{i}^{-1}(j)-1})^{\text{int}}\times N_{i}\\
\longrightarrow\,[0,1)^{k_{i}}\times\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{i}}\times\prod_{j=1}^{\max\ell_{i}}(\Delta^{\#\ell_{i}^{-1}(j)-1})^{\text{int}}\,\xrightarrow{lg_{b,i}}V_{i}^{\mathbb{R}}.

This map makes the square

(3.50)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{i}^{\mathbb{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{i}^{\mathbb{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{i}^{\mathbb{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi|_{U_{i}}}

commute due to the commutativity of (
3.44
) and (
3.48
). That
U_{i}^{\mathbb{R}}
is the fiber product can be checked using the local coordinates
lg_{i}
and
g_{i}
as well as their counterparts on the base, whence it becomes immediate. The universal property of the fiber product implies that
\pi_{i}^{\mathbb{R}}
agrees with
\pi_{j}^{\mathbb{R}}
on the intersection
U_{i}^{\mathbb{R}}\cap U_{j}^{\mathbb{R}}
, yielding the map
\pi^{\mathbb{R}}
.
Moreover, it follows from (
3.47
) that

(3.51)

U_{i}^{\mathbb{R}}\,=\,\Pi^{-1}(U_{i})\cap(\pi^{\mathbb{R}})^{-1}(V_{i}^{\mathbb{R}}).

Thus we may conclude by a formal argument, using that the squares (
3.50
) are Cartesian.
∎

3.4.3.
Disconnected buildings

Let us now here also record the construction of a global Kuranishi chart for moduli spaces of disconnected leveled buildings.

Definition 3.47
.

Suppose
\Lambda\colon\Gamma^{-}\to\Gamma^{+}
is a function, and let
\beta=(\beta-\gamma)_{\gamma}\in\Gamma^{+}
be a sequence of relative homology classes. We define
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}_{\text{SFT}}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)_{\Lambda}
to be the moduli space of leveled buildings with
|\Gamma^{+}|
components, where the curve restricted to a connected component is positively asymptotic to a unique Reeb orbit
\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}
and negatively asymptotic to the Reeb orbits in
\Lambda_{\gamma}\coloneqq\Lambda^{-1}(\gamma)
.

The corresponding moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)_{\Lambda}
of Pardon buildings is simply the product

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-})_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\prod\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}(\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma};\beta_{\gamma})}.

However, in the case of leveled buildings, we have to incorporate the relative translations between components.

Proposition 3.48
.

Let
\mathcal{K}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}
be the global Kuranishi chart constructed for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}(\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma};\beta_{\gamma})
in Theorem
3.29
with base space
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}
. Then, letting
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}
be the base space constructed in §
3.1.4
, the pullback chart

\mathcal{K}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}\,\coloneqq\,\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}\times_{\prod\limits_{\gamma}{\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}}}\prod\limits_{\gamma}{\mathcal{K}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}}

is a global Kuranishi chart with corners for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}_{\text{SFT}}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)_{\Lambda}
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to the proof of Theorem
3.40
, using the correspondence between forests and trees with a ‘ghost vertex’ discussed at the beginning of §
3.1.4
.
∎

3.5.
Kuranishi charts for buildings in symplectic cobordisms

In this subsection we construct global charts for moduli spaces of (leveled) buildings in symplectic cobordisms. Fix thus an exact symplectic cobordism
(\widehat{X},\omega=d\lambda)
from
(Y^{-},\lambda^{-})
to
(Y^{+},\lambda^{+})
as well as open embeddings

\displaystyle\Theta^{+}\colon(N,\infty)\times Y^{+}\to\widehat{X}

\displaystyle\Theta^{-}\colon(-\infty,-N)\times Y^{-}\to\widehat{X}

for some
N\gg 0
, so that
(\Theta^{\pm})^{*}\lambda=e^{s}\lambda^{\pm}
and
X\coloneqq\widehat{X}\setminus(\text{im}(\Theta^{+})\cup\text{im}(\Theta^{-}))
is compact.

Fix an
\omega
-compatible almost complex structure
\widehat{J}
on
\widehat{X}
whose pullback under
\Theta^{\pm}
is an adapted almost complex structure
J^{\pm}
on
\widehat{Y}^{\pm}
.

3.5.1.
Base space

The construction of the base space for buildings in symplectic cobordisms is similar.

Definition 3.49
.

Given a stable map
\varphi\colon C\to\mathbb{P}^{d}
of genus zero whose domain has a unique node
x
, we say that
x
is of
type
0
if it is non-separating or if it separates
C
into irreducible components
C_{0}
and
C_{1}
of degree
d_{0}
, respectively
d_{1}
so that

(3.52)

\mathrm{d}_{x}:=(d_{0}-p^{+}\sum\limits_{z^{+}_{i}\in C_{0}}{d_{i}^{+}}+p^{-}\sum\limits_{z^{-}_{j}\in C_{0}}{d_{j}^{-}})-(d_{1}-p^{+}\sum\limits_{z^{+}_{i}\in C_{1}}{d_{i}^{+}}+p^{-}\sum\limits_{z^{-}_{j}\in C_{1}}{d_{j}^{-}})=0.

We say
x
is of
type
1
with order
|\mathrm{d}_{x}|
otherwise.

We let
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{c}
be the real-oriented blow-up at the normal crossing divisor with irreducible components given by the divisors corresponding to curves with a unique node that has type
1
. Then, we define
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{c}
to be the total space of the torus bundle over
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{c}
obtained by adding asymptotic markers at the marked points.

Recall from Lemma
3.5
that there is a stratification
P\colon\mathcal{B}^{P}\to\mathscr{S}^{o}
which assigns to a map the tree type of its domain. Here, we can upgrade this to a stratification that keeps track of the targets by using the convention discussed in Remark
3.52
. For a vertex
v\in V(T)
let
D_{C_{v}}\subset C_{v}
be the divisor of special points on
C_{v}
. Then, define
\ast^{\pm}(v)
by setting

•

\ast^{\pm}(v)=0
if

p^{+}\mid\;\underbracket{\left(\deg(\varphi\!\mid_{C_{v}})-\deg(\omega_{C_{v}}(D_{C_{v}}))\right)}_{\neq 0}

•

\ast^{\pm}(v)=1
if

p^{-}\mid\underbracket{\left(\deg(\varphi\!\mid_{C_{v}})-\deg(\omega_{C_{v}}(D_{C_{v}}))\right)}_{\neq 0}

•

if
(\deg(\varphi|_{C_{v}})-\deg(\omega_{C_{v}}(D_{C_{v}})))=0
, then

(3.53)

\ast^{\pm}(v)=\begin{cases}1&\text{ if }p^{-}\mid|\mathrm{d}_{x}|\\
0&\text{ if }p^{+}\mid|\mathrm{d}_{x}|,\end{cases}

•

if
p^{\pm}\not\mid(\deg(\varphi|_{C_{v}})-\deg(\omega_{C_{v}}(D_{C_{v}})))
, then
\ast^{+}(v)=0
and
\ast^{-}(v)=1
.

Remark 3.50
.

Notice that the discussion above does not lift the stratification to
\mathscr{S}^{c}
since not all morphisms of
\mathscr{S}
induce morphisms in
\mathscr{S}^{c}
. Moreover, at this stage there is not always a function
\ast
on
E(T)
for which
(T,\ast,\ast^{\pm})
is a cobordism tree.
This issue can be resolved by a corner-blow-up construction as explained below.

In order to obtain the leveled base space
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{c}
, we need a preliminary definition. We call
(T,\ast_{\pm})
a
pre-cobordism tree (or forest)
if the functions
\ast_{\pm}\colon V(T)\to\{0,1\}
satisfy the conditions in Definition
2.17
. Similarly,
(T,\ast_{\pm},\ell)
is a
leveled pre-cobordism tree
if the level function
\ell
satisfies the conditions in Definition
2.18
.

Then, we define the
leveled cobordism base space

\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{c}
to be the space obtained by applying the generalized blow-up on
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{c}
associated to the refinement arising from maximally leveled pre-cobordism trees. This construction is similar to the construction for base space for leveled buildings. We may assume that
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{c}
carries a stratification by leveled cobordism trees by removing the locus corresponding to leveled pre-cobordism trees, which do not support a leveled cobordism tree as in Definition
2.18
.

3.5.2.
Framings

In order to obtain framings of buildings in
\widehat{X}
, we use a similar construction as in §
3.1.1
. However, we vary it somewhat to ensure that (in most cases) we can already see from the base space which ‘part’ of the cobordism an irreducible component is mapped to.

Definition 3.51
.

Let
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}^{+}\sqcup\mathcal{F}^{-}
be a finite set of Reeb orbits of
\lambda^{+}
and
\lambda^{-}
. We call a
1
-form
\widetilde{\lambda}
on
\widehat{X}
an
\mathcal{F}
-integral approximation
if

(i)

(\Theta^{\pm})^{*}\widetilde{\lambda}=e^{s}\widetilde{\lambda}^{\pm}
, where
\widetilde{\lambda}^{\pm}
is an
\mathcal{F}^{\pm}
-integral approximation,

(ii)

\forall\Gamma^{\pm}\subset\mathcal{F}\,:\,\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}(\Gamma^{+})-\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}(\Gamma^{-})>0\,\Rightarrow\,\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(\Gamma^{+})-\mathcal{A}_{wt\lambda}(\Gamma^{-})>0
.

The existence of
\widetilde{\lambda}
follows from the same argument as in Lemma
3.19
. Fix two prime numbers
p^{\pm}
so that

(3.54)

p^{-}>\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\mathscr{F}^{+}}{\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(\gamma)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad p^{+}\gg p^{-}

Given a smooth stable building
u=(u_{v},C_{v},z_{v,*}^{\pm})
with dual graph
T
, recall that the vertices of
T
are decorated with a pair of symbols
*^{\pm}(v)\in\{+,-\}^{2}
4
4

4

In Definition
2.17
we had that
\ast^{\pm}
took values in
\{0,1\}
. We abuse notation in this subsection by identifying
0\sim+,1\sim-
to reduce clutter in (
3.55
).

so that
u_{v}
maps to
Y^{*^{\pm}(v)}
if the two symbols agree and to
\widehat{X}
if they disagree. Define for
v\in V(T)
the line bundle

(3.55)

L_{u,v}\coloneqq\mathcal{O}_{C_{v}}\Bigl(p^{*^{+}(v)}\sum\limits_{e\in E_{v}^{+}}{\int\gamma_{e}^{*}\widetilde{\lambda}}-p^{*^{-}(v)}\sum\limits_{e\in E_{v}^{-}}{\int\gamma_{e}^{*}\widetilde{\lambda}}\Bigr).

As before, this yields a holomorphic line bundle

(3.56)

\mathfrak{L}_{u}\coloneqq\omega_{C}(D)\otimes L_{u}

on
C
, where
D
is the divisor of marked points, which is unique up to holomorphic isomorphism. By the definition of the
\mathcal{F}
-integral approximation and the choice of
p^{\pm}
,
\deg(\mathfrak{L}_{u}|_{C^{\prime}})>0
for each irreducible component
C^{\prime}
of
C
. Thus, we obtain a
\text{PGL}_{d+1}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit in
\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-}}(d)
as before, where
d=\deg(\mathfrak{L}_{u})
.

Remark 3.52
.

The numbers
p^{\pm}
were chosen in this specific way so that one can see from the framing which irreducible components are mapped to the ends
\widehat{Y}^{\pm}
and which are mapped to
\widehat{X}
proper. Concretely, given a framing
\varphi\colon C\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{d}
associated to the line bundle
\mathfrak{L}_{u}
, an irreducible component
C^{\prime}\subset C
is mapped to
\widehat{Y}^{\pm}
if and only if

p^{\pm}\mid\underbracket{\deg(\varphi|_{C^{\prime}})-\deg(\omega_{C}|_{C^{\prime}})}_{\neq 0}

while in the case where
\deg(\varphi|_{C^{\prime}})-\deg(\omega_{C}|_{C^{\prime}})=0
, the component
C^{\prime}
is mapped to
\widehat{Y}^{\pm}
if and only if
p^{\pm}\mid|\mathrm{d}_{x}|
where
x
is any type 1 node in
C^{\prime}
.

3.5.3.
Families and local models

We can now define the analogue of the infinite-dimensional spaces
\mathcal{Z}
of §
3.2.2
. Fix a decorated corolla
T
in
\mathscr{S}^{c}
and let
\widetilde{\lambda}
be a
\mathcal{P}(T)
-integral approximation, where we can define
\mathcal{P}(T)
for a cobordism tree as in Equation (
2.1
). Let
\Gamma^{\pm}
be the Reeb orbits labeling the positive and negative edges of
T
, and set
L=\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}(\Gamma^{+})
. Fix a constant
\kappa_{L}>0
so that any non-trivial
J
-holomorphic map between Reeb orbits of action at most
L
has
\omega
-energy at least
2\kappa_{L}
.

Definition 3.53
.

We define
\mathcal{Z}^{c}=\mathcal{Z}^{c}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(T)
to consist of tuples
(\varphi,T^{\prime},u)
of the form

i)

\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{P}
with image
\widecheck{\varphi}\in\mathcal{B}
;

ii)

T^{\prime}\in\mathscr{S}^{c}_{/T}
is mapped to
T_{\varphi}
by the forgetful functor
\mathscr{S}^{c}\to\mathscr{S}
and
\widecheck{\varphi}\in\mathcal{B}
satisfies

\deg(\widecheck{\varphi}_{v})=|D_{v}|-2+p^{\star^{+}(v)}\sum\limits_{\gamma\in E^{+}_{v}}{\int\gamma_{e}^{*}\widetilde{\lambda}}-p^{\star^{-}(v)}\sum\limits_{\gamma\in E^{-}_{v}}{\int\gamma_{e}^{*}\widetilde{\lambda}}

for each
v\in V(T_{\varphi})
, where
D_{v}
is the divisor of special points on
C_{v}
;

iii)

u=(u_{v})_{v\in V(T^{\prime})}
is a collection of maps where

(a)

u_{v}
is an equivalence class of smooth maps
\dot{C}_{v}\to\widehat{Y}^{+}
up to translation if
*^{\pm}(v)=0
,

(b)

u_{v}
is an equivalence class of smooth maps
\dot{C}_{v}\to\widehat{Y}^{-}
up to translation if
*^{\pm}(v)=1
,

(c)

u_{v}
is a smooth map
\dot{C}_{v}\to\widehat{X}
whenever
v
is a nontrivial vertex with
\ast^{+}(v)=0
and
*^{-}(v)=1
and a trivial cylinder over the associated Reeb orbit if
v
is trivial,

such that

•

u_{v}
is
J
-holomorphic near the punctures of
\dot{C}_{v}
;

•

if
x\in C_{v}
is a positive/negative node of type
1
, then
u_{v}
is positively/negatively asymptotic to a Reeb orbit
\gamma\in\mathcal{P}^{\pm}
near
x\in C
;

•

\int_{C_{v}}{u_{v}}^{*}\omega\geq 0
;

•

if
C_{v}
is unstable, then
\int_{C_{v}}u^{*}_{v}\omega\geq\kappa_{L}
.

We write
\mathcal{Z}^{c}_{\bar{\partial}}
for the locus of
J
-holomorphic elements of
\mathcal{Z}^{c}
.

Remark 3.54
.

V(T^{\prime})
is only bigger than
V(T_{\varphi})
if
\widehat{X}
is the trivial cobordism from
(Y,\lambda)
to itself. In this case, the base does not capture the full stratification of
\mathcal{Z}^{c}
and the resulting thickening will be a rel–
C^{1}
manifold with boundary (in the fibers). This will be important in §
4.4
.

Lemma 3.55
.

\mathcal{Z}^{c}
carries a canonical topology, which is stratified by
\mathscr{S}^{c}
.

Proof.

We equip
\mathcal{Z}^{c}
with the following topology. We use
\mathcal{C}_{\geq\epsilon}
as defined in (
3.20
). Given
(\varphi,T^{\prime},u)\in\mathcal{Z}^{c}
, define
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}(\varphi,T^{\prime},u)
to be the subset of points
(\varphi^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime},u^{\prime})
such that

•

d_{\mathcal{B}}(\varphi,\varphi^{\prime})<\epsilon
;

•

T^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\textbf{g}}}T^{\prime\prime}
for
g
in an
\epsilon
-neighborhood of
0
in
G^{c}_{T^{\prime}/}

•

the (orbits of the) graphs satisfy

d_{\text{H}}\Bigl(\mathbb{R}\cdot\text{graph}(\varphi_{v},u_{v})|_{\mathcal{C}_{\geq\epsilon}},\mathbb{R}\cdot\text{graph}(\varphi^{\prime}_{\pi_{\textbf{g}}(v)},u^{\prime}_{\pi_{\textbf{g}}(v)})|_{\mathcal{C}_{\geq\epsilon}}\Bigr)<\epsilon

in the Gromov-Hausdorff metric for every vertex
v
such that
\pi_{\textbf{g}}(v)
is a symplectization vertex, and we choose any representatives of the classes
[u_{v}]
and
[u^{\prime}_{\pi_{\textbf{g}}(v)}]
;

•

the graphs satisfy

d_{\text{H}}\Bigl(\text{graph}(\varphi_{v},\pi_{g}\circ u_{v})|_{\mathcal{C}_{\geq\epsilon}},\text{graph}(\varphi^{\prime}_{\pi_{\textbf{g}}(v)},u^{\prime}_{\pi_{\textbf{g}}(v)})|_{\mathcal{C}_{\geq\epsilon}}\Bigr)<\epsilon

for vertices
v
such that
\ast(\pi_{g}(v))=01
,

•

for
e\in E(T^{\prime\prime})
with associated Reeb orbit
\gamma_{e}
and corresponding node
x_{e}\in\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}
we have

d_{Y}(u^{\prime}_{Y}(z),\gamma_{e})<\epsilon

for any
z\in\mathcal{C}_{\varphi^{\prime}}
with
d_{\mathcal{C}}(z,x_{e})\leq\epsilon
.

Similar to
G_{\text{II}}
in
[
Par19
, §2.5]
, the gluing parameter space

(3.57)

G^{c}_{T/}:=\left\{\bigl(\{g_{e}\}_{e},\{g_{v}\}_{v}\bigr)\in(0,\infty]^{E^{\mathrm{int}}(T)}\times(0,\infty]^{E_{00}(T)}\;\middle|\;g_{v}=g_{e}+g_{v^{\prime}}\;\;\text{for }e=(v,v^{\prime})\text{ with }v\in E_{00}(T)\right\}

models neighborhoods of cobordism buildings. In the equation above, we interpret
g_{v^{\prime}}=0
if
v^{\prime}\not\in E_{00}(T)
. There is a natural stratification of
G^{c}_{T/}\to(\mathscr{S}^{c})_{T/}
obtained by sending
(\{g_{e}\},\{g_{v}\})
to the map
\pi:T\to T^{\prime}

such that

•

the edge
e
is contracted if
g_{e}<\infty

•

\ast(v)=00
is changed to
\ast(v)=01
if
g_{v}<\infty
.

Conceptually, we view
G^{c}
as the parameter space of gluing the
target
of the cobordism buildings. Any element
\textbf{g}\in G^{c}_{T/}
naturally induces a collection of maps

\{\pi_{*}:\widehat{X}_{v}\to\widehat{X}_{\pi(v)}\}_{v\in V(T)}

where

•

maps of the type
\widehat{Y}^{\pm}\to\widehat{Y}^{\pm}
are allowed to be
any
\mathbb{R}
-translation

•

maps of the type
\widehat{X}\to\widehat{X}
must be the identity

•

maps of the type
\widehat{Y}^{\pm}\to\widehat{X}
are the pre-composition of the relevant boundary collar identifying the ends of
\widehat{X}
with
\widehat{Y}^{\pm}
with any
\mathbb{R}-
translation of
\widehat{Y}^{\pm}
.

We denote by
0
the unique element in
G^{c}
corresponding to performing no gluing. An
\epsilon
-neighborhood of
0
is defined via the natural identification
[0,1)\cong(1,\infty]
via
t\mapsto 1/t
.
It follows from the construction of
\mathcal{Z}^{c}
that this topology is equipped with a natural stratification by
\mathscr{S}^{c}
.
∎

The following properties are shown by the same arguments as Lemma
3.21
and Lemma
3.38
.

Lemma 3.56
.

The
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-action on
\mathcal{Z}^{c}
is Palais proper.∎

Lemma 3.57
.

The induced map

\psi\colon{\raisebox{2.04439pt}{$\mathcal{Z}^{c}_{\bar{\partial}}$}\left/\raisebox{-2.04439pt}{$\text{PGL}_{d+1}(\mathbb{C})$}\right.}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\widehat{X},\,J}(T)

is an isomorphism.∎

3.5.4.
Construction

Recall that we have fixed a corolla
T
with degree
\beta
and positive/negative edges labeled by
\Gamma^{+}
and
\Gamma^{-}
respectively. We will write
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\widehat{X},J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)=\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\widehat{X},J}(T)
from now on to make the step to leveled buildings in Corollary
3.62
notationally easier.

Definition 3.58
.

A
pre-perturbation datum

\mathfrak{D}=(\widetilde{\lambda},\nabla,p^{\pm})
for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\widehat{X},J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)

consists of

•

a
\mathcal{P}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-})
-integral approximation
\widetilde{\lambda}
of
\lambda
as in Definition
3.18
;

•

a
J
-linear connection
\nabla
on
T\widehat{X}
so that
(\Theta^{\pm})^{*}\nabla
is translation invariant;

•

primes
p^{\pm}
satisfying (
3.54
).

Given this, we set

(3.58)

d^{\prime}\coloneqq p^{+}\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(\gamma)-p^{-}\sum\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{-}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(\gamma)

and
d\coloneqq d^{\prime}+|\Gamma^{+}|+|\Gamma^{-}|-2
. Let
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{c}
be the smooth manifold with corners defined in §
3.5.1
and set
G:=\text{PU}(d+1)
and
\mathcal{G}:=\text{PGL}_{d+1}(\mathbb{C})
.
We let
\mathcal{Z}^{c}=\mathcal{Z}^{c}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(T)\to\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{c}
be the family of buildings of Definition
3.53
with
T
being the corolla with positive/negative edges labeled by
\Gamma^{\pm}
.

Definition 3.59
.

A
perturbation datum
extending
\mathfrak{D}
is a tuple
\alpha=(\mathfrak{D},\mathcal{U},\lambda,E,\mu,\mu^{\pm})
, consisting of

•

a good covering
\mathcal{U}:=\{(U_{i},\sigma_{ij},D_{ij},\chi_{i})\}_{i\in I}
of
\mathcal{Z}^{c}
and a
\mathcal{G}
-equivariant map

\zeta\colon\mathcal{B}^{\text{st}}_{d^{\prime}}(d)/S_{d^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{G}/G

yielding a
\mathcal{G}
-equivariant map
\zeta_{\mathcal{U}}\colon\mathcal{Z}\to\mathfrak{s}\mathfrak{u}(d+1)
(by Lemma
3.26
);

•

a
joint perturbation space

(E,\mu,\mu^{\pm})
consisting of a finite-dimensional
G
-representation
E
equipped with
G
-equivariant linear maps

\mu\colon E\to C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{o}}\times\widehat{X},\Lambda^{*,0,1}_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{o}}/\mathcal{B}^{P}_{d}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}T\widehat{X})

and

\mu^{\pm}\colon E\to C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{o}}\times\widehat{Y}^{\pm},\Lambda^{*,0,1}_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{o}}/\mathcal{B}^{P}_{d}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}T\widehat{Y}^{\pm})^{\mathbb{R}}

so that
(\Theta^{\pm})^{*}\mu(e)
agrees with
\mu^{\pm}(e)
restricted to the respective end in
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{o}}\times\widehat{Y}^{\pm}
. We require that the map

(3.59)

E\to\text{coker}(D_{u}):e\mapsto[\mu(e)|_{\text{graph}(\varphi,u)}]

is surjective for any
(\varphi,u)\in\mathcal{Z}^{c}_{\bar{\partial}}
with
\zeta_{\mathcal{U}}(\varphi,u)=0
.

Construction 3.60
.

Given a perturbation data
\alpha
, we define

\mathcal{K}^{c}_{\alpha}:=(\mathbb{T}^{\Gamma^{+}\sqcup\Gamma^{-}}\times G,\mathcal{T}^{c}/\mathcal{B}^{P},\mathcal{E},\mathfrak{s})

by letting
\mathcal{T}\subset\mathcal{Z}\times E
be the space of tuples
(\varphi,\widetilde{T},u,w)
such that

a)

for each nontrivial vertex
v\in V(\widetilde{T})
the associated map
u_{v}
(respectively a representative thereof) satisfies

(3.60)

\bar{\partial}_{\widehat{J}}\,u_{v}+\mu_{k}^{\ast(v)}(w)|_{\text{graph}(\varphi_{v},u_{v})}=0,

on
\dot{C}_{v}

b)

the linearized operator of (
3.60
) is surjective (without variation of the framing
\varphi
).

The obstruction bundle
\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{T}
is the trivial bundle

\mathcal{E}=E\oplus\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}(d+1),

while the obstruction section
\mathfrak{s}
is given by a mollification of
\widehat{\mathfrak{s}}(\varphi,u,w)=(w,\lambda_{\mathcal{U}}(\varphi,u))
as in Lemma
3.39
.

Theorem 3.61
.

Given an exact symplectic cobordism
(\widehat{X},d\lambda)
from
(Y^{+},\lambda^{+})
to
(Y^{-},\lambda^{-})
, a compatible almost complex structure
J
on
\widehat{X}
, and a tree
T
in
\mathscr{S}^{c}
, the following holds

(1)

The moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\widehat{X}\,J}(T)
of buildings in
\widehat{X}
admits perturbation data. Construction
3.60
associates to each perturbation datum
\alpha
a rel–
C^{1}
global Kuranishi chart
\mathcal{K}^{c}_{\alpha}
with corners for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\widehat{X}\,J}(T)
.

(2)

If the Reeb orbits
\Gamma^{+}
and
\Gamma^{-}
labeling the exterior edges of
T
consist of good Reeb orbits, then there exists a canonical isomorphism

\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{K}^{c}_{\alpha}}\cong\bigotimes\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}\mathfrak{o}_{\gamma}\otimes\bigotimes\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{-}}\mathfrak{o}^{\vee}_{\gamma}

of orientation lines.

As in the case of buildings in symplectizations, this yields a chart for leveled buildings.

Corollary 3.62
.

The pullback Kuranishi chart

(3.61)

\mathcal{K}^{c,\mathbb{R}}\coloneqq\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{c}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{P}_{c}}\mathcal{K}^{c}_{\alpha}

is a global Kuranishi chart for the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\widehat{X},\,J}_{\text{SFT}}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to the proof of Theorem
3.40
.
∎

The proof of the first assertion is analogous to the proof of Theorem
3.29
(
1
) and (
2
). We simply have to replace Lemma
3.33
with the following definition and existence result. Then the arguments carry over verbatim. The proof of Claim (
2
) follows from the arguments of §
3.6
.

Definition 3.63
.

Suppose
V\to B
and
E^{\pm}\to B^{\pm}
are three smooth
G
-vector bundles and that
B^{\pm}
admits a free
\mathbb{R}
-action, which commutes with the
G
-action and lifts to
E^{\pm}
. Suppose there exist open
G
-invariant subsets
B^{\pm}_{\circ}\subset B^{\pm}
whose orbit under the
\mathbb{R}
-action covers all of
B^{\pm}
and which admit smooth
G
-equivariant open embeddings
j^{\pm}\colon B^{\pm}_{\circ}\hookrightarrow B
with disjoint image lifting to embeddings of vector bundles. Assume additionally that the quotients
B^{\pm}/\mathbb{R}
and
B\setminus\text{im}(j^{+})\sqcup\text{im}(j^{-})
are compact. Then, a
joint finite-dimensional approximation scheme
of
(V,E^{\pm})
is a sequence
(E_{\ell},\mu_{\ell},\mu^{\pm}_{\ell})
of finite-dimensional
G
-representations together with
G
-equivariant linear maps

\mu_{\ell}\colon E_{\ell}\hookrightarrow C^{\infty}(B,V)

and

\mu^{\pm}_{\ell}\colon E_{\ell}\hookrightarrow C^{\infty}_{c}(B,V)^{\mathbb{R}}:=\{\eta\in C^{\infty}(B^{\pm},E^{\pm})^{\mathbb{R}}\mid\text{supp}(\eta)/\mathbb{R}\text{ is compact}\}

satisfying

i)

E_{\ell}
is a subrepresentation of
E_{\ell+1}
with
\mu_{\ell+1}|_{E_{\ell}}=\mu_{\ell}
and
\mu^{\pm}_{\ell+1}|_{E_{\ell}}=\mu^{\pm}_{\ell}
,

ii)

\bigcup\limits{\text{im}(\mu^{\pm}_{\ell})}
is dense in
C^{\infty}_{c}(B^{\pm},E^{\pm})^{\mathbb{R}}
in the
C^{\infty}_{\text{loc}}
-topology,

iii)

\text{supp}(\mu_{\ell}(v)|_{\text{im}(j_{*}^{\pm})}-j_{*}^{\pm}\mu_{\ell}^{\pm}(v)|_{B_{\circ}^{\pm}})
is precompact in
B
for any
\ell\geq 1
and
v\in E_{\ell}
,

iv)

\bigcup\limits{\text{im}(\mu_{\ell}-{j^{+}}_{*}\mu^{+}_{\ell}-{j^{-}}_{*}\mu^{-}_{\ell})}
is dense in
C^{\infty}_{c}(B,V)
in the
C^{\infty}_{\text{loc}}
-topology.

Note that the last property makes sense due to the third one.

Lemma 3.64
.

Given finite approximation schemes
\mu^{\pm}_{*}
, there exists a choice of
\mu_{*}
such that
(\mu^{+},\mu,\mu^{-})
forms a joint finite-dimensional approximation scheme.

Proof.

We adapt the proof of
[
AMS24
, Lemma 4.2]
. Fix
G\times\mathbb{R}
-invariant connections
\nabla^{\pm}
on
E^{\pm}
and let
\nabla
be a
G
-invariant connection on
V
so that
{j^{\pm}}^{*}\nabla
agrees with
\nabla^{\pm}|_{B^{\pm}_{\circ}}
away from a subset
K\subset B^{\pm}_{\circ}
, which is precompact in
B^{\pm}
.
Let
A\coloneqq B\,\setminus\;(\text{im}(j^{+})\cup\text{im}(j^{-}))
and let
(A_{n})_{n}
be a countable exhaustion of
A
so that each
A_{n}
is a smooth
G
-invariant manifold with boundary. Fix an increasing sequence
(\rho_{n,k})_{k}
of
G
-invariant smooth bump functions with support in
A_{n}
and
A_{n}=\bigcup\limits{\rho_{n,k}^{-1}(1)}
for each
n
.

Similarly, let
(B^{\pm}_{n})_{n}
be a countable exhaustion of
B^{\pm}_{\circ}
by
G
-invariant smooth manifolds with boundary, and let
\rho^{\pm}_{n}
be a
G
-invariant smooth bump function that is identically
1
on
B^{\pm}_{n}
and supported in
B^{\pm}_{\circ}
.
Then, let
\widecheck{\nabla}^{\pm}
be the induced connection on
E^{\pm}/\mathbb{R}\to B^{\pm}/\mathbb{R}
and let
(\lambda_{\ell}^{\pm})_{\ell}
be the increasing sequence of non-negative eigenvalues of the Laplacian associated to
\widecheck{\nabla}^{\pm}
.

Let
\widecheck{W}_{\ell}^{\pm}
be the preimage of the space of eigenfunctions associated to the non-negative eigenvalues
\lambda_{j}^{\pm}
with
j\leq\ell
and let
W^{\pm}_{\ell}\subset C^{\infty}_{c}(B^{\pm},V^{\ell})^{\mathbb{R}}
be the preimage of
\widecheck{W}_{\ell}^{\pm}
. Define

E^{\pm}_{\ell}:=\bigoplus\limits_{n\leq\ell}W^{\pm}_{n}

and let
\mu^{\pm}_{\ell}\colon E^{\pm}_{\ell}\to C^{\infty}_{c}(B^{\pm},E^{\pm})^{\mathbb{R}}
be the inclusion on each summand. Then, define

\mu_{\ell}\colon E^{\pm}_{\ell}\to C^{\infty}(B,V)

by

\mu_{\ell}((v_{n})_{n})=\sum\limits_{n\leq\ell}{\rho_{n}^{\pm}\,j^{\pm}_{*}\mu^{\pm}_{n}(v_{n})}.

Finally, doubling
A_{n+1}
and
V|_{A_{n+1}}
and considering the eigenspaces of the Laplacian of the induced connection on the doubled vector bundle, we obtain for each
n
a sequence of vector spaces
(E_{n,k}^{\mathrm{o}})_{k}
together with maps

\mu_{n,k}\colon E_{n,k}^{\mathrm{o}}\to C^{\infty}_{c}(B,V):v\mapsto\rho_{n,k}\,v.

Define for
\ell\geq 1
the vector space

E^{\mathrm{o}}_{\ell}:=\bigoplus\limits_{n,k\leq\ell}E_{n,k}^{\mathrm{o}}

and let
\mu_{\ell}\colon E^{\mathrm{o}}_{\ell}\hookrightarrow C^{\infty}_{c}(B,V)
be the canonical map induced by the maps
\mu^{\mathrm{o}}_{n,k}
. We finally define

E_{\ell}\coloneqq V^{+}_{\ell}\oplus E^{\mathrm{o}}_{\ell}\oplus V^{-}_{\ell}

and let
\mu_{\ell}
be given by the sum of the maps
\mu_{\ell}
defined above. Extend
\mu^{\pm}_{\ell}
to
E_{\ell}
by letting it be
0
on
E^{\mathrm{o}}_{\ell}\oplus V^{\mp}_{\ell}
.
∎

3.5.5.
Disconnected buildings in symplectic cobordisms

Given a symplectic cobordism
(\widehat{X},\omega)
as above and sequences
\Gamma^{\pm}
of Reeb orbits of
\lambda^{\pm}
as well as a partition
\Lambda\colon\Gamma^{-}\to\Gamma^{+}
and a sequence
\beta(\beta_{\gamma})_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}
of relative homology classes, we define the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\widehat{X},J}_{\text{SFT}}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)
of disconnected leveled buildings in
\widehat{X}
exactly as in Definition
3.47
except that (one level of) the buildings now maps to the symplectization.

Given
\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}
let
\mathcal{K}_{\gamma}^{c}
be the global Kuranishi chart for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\widehat{X},J}(\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma};\beta_{\gamma})
with base space
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{c,\gamma}
given by Theorem
3.61
. Recall that
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{c,\gamma}
was defined in §
3.5.1
as the corner blow-up of
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{*}
corresponding to refinement using maximally leveled pre-cobordism trees. Similarly, let
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{c,\Lambda}
be the corner blow-up of
\prod\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\mathcal{B}^{P}_{c,\gamma}}
corresponding to refinements as in §
3.1.3
but using maximally leveled pre-cobordism forests.

Proposition 3.65
.

The pullback global Kuranishi chart

\mathcal{K}^{\mathbb{R}}_{c,\Lambda}\,\coloneqq\,\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{c,\Lambda}\times_{\prod\limits_{\gamma}{\mathcal{B}^{P}_{c,\gamma}}}\prod\limits_{\gamma}{\mathcal{K}^{c}_{\gamma}}

is a global Kuranishi chart for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\widehat{X},J}_{\text{SFT}}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)_{\Lambda}
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to the proof of Theorem
3.40
.
∎

3.6.
Orientations

The
determinant line
of a global Kuranishi chart
\mathcal{K}=(G,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{E},\mathfrak{s})
, is the real line bundle

(3.62)

\det(\mathcal{K}):=\det(T\mathcal{T})\otimes\det(\mathfrak{g})^{\vee}\otimes\det(\mathcal{E})^{\vee}

on
\mathcal{T}
restricted to the zero locus
\mathfrak{s}^{-1}(0)
.
Its
orientation line
is the sheaf of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-torsors

(3.63)

\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathcal{K}):=(\det(\mathcal{K})\setminus 0)/\mathbb{R}_{>0}.

in degree
\text{vdim}(\mathcal{K})
. Given
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we define
\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(n)\coloneqq\mathbb{Z}_{2}[-n]
.

Definition 3.66
.

An
orientation
of
\mathcal{K}
is an isomorphism
\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\text{vdim}\mathcal{K})\cong\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathcal{K})
.

We will throughout use the isomorphism

(3.64)

\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}\otimes\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}^{\vee}\cong\mathfrak{o}(0):v\otimes f\mapsto f(v)

to trivialize the multiplication of an orientation line with its dual. Given a finite-dimensional vector space
V
, we let
\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(V)
be the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-torsor in degree
\dim V
associated to
H_{\dim V}(V,V\setminus\{0\};\mathbb{Z})
. Given a Cauchy–Riemann operator
D
we define its orientation line to be

\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(D)\coloneqq\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\ker D)\otimes\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\text{coker}D)^{\vee}.

Remark 3.67
.

Given a finite-dimensional vector space
V
, the zero map
D_{0}=0\colon V\to V
has orientation line
\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(D_{0})\cong\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(V)\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(V)^{\vee}
. On the other hand, it is homotopic to the identity
D_{1}=\text{id}
with orientation line
\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(D_{1})=\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(0)
. Our choice of trivialization in (
3.64
) ensures that the canonical isomorphism
\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(D_{0})\cong\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(D_{1})
is orientation-preserving.

Lemma 3.68
.

Let
\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}
be the global Kuranishi chart of Theorem
3.29
. Then there exists a canonical isomorphism

(3.65)

\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathcal{K})\cong\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\bar{\partial}_{J})\otimes\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathbb{R})^{\vee}\otimes\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(2|\Gamma^{+}|-2|\Gamma^{-}|-6).

Proof.

Observe first that
\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}
admits a well-defined vector lift of its tangent micro-bundle, given by

T\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}=T_{\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}/\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\alpha}}\oplus\pi^{*}T\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\alpha},

where
\pi\colon\mathcal{T}_{\alpha}\to\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\alpha}
is the forgetful map. We will call it from now on simply the tangent bundle and will omit the subscript
\alpha
. Recall that
\mathcal{B}^{P}
is a torus bundle over a blow-up of the complex manifold
\mathcal{B}\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,|\Gamma^{+}|+|\Gamma^{-}|}(\mathbb{P}^{d},d)
. Thus,

\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathcal{B}^{P})\cong\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathcal{B})\otimes\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(S^{1})^{\otimes\Gamma^{+}\sqcup\Gamma^{-}}\cong\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{g}\mathfrak{l})\otimes\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,|\Gamma^{+}|\sqcup\Gamma^{-}})\otimes\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(S^{1})^{\otimes\Gamma^{+}\sqcup\Gamma^{-}}

canonically. Meanwhile, for
(\varphi,u,w)\in\mathcal{T}
we have

(T_{\mathcal{T}/\mathcal{B}^{P}})_{(\varphi,u,w)}=\ker\Bigl(D_{u}^{\nabla}+\mu_{k}(-)|_{\text{graph}(\varphi,u)}\colon C^{\infty}(\dot{C},u^{*}T\widehat{Y})\oplus E_{k}\to\Omega^{0,1}(\dot{C},u^{*}T\widehat{Y})\Bigr)

which agrees with the index of the Cauchy–Riemann operator
D_{u}^{\nabla}+\mu_{k}(-)|_{\text{graph}(\varphi,u)}
. By
[
Bao23
, Lemma 3.2]
, there exists a canonical isomorphism

(3.66)

\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(D_{u}^{\nabla}+\mu_{k}(-)|_{\text{graph}(\varphi,u)})\cong\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(D_{u}^{\nabla})\otimes\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(E_{k}).

Combining these two isomorphisms with the polarization isomorphism
\mathcal{G}\cong G\times\mathfrak{g}
, we obtain the canonical isomorphisms (over the locus of curves with smooth domains)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathcal{K}_{\alpha})\,

\displaystyle\cong\,\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(D^{\nabla})\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(E_{k})\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathbb{R})^{\vee}\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathcal{B}^{P})\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}})^{\vee}\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathfrak{g})^{\vee}\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(E_{k})^{\vee}

\displaystyle\cong\,\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(D^{\nabla})\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(E_{k})\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathbb{R})^{\vee}\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{g}\mathfrak{l})\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,|\Gamma^{+}|+|\Gamma^{-}|})\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(S^{1})^{\otimes\Gamma^{+}\sqcup\Gamma^{-}}(\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(S^{1})^{\otimes\Gamma^{+}\sqcup\Gamma^{-}})^{\vee}\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathfrak{g})^{\vee}\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathfrak{g})^{\vee}\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(E_{k})^{\vee}

\displaystyle\cong\,\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(D^{\nabla})\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathbb{R})^{\vee}\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,|\Gamma^{+}|+|\Gamma^{-}|})\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{g}\mathfrak{l})\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{g}\mathfrak{l})^{\vee}\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(E_{k})^{\vee}\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(E_{k})

\displaystyle\cong\,\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(D^{\nabla})\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\mathbb{R})^{\vee}\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,|\Gamma^{+}|+|\Gamma^{-}|}),

where we omitted the tensor product. Note that we use the Koszul sign rule when switching two orientation lines.
∎

We associate to any based Reeb orbit
(\gamma,b_{\gamma})
a virtual vector space
V_{\gamma}=(V_{\gamma}^{+},V_{\gamma}^{-})
as follows.

Definition 3.69

(
[
Par19
, Definition 2.46]
)
.

Let
\widetilde{\gamma}
be the constant-speed parametrization determined by a base point
b\in\overline{\gamma}
. Pull back the complex bundle
\widetilde{\gamma}^{*}\xi\oplus\mathbb{C}\to S^{1}
to a bundle
\mathcal{V}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. The Lie derivative
\mathcal{L}_{R}
and the trivial connection on
\mathbb{C}
pull back to yield a connection
\nabla
on
\mathcal{V}
. Let
(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\bar{\partial})
be an extension of
(\mathcal{V},\nabla^{0,1})
to all of
\mathbb{C}
. We define

(3.67)

V_{\gamma,b}\coloneqq\text{Ind}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\bar{\partial})

to be the index bundle of this extension.

By
[
Par19
, Lemma 2.47]
, such extensions
(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\bar{\partial})
exist, and any two extensions differ by the direct sum with a complex vector space (up to isomorphism). In particular, the associated orientation line
\mathfrak{o}(V_{\gamma,b})

is independent of the choice of extension. Recall that a Reeb orbit
\gamma
is
good
if it is
not
an even multiple cover of a simple Reeb orbit
\overline{\gamma}
with
|\sigma(\mathcal{A}_{\overline{\gamma}})\cap(-1,0)|\equiv 1
mod
2
. Equivalently,
\gamma
is good if the action of
\mathbb{Z}/m_{\gamma}
on
\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(V_{\gamma,b})
is trivial. Thus, for good Reeb orbits, we have a canonical isomorphism
\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(V_{\gamma,b})\cong\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(V_{\gamma,b^{\prime}})
for any two base points
b,b^{\prime}
and we can set

(3.68)

\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}_{\gamma}\coloneqq\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(V_{\gamma,b}).

By a straightforward generalization of
[
Par19
, Lemma 2.51]
to the case with several positive punctures, the orientation line
\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\bar{\partial}_{J})=\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(D^{\nabla})
is canonically isomorphic to

\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}(\Gamma^{+};\Gamma^{-})\coloneqq\bigotimes\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}_{\gamma}\otimes\bigotimes\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{-}}\hskip 0.5pt\mathfrak{o}_{\gamma}^{\vee}

This completes the proof of Theorem
3.29
(
3
).

4.
A contact flow category and bimodules

In this section we associate a flow category to a contact manifold
(Y,\lambda)
and a flow bimodule to an exact symplectic cobordism
(X,\omega)
. The objects are finite sequences of Reeb orbits in either case, and morphisms are buildings of genus zero. Due to a technical obstruction, we can only construct the flow category and bimodule after restricting the action of the Reeb orbits. Thus, in §
4.5
, we show that the “full” contact flow category can be obtained via a colimit.

4.1.
Flow categories

Our flow categories are more general than the flow categories of
[
AB24
]
; their objects are orbifolds, and the composition is defined on a certain fiber product instead of the usual product. The precise definition is given in §
4.1.2
.

4.1.1.
Preliminaries

Recall that a Lie groupoid
X=[X_{1}\rightrightarrows X_{0}]
is a groupoid where the set of objects and morphisms carry the structure of smooth manifolds, all structure maps are smooth, and the source and target maps
s,t\colon X_{1}\to X_{0}
are submersions (whence the multiplication is a well-defined smooth map). We write
\phi\colon x\to y
for
\phi\in X_{1}
with source
x=s(\phi)
and target
y=t(\phi)
. We will also abuse notation and write
y=\phi(x)
.

We call
X

étale
if
s
(and thus
t
) is a local diffeomorphism and
proper
if
(s,t)\colon X_{1}\to X_{0}\times X_{0}
is proper. By
[
Par25
, Corollary 1.4]
, any étale proper Lie groupoid with a finite number of isotropy types is equivalent to a
transformation groupoid

[M/G]:=[G\times M\rightrightarrows M]
given by the action of a compact Lie group
G
on a smooth manifold
M
so that all points have finite isotropy. All Lie groupoids we consider in our main application are of this form. We need a weakening of the notion of smoothness. See also
[
Swa21
]
for more details.

Definition 4.1
.

A map
\pi\colon M\to B
to a smooth manifold is
of class rel–
C^{1}
if there exist local charts
\{\phi_{i}\colon U_{i}\to B\}
and
\{\varphi_{i}\colon U_{i}\times N\to M\}
so that
\pi\circ\varphi=\phi\circ\text{pr}_{U}
, the transition maps
\varphi_{ij}(u)=\varphi_{i}^{-1}\varphi_{j}(u,\cdot)
is of class
C^{1}
for
u\in U_{i}\cap U_{j}
, and
u\mapsto\varphi_{ij}(u)
is continuous in the
C^{1}_{loc}
-topology.

A
rel–
C^{1}
Lie groupoid
is a topological groupoid
X
equipped with a morphism
X\to B
of groupoids so that
X_{1}/B_{1}
and
X_{0}/B_{0}
are rel–
C^{1}
manifolds and the structure maps are of class rel–
C^{1}
. The following notions can be defined verbatim in the rel–
C^{1}
setting. We refrain from doing so here for the sake of clarity, but we will use them in that generality in §
4.2
.

Definition 4.2
.

A
slicing
of a morphism of Lie groupoids
f\colon X\to[Y/G]
is a
G
-action on
X_{0}
, whose action map factors through
G\times X_{0}\to X_{1}
and which satisfies
f_{1}(g,x)=(g,f_{0}(y))
.
We say
f
is
sliced
if it is (implicitly) equipped with a slicing and
sliceable
if it admits a slicing. We say a slicing is
free
if the
G
-action on
X_{0}
is free.

Example 4.3
.

A morphism
f\colon G\to G^{\prime}
of groups is sliceable if there exists an inclusion
G^{\prime}\hookrightarrow G
of groups, which is a section of
f
.

In our main application,
X
is the groupoid associated to an action of a compact Lie group
G_{X}
on a smooth manifold
\widetilde{X}
and similarly for
Y
, with
G_{X}=G_{Y}\times G^{\prime}_{X}
canonically.

Definition 4.4
.

Given two morphisms
f\colon X\to Z=[Z_{0}/G]
and
g\colon Y\to Z
that intersect transversely in
Z_{0}
and are equipped with free slicings
j_{f}
and
j_{g}
, we define the
quotient fiber product

X\overline{\times}_{Z}Y
to be the Lie groupoid with objects given by

(4.1)

(X\bar{\times}_{Z}Y)_{0}\coloneqq X_{0}\times_{Z_{0}}Y_{0}/G

and morphisms
(X\overline{\times}_{Z}Y)_{1}\coloneqq f_{1}^{-1}(\text{id})\times g_{1}^{-1}(\text{id})
.

Example 4.5
.

Suppose

f\colon X=[M/G_{Z}]\to[Z_{0}/G_{Z}]\qquad\text{and}\qquad g\colon Y=[N/G_{Z}]\to[Z_{0}/G_{Z}]

are submersions on the level of objects, and
G_{Z}
acts freely on
M\times Z
. Then,
X\overline{\times}_{Z}Y
is the manifold
(M\times_{Z_{0}}N)/G_{Z}
, where
G_{Z}
acts diagonally via the slicings on
M
and
N
. If, moreover, the action of
G_{Z}
on
Z_{0}
is transitive, then we have a canonical isomorphism
\mathfrak{g}_{Z}\cong TZ_{0}
and therefore, a canonical identification

(4.2)

T(X\,\overline{\times}_{Z}\,Y)\,\cong\,TX\oplus TY

of
G_{Z}
-vector bundles on
M\times_{Z_{0}}N
. The same is true if
M
and
N
are equipped with almost free actions by
G\times G_{Z}
and
G^{\prime}\times G_{Z}
, respectively.

This quotient fiber product commutes with filtered colimits in the following sense.

Lemma 4.6
.

Suppose
\{X_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in A}
and
\{Y_{\beta}\}_{\beta\in B}
are filtered diagrams in
\text{dOrb}_{/\cdot}
with
S_{X_{\alpha}}=S_{X_{\alpha^{\prime}}}
and
T_{X_{\alpha}}=S_{Y_{\beta}}
for all
\alpha,\beta
and
T_{Y_{\alpha}}=T_{Y_{\beta}}
. If the colimits
X
and
Y
exist, then the colimit of
\{(X_{\alpha}\overline{\times}Y_{\beta})\}_{(\alpha,\beta)\in A\times B}
exists and is given by
X\overline{\times}Y
.

Proof.

Write
S=S_{X_{\alpha}}
,
Z=T_{X_{\alpha}}=[Z_{1}\rightrightarrows Z_{0}]
and
T=T_{Y_{\beta}}
for some
\alpha
and
\beta
. Then, we have that
X_{\alpha}\overline{\times}Y_{\beta}
is a quotient of the fiber product
X_{\alpha}\times_{Z_{0}}Y_{\beta}
. Since finite limits commute with filtered colimits, we have that

\colim\limits_{(\alpha,\beta)}X_{\alpha}\times_{Z_{0}}Y_{\beta}=X\times_{Z_{0}}Y.

Since the morphisms in
\text{dOrb}_{/\cdot}
are compatible with the slicings of
t_{X_{\alpha}}
and
s_{Y_{\beta}}
, the claim follows now directly from the definition.
∎

4.1.2.
A more general notion of flow category

Given the setup in the previous subsection, the definition of a flow category looks almost as before, except that we replaced the Cartesian product by the quotient fiber product and that we have a symmetric action on the objects. Note that in the case where each object is simply a point and the symmetric action is trivial, this recovers the original definition of a flow category as in
[
AB24
]
or
[
CJS95
]
.

Recall from
[
AB24
]
that a
strong equivalence

f\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{Y}
of derived orbifolds is a morphism of derived orbifolds (that is, spaces equipped with global Kuranishi charts) together with a morphism
\pi\colon Y\to X
of the underlying thickenings so that
X\to Y
corresponds to the inclusion of the zero section.

Definition 4.7
.

We define the category
\text{dOrb}_{/\cdot}
to have as objects derived orbifolds
\mathbb{X}
with corners equipped with two maps
s_{\mathbb{X}}\colon\mathbb{X}\to S_{\mathbb{X}}=[\widetilde{S}_{\mathbb{X}}/G^{S}_{\mathbb{X}}]
and
t_{\mathbb{X}}\colon\mathbb{X}\to T_{\mathbb{X}}=[\widetilde{T}_{\mathbb{X}}/G^{T}_{\mathbb{X}}]
to compact transitive orbifolds so that
s_{\mathbb{X}}
and
t_{\mathbb{X}}
are freely sliced submersions. The morphisms are given by strong equivalences
f\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{Y}
so that
s_{\mathbb{Y}}\circ f=s_{\mathbb{X}}
and
t_{\mathbb{Y}}\circ f=t_{\mathbb{X}}
.

Given
\mathbb{X},\mathbb{Y}
in
\text{dOrb}_{/\cdot}
with
T_{\mathbb{X}}=S_{\mathbb{Y}}
, we define

(4.3)

\mathbb{X}\,\overline{\times}\,\mathbb{Y}\,\coloneqq\,(\mathbb{X}\,\overline{\times}_{S_{\mathbb{Y}}}\,\mathbb{Y},S_{\mathbb{X}},T_{\mathbb{Y}})

with the canonical structural maps. This yields one part of our generalization. In order to encode the symmetric action concisely, we introduce the following definition.

Definition 4.8
.

Let
\Delta^{*}
be the groupoid whose objects are pairs
(n,\prec)
, where
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
is an integer and
\prec
is a total ordering of
\{1,\dots,n\}
, and whose morphisms are order-preserving isomorphisms
(n,\prec)\to(n,\prec^{\prime})
. A
symmetric set
in
\mathcal{C}
with
orbit set

I
is a functor
P\colon I\times\Delta^{*}\to\mathcal{C}
, where we consider the set
I
as a discrete category. We set
|P(i,\{(n,\prec)\})|:=n
and will identify
P
with its image in
\text{Ob}(\mathcal{C})
.

In the future we will repeatedly use the observation that a disjoint union of symmetric sets is canonically a symmetric set.

Definition 4.9
.

A
symmetric flow category

\mathscr{M}
consists of a symmetric set
\mathcal{P}
of closed orbifolds, the objects of
\mathscr{M}
, and for any
\alpha,\beta\in\mathcal{P}
a derived orbifold
\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\beta)
of morphisms equipped with

•

a proper function
E\colon\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\beta)\to[0,\infty)
,

•

free sliced submersions
s_{\alpha\beta}\colon\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\beta)\to\alpha
and
t_{\alpha\beta}\colon\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\beta)\to\beta

•

isomorphisms

(4.4)

\mathscr{M}(\sigma\cdot\alpha,\beta)\cong\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\beta)\cong\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\sigma^{\prime}\cdot\beta)

for any
\sigma\in S_{|\alpha|}
and
\sigma^{\prime}\in S_{|\beta|}.

The composition functions, defined on the quotient fiber product

(4.5)

\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\beta)\,\overline{\times}_{\,\beta}\;\mathscr{M}(\beta,\gamma)\;\to\;\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\gamma),

are smooth embeddings onto a codimension-
1
boundary stratum
\partial_{\beta}\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\gamma)
of
\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\gamma)
. They are

•

additive with respect to
E
.

•

compatible with the symmetric action in the sense that
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\beta)\,\overline{\times}_{\,\beta}\;\mathscr{M}(\beta,\gamma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\sigma\cdot\beta)\,\overline{\times}_{\,\sigma\cdot\beta}\;\mathscr{M}(\sigma\cdot\beta,\gamma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

commutes for any
\beta
and
\sigma\in S_{|\beta|}
, whence

\partial_{\beta}\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\gamma)=\partial_{\sigma\cdot\beta}\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\gamma).

Thus, we can write
\partial_{[\beta]}\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\gamma)
, using the orbit of
\beta
under the symmetric action to indicate the boundary stratum. We require that these strata cover the boundary of
\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\gamma)
and that

(4.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\beta)\,\overline{\times}_{\,\beta}\,\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\beta^{\prime})\,\overline{\times}_{\,\beta^{\prime}}\,\mathscr{M}(\beta^{\prime},\gamma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\beta)\,\overline{\times}_{\,\beta}\,\mathscr{M}(\beta,\gamma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\beta^{\prime})\,\overline{\times}_{\,\beta^{\prime}}\,\mathscr{M}(\beta^{\prime},\gamma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\gamma)}

is a pullback square for any
\alpha,\beta,\beta^{\prime},\gamma\in\mathcal{P}
.

Definition 4.10
.

We say a symmetric flow category
\mathscr{M}
is
of class rel–
C^{1}
if the morphism spaces are derived orbifolds of class rel-
C^{1}
, the maps
s_{\alpha\beta}
and
t_{\alpha\beta}
are of class rel–
C^{1}
as described in §
A
as well as the symmetric actions and composition maps are of class rel–
C^{1}
.

Remark 4.11
.

Since this generalization only makes the notation heavier, we will phrase all remaining proofs in terms of smooth flow categories. However, the definitions and proofs carry over verbatim to the rel–
C^{1}
setting.

Example 4.12
.

In our main example, each object of
\mathscr{M}
is of the form
B\Gamma=[E\Gamma/\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma}]
, where
\Gamma=(\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{k})
is a sequence of Reeb orbits and
\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma}=(S^{1})^{\Gamma}
. The symmetric action permutes the ordering of the sequence, and the morphism spaces are manifolds
\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})
equipped with an action by the Lie group
\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{-}}\times\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{+}}\times G_{\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+}}
. The action of
\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{-}}\times\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{+}}
comes from rotating the asymptotic markers. Then, the quotient fiber product over
\Gamma
is the quotient of

\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma)\times_{E\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}(\Gamma,\Gamma^{+})

by the (free) diagonal
\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma}
action; it carries the induced action of
\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{-}}\times G_{\Gamma^{-},\Gamma}\times\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{+}}\times G_{\Gamma,\Gamma^{+}}
.

We briefly discuss stable complex structures on the flow categories of Definition
4.9
; see also
[
AB24
, Definition 3.8]
. The definition of framed structures as in
[
AB24
]
can be adapted similarly.

Definition 4.13
.

A
stably complex lift

\mathscr{M}^{U}
of a symmetric flow category
\mathscr{M}
consists of

(1)

a symmetric set of virtual orbi-bundles
V_{\alpha}\to\alpha
, for object
\alpha
of
\mathscr{M}
, lifting the symmetric set
\mathcal{P}
of objects,

(2)

a complex virtual vector bundle
I_{\alpha\beta}
on
\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\beta)
,

(3)

a vector bundle
W_{\alpha\beta}
on
\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\beta)
,

(4)

a virtual vector space
U_{\alpha\beta}
of the form
U_{\alpha\beta}=(0,\mathbb{R}^{\{\beta\}})

(5)

an equivalence

(4.7)

T\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\beta)\oplus V_{\beta}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{\{\beta\}}\,\simeq\,(W_{\alpha\beta},W_{\alpha\beta})\oplus I_{\alpha\beta}\oplus V_{\alpha}

of virtual vector bundles;

(6)

compatible equivalences

(4.8)

I_{(\sigma\cdot\alpha)\beta}\cong I_{\alpha\beta}\cong I_{\alpha(\sigma^{\prime}\cdot\beta)}

(4.9)

W_{(\sigma\cdot\alpha)\beta}\cong W_{\alpha\beta}\cong W_{\alpha(\sigma^{\prime}\cdot\beta)}

of complex virtual vector bundles, respectively vector bundles, that lift (
4.4
) and intertwine the equivalences (
4.7
).

Moreover, for any objects
\alpha,\beta,\gamma
of
\mathscr{M}
we have split embeddings and isomorphisms

(4.10)

\displaystyle I_{\alpha\beta}\oplus I_{\beta\gamma}\,\to\,I_{\alpha\gamma},

(4.11)

\displaystyle W_{\alpha\beta}\oplus W_{\beta\gamma}\,\to\,W_{\alpha\gamma},

(4.12)

\displaystyle U_{\alpha\beta}\oplus U_{\beta\gamma}\,\cong\,(0,\mathbb{R}^{\{\beta\}})\oplus U_{\alpha\gamma},

respectively, that cover the composition map (
4.5
) and are compatible with the equivalences (
4.8
) and (
4.9
), so that the square

(4.13)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\beta)\oplus V_{\beta}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{\{\beta\}}\oplus T\mathscr{M}(\beta,\gamma)\oplus V_{\gamma}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{\{\gamma\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\gamma)\oplus V_{\gamma}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{\{\gamma\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(W_{\alpha\beta},W_{\alpha\beta})\oplus I_{\alpha\beta}\oplus V_{\alpha}\oplus(W_{\beta\gamma},W_{\beta\gamma})\oplus I_{\beta\gamma}\oplus V_{\beta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(W_{\alpha\gamma},W_{\alpha\gamma})\oplus I_{\alpha\gamma}\oplus V_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

commutes, where we implicitly use the isomorphism (
4.2
)

Since we will need the main result of
[
AB24
]
that symmetric flow categories are the
0
-simplices of a stable
\infty
-category, we introduce the relevant adaptions of their definitions here. Given a finite set
\mathcal{P}=(\mathcal{P}_{0},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{n})
of sets, we define the category
\mathcal{P}^{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}(p,q)
for
p\in\mathcal{P}_{i}
and
q\in\mathcal{P}_{j}
with
i\leq j
to have

•

objects linear trees
T
with two exterior edges so that

–

each inter edge labeled by an element of
\mathcal{P}_{k}
for
i\leq k\leq j
,

–

the incoming edge is labeled by
p
and the outgoing one by
q
,

–

each vertex is labeled by
m\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and a subset of
\{k+1,\dots,\ell-1\}
, where
k
and
\ell
are the labels of the edges adjacent to
v
;

•

morphism from
T
to
T^{\prime}
given by collapsing a (possibly empty) sequence of consecutive edges so that the labels of the collapsing of
T
agree with those of
T^{\prime}
as described in
[
AB24
, Definition 4.2]
.

This yields a strict
2
-category
\mathcal{P}^{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}
, where the horizontal composition is given by gluing two trees along the ‘common’ exterior edge. If
\mathcal{P}
is a symmetric set, we can define the
2
-category
\mathcal{P}^{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}
analogously. When
\mathcal{P}
is a symmetric set, then there are canonical isomorphisms
\mathcal{P}^{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}(\sigma\cdot p,q)\cong\mathcal{P}^{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}(p,q)\cong\mathcal{P}^{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}(p,\sigma^{\prime}\cdot q)
induced by the symmetric actions on the labels.

Definition 4.14
.

A
(non-unital graded) symmetric category

\mathcal{C}

enriched in
\text{dOrb}_{/\cdot}
consists of

•

a symmetric set
\text{Ob}(\mathcal{C})
in the category of closed orbifolds, associating to
x
the orbifold
B_{x}
,

•

for any pair of objects
x,y
an object
(\mathcal{C}(x,y),B_{x},B_{y})
of
\text{dOrb}_{/\cdot}
together with a proper energy map

(4.14)

E_{xy}\colon\mathcal{C}(x,y)\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},

•

for any
\sigma\in S_{|x|}
and
\sigma^{\prime}\in S_{|y|}
isomorphisms

(4.15)

\mathcal{C}(\sigma\cdot x,y)\xrightarrow{\sigma^{*}}\mathcal{C}(x,y)\xrightarrow{\sigma^{\prime}_{*}}\mathcal{C}(x,\sigma^{\prime}\cdot y)

that are compatible with the symmetric actions on
\text{Ob}(\mathcal{C})
and which intertwine the energy maps.

•

for any triple
x,y,z
the composition map is a strong equivalence

(4.16)

\mathcal{C}(x,y)\,\overline{\times}_{B_{y}}\,\mathcal{C}(y,z)\to\partial_{y}\mathcal{C}(x,y),

which is a component of the boundary
\partial_{y}\mathcal{C}(x,z)
so that
E_{xz}
restricts to
E_{xy}+E_{yx}
on the image and
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(x,y)\,\overline{\times}_{B_{y}}\,\mathcal{C}(y,z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(x,\tau\cdot y)\,\overline{\times}_{B_{\tau\cdot y}}\,\mathcal{C}(\tau\cdot y,z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(x,z)}

commutes for any
\tau\in S_{|y|}
. We can thus let
\partial_{[y]}\mathcal{C}(x,z)
denote the image of (
4.16
), where
[y]
is the orbit of
y
under the symmetric action.

We require that

(4.17)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(x,y)\,\overline{\times}_{\,B_{y}}\,\mathcal{C}(y,y^{\prime})\,\overline{\times}_{\,B_{y^{\prime}}}\,\mathcal{C}(y^{\prime},z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(x,y)\,\overline{\times}_{\,B_{y}}\,\mathcal{C}(y,z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(x,y^{\prime})\,\overline{\times}_{\,B_{y^{\prime}}}\,\mathcal{C}(y^{\prime},z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(x,z)}

is a pullback square and that
\partial\mathcal{C}(x,z)=\bigsqcup\limits_{[y]}\,\partial_{[y]}\mathcal{C}(x,z).

Definition 4.15

(cf.
[
AB24
, Definition 4.8]
)
.

An
elementary symmetric
n
-flow simplex
consists of a sequence
\mathcal{P}=(\mathcal{P}_{0},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{n})
of symmetric sets, a closed orbifold
B_{p}
for each
p\in\bigsqcup\limits_{i}\mathcal{P}_{i}
, and the data of a symmetric category
\mathscr{X}
enriched in
\text{dOrb}_{/\cdot}
with objects given by the symmetric set
\text{Ob}(\mathscr{X})=\bigsqcup\limits_{i}\mathcal{P}_{i}
, together with a strict
2
-functor
\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{X}}\to\mathcal{P}^{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}
, where
\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{X}}
is the stratifying category of the corners of
\mathscr{X}
. We require the energy map

(4.18)

E\colon\bigsqcup\limits_{q}\mathscr{X}(p,q)\to\mathbb{R}

to be proper for any
p\in\text{Ob}(\mathscr{X})
, with a uniform lower bound independent of
p
.

For the next definition, observe that we can identify the strata of the standard simplex
\Delta^{n}
with subsets of
\{0,\dots,n\}
. Thus, letting
I=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}\}
be the subset associated to a stratum
\sigma\subset\Delta^{n}
, we write
\partial^{\sigma}\mathcal{P}=(\mathcal{P}_{i_{1}},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{i_{k}})
. We write
\epsilon_{i}
for the stratum corresponding to the complement of the singleton
\{i\}
.
Given an elementary symmetric flow simplex
\mathscr{X}
as in Definition
4.15
, we let
\partial^{\sigma}\mathscr{X}
be the restriction of
\mathscr{X}
to
\partial^{\sigma}\mathcal{P}
.
As pointed out in
[
AB24
, Remark 4.12]
, Definition
4.15
