is not quite sufficient since the simplicial set obtained from these elementary symmetric flow simplices might not satisfy the horn-filling property and is thus not an
\infty
-category. This is remedied by the following condition.

Definition 4.16

(cf.
[
AB24
, Definition 4.10]
)
.

A
symmetric
n
-flow simplex
consists of a sequence
\mathcal{P}=(\mathcal{P}_{0},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{n})
of sets, an orbifold
B_{p}
for each
p\in\bigsqcup\limits_{i}\mathcal{P}_{i}
, an elementary symmetric flow simplex
\mathscr{X}^{\sigma}
lifting
\partial^{\sigma}\mathcal{P}
for each stratum
\sigma
of
\Delta^{n}
, and a functor
\mathscr{X}_{\tau}\to\partial^{\tau}X_{\sigma}
enriched in
\text{dOrb}_{/}
for any strata
\tau\subset\sigma
. They lift the isomorphisms of stratifying categories and satisfy the usual associativity condition for any triple
\rho\subset\tau\subset\sigma
of strata. For each
i\in\{0,\dots,n\}
we define

(4.19)

\partial_{i}\mathscr{X}:=(\mathscr{X}_{\sigma})_{\sigma\subseteq\epsilon_{i}}.

The definition of a
structured symmetric flow simplex
in our setting is equivalent to Definition
4.16
with the changes of
[
AB24
, Definition 4.18 and Definition 4.19]
. This uses the isomorphism (
4.2
).
We can now define the semisimplicial set underlying the stable
\infty
-category
\text{Flow}^{\Sigma}
.

Lemma 4.17

(Flow)
.

Letting
\text{Flow}^{\Sigma}_{n}
be the set of symmetric
n
-flow simplices for
n\geq 0
and taking
\partial_{i}\colon\text{Flow}^{\Sigma}_{n}\to\text{Flow}^{\Sigma}_{n-1}
to be the map given by (
4.19
), defines a semisimplicial set
\text{Flow}^{\Sigma}
.∎

The proof is a straightforward verification. As the set-theoretic problems facing this definition are exactly the same as in
[
AB24
]
, we refer to
[
AB24
, Remark 4.14]
for an approach on how to deal with them. We can now state the main result of this subsection, generalizing
[
AB24
, Theorem 1.6]
.

Proposition 4.18
.

\text{Flow}^{\Sigma}
admits the structure of a stable
\infty
-category whose morphisms are symmetric bimodules. The same is true for the stably complex case
\text{Flow}^{\Sigma,U}
.

Proof.

The proof follows from observing that the arguments of
[
AB24
]
carry through. We first observe that the arguments of
[
AB24
, §6]
, in particular, Proposition 6.4 loc. cit., which lift their semisimplicial set
\text{Flow}^{\Sigma}
to a simplicial set, carry over verbatim to our setting. Indeed, the reasoning is formal, using
[
Ste18
]
adapted as in
[
AB24
]
, once one has constructed the inital and terminal degeneracies

\displaystyle s_{0}\colon\text{Flow}^{\Sigma}_{n}\to\text{Flow}^{\Sigma}_{n+1}

\displaystyle s_{n}\colon\text{Flow}^{\Sigma}_{n}\to\text{Flow}^{\Sigma}_{n+1},

and the constructions of
[
AB24
, §6.2]
extends to our setting by replacing the usual product by the sliced fiber product of Definition
4.4
and keeping track of the symmetric action on objects and morphism spaces.

The proof of the horn-filling property is the part that is the least obvious to adapt to our setting as it requires delicate geometric arguments. However, they are always about one morphism space at at a time. Thus, these arguments carry through when having symmetric sets of objects, where each object is simply a point, due to the naturality of the constructions and by replacing the products appearing in the definition of the map (5.5) loc. cit. by quotient fiber products. The proof of
[
AB24
, Lemma 5.8]
, showing the claim of
[
AB24
, Theorem 5.1]
under the simplifying Assumption 1 loc. cit. requires Lemma
4.6
in our setting. The proof without the assumption (cf.
[
AB24
, Proposition 5.12]
) is about a single morphism space and thus is not affected by our generalization of the definition of a flow category. This shows that
\text{Flow}^{\Sigma}
as in Lemma
4.17
is an
\infty
-category.

Recall that an
\infty
-category
C
is
stable
by
[
Lur09
, Theorem 1.1.2.14 and Remark 1.1.2.15]
) if

•

C
has a zero object
*
,

•

the suspension
\Sigma\colon C\to C
, taking
x
to the pushout
\Sigma x
of
*\to x\leftarrow*
, exists and is an auto-equivalence,

•

any morphism in
C
admits a cofiber.

The unit of
\text{Flow}^{\Sigma}
is the flow category
\emptyset
whose set of objects is empty, just as in
[
AB24
, §7.2]
. The proof that the suspension
\Sigma
exists and is an auto-equivalence is the same as the proofs of Lemma 7.8 and Lemma 7.9 in
[
AB24
]
, by associating to the “additional” elements of
s_{i}\mathcal{P}
in Equations (7.31) and (7.32) the obvious orbifolds, and by replacing the products in Equation (7.36) with quotient fiber products. The last property follows from the constructions of
[
AB24
, §7.4]
by noting that they do not require the boundary strata to be products of other morphism spaces. This shows the claim in the unstructured case. The arguments of
[
AB24
, §7.5]
allows us to lift the assertion to structured flow categories.
∎

Lemma 4.19
.

The stable
\infty
-category
\text{Flow}^{\Sigma}
of unstructured flow categories admits all
\aleph_{0}
-small (homotopy) colimits. The same is true for stably complex flow categories.

Proof.

By
[
Lur09
, Proposition 4.4.3.2]
and Theorem
4.18
, it suffices to show that
\text{Flow}^{\Sigma}
has all
\aleph_{0}
-small coproducts. Its coproducts are given by disjoint unions of flow categories: if
\{\mathscr{X}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is a set of symmetric flow categories with object sets
P_{i}
, let
\mathscr{X}
be the flow category with objects the symmetric set
P=\bigsqcup\limits_{i}P_{i}
and morphism spaces

\mathscr{X}(p,q)=\begin{cases}\mathscr{X}_{i}(p,q)&p,q\in P_{i}\\
\emptyset&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

equipped with the obvious symmetric actions and composition maps. It is a straightforward verification that this is indeed a coproduct. The argument carries over verbatim to the stably complex case.
∎

4.2.
A symmetric flow category with bounded action

We can now construct a flow category using the Kuranishi charts of the previous section. Throughout,
(Y,\lambda)
is a closed contact manifold equipped with a non-degenerate contact form
\lambda
. We denote its Reeb vector field by
R
and let
(\widehat{Y},\omega)=(\mathbb{R}\times Y,d(e^{s}\lambda))
be the symplectisation of
(Y,\lambda)
.
Given
L>0
, let

\mathcal{P}_{L}\coloneqq\{\Gamma=(\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{k})\mid\gamma_{i}\text{ is a Reeb orbit, with action }\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}(\gamma_{i})\leq L\}

be the set of finite sequences of unparametrized Reeb orbits of action at most
L
. Recall that given by a function
\Lambda\colon\Gamma^{-}\to\Gamma^{+}
and a sequence
\beta=(\beta_{\gamma})_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}
of relative homology classes, we defined the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\text{SFT}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)_{\Lambda}
of buildings with disconnected domains in Definition
3.47
.

Theorem 4.20
.

Given
L>0
and a choice of
\lambda
-adapted almost complex structure
J
, there exists a symmetric flow category
\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{\leq L}
of class rel–
C^{1}
whose objects are elements of
\mathcal{P}_{L}
and whose morphism spaces are

\mathscr{M}_{\leq L}^{Y,\lambda}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})\,\coloneqq\,\bigsqcup\limits_{\Lambda}\bigsqcup\limits_{\beta}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\text{SFT}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)_{\Lambda}

for any
\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-}\in\mathcal{P}_{L}(Y)
, where the disjoint unions range over functions
\Lambda\colon\Gamma^{-}\to\Gamma^{+}
and sequences
\beta=(\beta_{\gamma})_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}
of relative homology classes.

Observe that the order of
\Gamma^{-}
and
\Gamma^{+}
is opposite the usual one in the morphism space of the symmetric flow category. We do so to be compatible with the conventions of
[
AB24
]
. We use the energy functions

E=E_{\Gamma^{-}\Gamma^{+}}\colon\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{\leq L}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})\to\subset[0,\infty):\mathscr{A}_{\lambda}(\Gamma^{+})-\mathscr{A}_{\lambda}(\Gamma^{-}),

which are clearly additive under composition and proper by
[
BEH+03
]
. The composition is given by the canonical “stacking” of buildings, as shown in

\import

./flo-comp.pdf_tex

Figure 6.
Composition in
\mathscr{M}_{\leq L}^{Y,\lambda}

Remark 4.21
.

The bound by
L
selects Reeb orbits with action less than
L
but does not constrain the length (and action) of
\Gamma
. Any Reeb orbit with action greater than
L
will not occur in the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}_{\text{SFT}}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-},\beta)_{\Lambda}
if
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}(\gamma_{i}^{\pm})\leq L
for all
\gamma_{i}^{+}
. This is due to the fact that each connected component of the holomorphic buildings we consider has exactly one positive puncture.

In order to prove Theorem
4.20
, we construct the global Kuranishi charts for the moduli spaces (
4.20
) inductively as in
[
BX22
]
. Fix a pre-perturbation datum
\mathfrak{D}
consisting of

•

an action bound
L>0
;

•

a
\mathcal{P}_{L}
-integral approximation
\widetilde{\lambda}
of
\lambda
,

•

a translation-invariant
J
-linear connection
\widehat{\nabla}
on
T\widehat{Y}
,

•

a prime number
p\gg\max\{\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(\gamma)\mid\gamma\in\mathcal{P}_{L}\}
.

Recall that
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\text{SFT}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)_{\Lambda}
is the moduli space of disconnected leveled buildings, where each connected component has one positive puncture asymptotic to some
\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}
and negative punctures asymptotic to the elements
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\gamma}\coloneqq\Lambda^{-1}(\gamma)
and of degree
\beta_{\gamma}
.

We will from now on drop the mention of degree to make the notation more tractable. Define a partial order
\prec
on
\mathcal{P}_{L}
by

\Gamma\prec\Gamma^{\prime}\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad\exists\Lambda:\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}_{\text{SFT}}(\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma)_{\Lambda}\neq\emptyset

and define the “norm”

\left\lVert(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime})\right\rVert:=\sup\left\{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\mid\exists\Gamma_{0},\dots,\Gamma_{k}\in\mathcal{P}_{L}:\Gamma=\Gamma_{0}\prec\Gamma_{1}\prec\dots\prec\Gamma_{k}\prec\Gamma^{\prime}\right\}

on
\mathcal{P}_{L}\times\mathcal{P}_{L}
. Note that
\Gamma\nprec\Gamma^{\prime}
if and only if
\left\lVert(\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime})\right\rVert=-\infty
.
We use induction on
\left\lVert(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})\right\rVert
to construct perturbation data for the moduli spaces
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\text{SFT}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-})_{\Lambda}
.
If
\left\lVert(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})\right\rVert=-\infty
, there is nothing to do. Given a pair
\Gamma^{\pm}
of norm
0
and a partition
\Lambda\colon\Gamma^{-}\to\Gamma^{+}
, extend
(\widetilde{\lambda},\widehat{\nabla},p)
to an arbitrary perturbation datum
\alpha_{\Lambda}
for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-})_{\Lambda}
. Let

\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}=(\widehat{G}_{\Lambda},\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda},\mathcal{E}_{\Lambda},\mathfrak{s}_{\Lambda})

be the associated global Kuranishi chart given by Proposition
3.48
. Given permutations
\sigma^{\pm}
, we define
\mathcal{K}_{\sigma^{+}\circ\Lambda\circ\sigma^{-}}
to be the global Kuranishi chart obtained from
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}
by permuting the labels of the positive/negative marked punctures according to
\sigma^{+}
and
\sigma^{-}
respectively. Note that we also have to change the partition
\Lambda
.

Returning to
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}
, recall that its thickening (and obstruction bundle) are rel–
C^{1}
over the base space
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}
, constructed in §
3.1.4
.
It admits canonical smooth maps

(4.20)

\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}\to\prod\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}}\to\prod\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\mathcal{B}(d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}})}

where
\mathcal{B}(d)\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{d},d)
was defined in §
3.1
and the degree is given by

d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}=1+\#\Lambda_{\gamma}-2+p\,\Bigl(\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(\gamma)-\sum\limits_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\gamma}}{\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(\gamma^{\prime})}\Bigr).

In particular, the evaluations maps on the products of
\mathcal{B}(d)
induce smooth
\text{PU}(d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}+1)
-equivariant evaluation maps

(4.21)

\text{ev}^{+}_{\gamma}\colon\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}\to\mathbb{P}^{d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}}

for
\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}
and

(4.22)

\text{ev}^{-}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\colon\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}\to\mathbb{P}^{d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}}

for
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\gamma}
.

4.2.1.
Embeddings of base spaces

We will lift the evaluation maps
\text{ev}^{\pm}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
to smooth maps to the spheres
S^{2d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}+1}
as in
[
BX22
, Definition 5.2.1]
but via a different construction. Then, we will use these lifts to construct embeddings of base spaces that will induce the composition maps of the symmetric flow category later on.
Fix thus
\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}
and set
d\coloneqq d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}
. Let
J_{0}
be the standard complex structure on

(4.23)

\mathbb{R}\times S^{2d+1}\,\cong\,\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(-1)\setminus 0\,\cong\,\mathbb{C}^{d+1}\setminus\{0\},

considered as the symplectization of the contact manifold
S^{2d+1}
equipped with the round (Morse-Bott) contact form. We denote the moduli space of Pardon buildings in
\mathbb{R}\times S^{2d+1}
by
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J_{0}}_{\gamma\sqcup\Lambda_{\gamma}}(S^{2d+1})
and we equip it with the Gromov topology as described in
[
Par19
]
. Note that we do not fix the Reeb orbits the punctures have to be asymptotic to, nor any base point of the Reeb orbits. The quotient map
S^{2d+1}\to\mathbb{P}^{d}
induces a continuous map

(4.24)

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J_{0}}_{\gamma\sqcup\Lambda_{\gamma}}(S^{2d+1})\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,\gamma\sqcup\Lambda_{\gamma}}(\mathbb{P}^{d},d)

and we denote by
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}
the preimage of
\mathcal{B}_{\gamma\sqcup\Lambda_{\gamma}}(d)
under (
4.24
).
By construction, the map (
4.24
) lifts to a continuous map
q\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}\to\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}
.

Lemma 4.22
.

The map

q\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}\to\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}
is a principal
S^{1}
-bundle.

Proof.

The
S^{1}
-action on
S^{2d+1}
induces a continuous
S^{1}
-action on
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}
, with respect to which the evaluation
\text{ev}^{+}_{\gamma}
at the positive puncture is equivariant. Since
S^{1}
acts freely on
S^{2d+1}
, the action on
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}
is free as well. The map
q
is clearly
S^{1}
-invariant. Since
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}\to\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}
is the pullback of the proper map (
4.24
), it is proper, and thus so is
q
.

It remains to show that the descent of
q
to the quotient is bijective.
To see surjectivity, fix
[\varphi,C,b,m]\in\mathcal{B}^{P}_{d}
, where
b
denotes the asymptotic markers and
m
denotes the matching isomorphisms, and let
T
be the underlying decorated graph. Recall that to every vertex
v
with one incoming edge and
k
outgoing edges we can associate numbers
d^{+}_{v},d^{-}_{v,1},\dots d^{-}_{v,k}
such that

\deg(\varphi_{v})=d^{+}_{v}-d^{-}_{v,1}-\dots d^{-}_{v,k}+(k+1)-2=(d^{+}_{v}-1)-(d^{-}_{v,1}-1)-\dots(d^{-}_{v,k}-1).

Thus, there is a
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-family of sections of
\varphi_{v}^{*}\mathcal{O}(-1)
with pole of order
d^{+}_{v}-1
at
z_{v,e}\in\mathbb{C}_{v}
for
e\in E^{+}_{v}
and zeroes of order
d^{-}_{v,i}-1
at
z_{v,e_{i}}\in\mathbb{C}_{v}
for
e_{i}\in E^{-}_{v}
with
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
. Note that the degrees
d_{v,i}^{-}
and
d_{v}^{+}
are always at least
3
by our choice of integer
p
.
Using the identification (
4.23
), we can construct the lifts of
\varphi_{v}
by choosing sections of
\varphi^{*}_{v}\mathcal{O}(-1)
satisfying the pole-zero arithmetic described above and then projecting to
S^{2d+1}
. Note that the choice of the meromorphic section over
\varphi_{v}^{*}\mathcal{O}(-1)
is only well defined up to the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action given by scaling. Thus, writing
D_{v}
for the divisor of punctures of
C_{v}
, a lift
\Phi_{v}:B_{D_{v}}C_{v}\to S^{2d+1}
is only well defined up to the Hopf action. Fix a vertex
v\in V(T)
and a lift
\Phi_{v}
. By using the matching condition at the punctures, we can find unique lifts
\Phi_{v^{\prime}}
for vertices
v^{\prime}\neq v
such that
\Phi\colon\cup_{v\in V(T)}C_{v}\setminus D_{v}\to S^{2d+1}
is a Pardon building. From the construction it is clear that there is an
S^{1}
family of such lifts of
[\varphi,C,b,m]
. The injectivity of the descent of
q
follows directly.
∎

Lemma 4.23
.

For
p\in\{\gamma\}\sqcup\Lambda_{\gamma}
, let
\text{ev}_{p}
be the respective evaluation map of (
4.21
) or (
4.22
). Then the following holds.

1)

\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}
is isomorphic as a topological principal
S^{1}
-bundle to
\text{ev}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{*}S^{2d+1}
,

2)

If we equip
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}
with the smooth structure pulled back from
\text{ev}_{\gamma}^{*}S^{2d+1}
, then there exists a
U(d+1)
-equivariant smooth submersion
\widetilde{}\text{ev}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}\to S^{2d+1}
so that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{2d+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{}\text{ev}_{\gamma^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{ev}_{\gamma^{\prime}}}

commutes for each
p\in\Lambda_{\gamma}
.

3)

The restriction of
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}
to

(4.25)

\mathscr{B}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}:=\text{ev}_{\gamma}^{-1}(\{[1:0:\dots:0]\})

is a trivializable principal bundle with a compatible
\text{U}(d)
-action, where
\text{U}(d)\hookrightarrow\text{PU}(d+1)
is the canonical embedding.

Proof.

The first assertion is a consequence of Lemma
4.22
and the fact that the induced map
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}\to\text{ev}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{*}S^{2d+1}
is equivariant, hence a morphism of principal bundles. Taking
p=\gamma
, this allows us to pull back the smooth structure on
\text{ev}_{\gamma}^{*}S^{2d+1}
to
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}
. Note that
\widetilde{}\text{ev}_{\gamma}
is smooth with respect to this smooth structure. Now, for
p\in\{\gamma\}\sqcup\Lambda_{\gamma}
, we obtain by
[
MW09
, Proposition I.13]
a diffeomorphism
\psi_{\gamma^{\prime}}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}\to\text{ev}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{*}S^{2d+1}
, which is the canonical map if
p=\gamma
. By using an equivariant version of the Whitney approximation theorem, we can ensure that
\psi_{\gamma^{\prime}}
is
U(d+1)
-equivariant. Thus, we can define
\widetilde{}\text{ev}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
to be the composition

(4.26)

\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}\,\xrightarrow{\psi_{\gamma^{\prime}}}\,\text{ev}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{*}S^{2d+1}\,\to\,S^{2d+1}.

Since the second map in (
4.26
) is a smooth submersion, the whole composition is a smooth submersion. It lifts
\text{ev}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
by construction.
Now, let

\widetilde{\text{ev}}_{\gamma}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}\to S^{2d+1}

be the evaluation map at the positive puncture. Then the restriction of
q
to

\widetilde{\text{ev}}_{\gamma}^{-1}(\{(1,0,\dots,0)\})\,\to\mathscr{B}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}

is an isomorphism, whence the claim follows.
∎

Remark 4.24
.

Note that we do not use regularity of
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}
to obtain a smooth structure and that the lifted evaluation maps
\widetilde{}\text{ev}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
might not agree with the natural evaluation maps on this moduli space of buildings. While we believe this to be true, we do not need and hence do not show it.

Corollary 4.25

(Spherical evaluation map)
.

There exists a lift of the evaluation map
\text{ev}^{-}\colon\mathscr{B}^{P}_{d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}}\to(\mathbb{P}^{d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}})^{\Lambda_{\gamma}}
to a smooth
U(d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}})
-equivariant map

(4.27)

\widetilde{}\text{ev}^{-}\colon\mathscr{B}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}\to(S^{2d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}+1})^{\Lambda_{\gamma}}.

Proof.

We may assume without loss of generality that
\Lambda^{-1}(\gamma)\neq\emptyset
. Take
\widetilde{}\text{ev}^{-}
to be the product of the compositions

\mathscr{B}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}\,\xrightarrow{\sim}\,\widetilde{}\text{ev}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{-1}(\{(1,0,\dots,0)\})\,\xrightarrow{\widetilde{}\text{ev}_{\gamma^{\prime}}}\,S^{2d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}+1}

over all
p\in\Lambda_{\gamma}
. Since
\widetilde{}\text{ev}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
is
U(d+1)
-equivariant, its restriction to
\widetilde{}\text{ev}_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{-1}(\{(1,0,\dots,0)\})
is
U(d)
-equivariant. As the first isomorphism is the inverse of a
U(d)
-equivariant map, the resulting map is equivariant as desired.
∎

Set
\mathscr{B}^{P}_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\prod\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\mathscr{B}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda_{\gamma}}}

and let
\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}=\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\Lambda}}\mathscr{B}^{P}_{\Lambda}
be the pullback, where
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}
and the map to
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\Lambda}
were constructed in §
3.1.4
. Equivalently,
\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}
is a fiber of the (submersive) evaluation map
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}\to\prod\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\mathbb{P}^{d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}}}.
Abbreviate

(4.28)

\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\prod\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\text{PGL}_{d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}+1}(\mathbb{C})_{[1:0:\dots:0]}}\qquad\qquad G_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\prod\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\text{U}(d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}})}

which acts smoothly on
\mathscr{B}^{P}_{\Lambda}
and
\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}
via the embedding

A\in U(d)\;\mapsto\;\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&A\end{pmatrix}\in\text{PU}(d+1)

onto the stabilizer of
[1:0:\dots:0]\in\mathbb{P}^{d}
. Since
\text{PU}(d+1)
acts transitively on
\mathbb{P}^{d}
for any
d\geq 1
, the inclusion induces an isomorphism

(4.29)

\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}/G_{\Lambda}\,\cong\,\prod\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\text{PGL}_{d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}+1}(\mathbb{C})/\text{PU}(d_{\Lambda}+1)}.

Proposition 4.26
.

Given a factorization
\Gamma^{-}\xrightarrow{\Lambda^{0}}\Gamma_{0}\to\dots\to\Gamma_{k-1}\xrightarrow{\Lambda^{k}}\Gamma^{+}
, there exists a smooth embedding

(4.30)

\Psi\colon\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{0}}\underset{\;\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma_{0}}}{\times}\dots\underset{\;\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma_{k-1}}}{\times}\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{k}}\,\hookrightarrow\,\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda},

where
\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{j-1}}\underset{\;\;\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{j-1}}}{\times}\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{j}}
is the quotient of the product by the diagonal action of
\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{j-1}}
. The embedding maps to a boundary stratum of codimension
k
and is equivariant with respect to the block matrix inclusion

(4.31)

G_{\Lambda^{k}}\times\dots\times G_{\Lambda^{0}}\hookrightarrow G_{\Lambda}.

Proof.

We construct this map first at the level of the base spaces
\mathcal{B}^{P}
and then lift it to (
4.30
). We only discuss the case of
k=1
, the more general case follows analogously.
Suppose thus
\Lambda^{1}\colon\Gamma\to\Gamma^{+}
and
\Lambda^{0}\colon\Gamma^{-}\to\Gamma
are two partitions with
\Lambda=\Lambda^{1}\circ\Lambda^{0}
. We will consider the case where
\Gamma^{+}=\{\gamma^{+}\}
; the case of more components in the top level is a straightforward generalization. Assume without loss of generality that
\Gamma\neq\emptyset
.

For any
d_{0},\dots,d_{n}\geq 1
we have a smooth map

\displaystyle F_{i}\colon S^{2d+1}\times\mathbb{P}^{d_{i}}\,\to\,\mathbb{P}^{d_{0}+\dots+d_{n}}

(4.32)

\displaystyle(a,[z])\mapsto[a_{0}z_{0}:\dots:a_{d_{0}}z_{0}:0:\dots:0:z_{1}:\dots:z_{d_{i}}:0:\dots:0],

considering
S^{2d+1}
as a subset of
\mathbb{C}^{d+1}
and inserting
z_{1},\dots,z_{d_{i}}
in the positions
(d_{0}+\dots+d_{i-1}+1)
to
(d_{0}+\dots+d_{i}+1)
. Now, given
\varphi_{\gamma^{+}}=[\check{\varphi}_{\gamma^{+}},C_{\gamma^{+}},m_{\gamma^{+}}]\in\mathscr{B}^{P}_{{\gamma^{+}},\Gamma}
and
\varphi_{\gamma}=[\check{\varphi}_{\gamma},C_{\gamma},m_{\gamma}]\in\mathscr{B}^{P}_{\gamma,\Lambda^{0}_{\gamma}}
for
\gamma\in\Gamma
, we define

\widetilde{\varphi}\colon C_{\gamma^{+}}\sqcup\bigsqcup\limits_{\gamma}C_{\gamma}\,\to\,\mathbb{P}^{d_{\Lambda_{\gamma^{+}}}}

by

(4.33)

\widetilde{\varphi}|_{C_{\gamma}}=\begin{cases}j\circ\varphi_{\gamma^{+}}\quad&\gamma=\gamma^{+}\\
F_{\gamma^{+}}(\widetilde{}\text{ev}_{\gamma}(\varphi_{\gamma^{+}},-)\circ\varphi_{\gamma}\quad&\gamma\in\Gamma,\end{cases}

where
j\colon\mathbb{P}^{d_{\Gamma}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{d_{\Lambda_{\gamma^{+}}}}:[z]\mapsto[z:0\dots:0]
is the inclusion into the first homogeneous coordinates.
By Corollary
4.25
and the definition of
\mathscr{B}
, this descends to a holomorphic map
\check{\varphi}\colon C\to\mathbb{P}^{d_{\Lambda_{\gamma^{+}}}}
on the curve
C
obtained from clutching
C_{0}
and the
C_{\gamma}
at the respective marked point. Using the lift of the clutching map to the real-oriented blow-up, we obtain the map (
4.73
).
Since

j^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{d_{\Lambda_{\gamma^{+}}}}}(1)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{d_{\Gamma}}}(1)\qquad\quad\qquad F_{\gamma}(a_{\gamma},\cdot)^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{d_{\Lambda_{\gamma^{+}}}}}(1)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{d_{\Lambda^{0}_{\gamma}}}}(1),

the map
\Psi_{\Lambda^{1},\Lambda^{0}}
is a well-defined map

(4.34)

\mathscr{B}^{P}_{\Lambda^{0}}\underset{\,\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma}}{\times}\mathscr{B}^{P}_{\Lambda^{1}}\to\mathscr{B}^{P}_{\Lambda}

It is smooth and equivariant with respect to the inclusion (
4.31
) by construction. To lift it, observe that the universal family
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{B}(\Lambda)
is canonically embedded in the product
\mathcal{B}(\Lambda)\times\prod\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma}{\mathbb{P}^{d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}}}
. Pulling back the Fubini–Study metric on projective space, we obtain for any asymptotic marker a canonical lift to the normal bundle of the respective divisor. Using the explicit lift description in the proof of Lemma
3.17
and in the discussion below Theorem
3.7
, we can lift the map (
4.34
) to (
4.30
) by incorporating the lengths into the matching isomorphisms. Concretely, recall that the matching isomorphisms at the newly created level jump are given by the sequence
(m_{\gamma})_{\gamma\in\Gamma}=([b^{0}_{\gamma}\otimes b^{1}_{\gamma}])_{\gamma}
. The
b^{i}_{\gamma}\in(T_{z_{\gamma}}C^{i}\setminus 0)/\mathbb{R}_{>0}
are the respective asymptotic markers, and
[b^{0}_{\gamma}\otimes b^{1}_{\gamma}]
indicates their image in the quotient by the
S^{1}
-action. Writing
\widetilde{b}^{i}_{\gamma}
for the lift to
T_{z_{\gamma}}C^{i}
and given
a\in\mathbb{R}^{\Gamma}_{>0}/\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, we define the ‘refined matching isomorphism’
\widetilde{m}
to be the image of
(\widetilde{b}^{0}_{\gamma}\,\otimes\,a_{\gamma}\widetilde{b}^{1}_{\gamma})_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
in the quotient

\mathbb{P}_{>0}\Bigl(\bigoplus\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\sigma_{\gamma}^{*}T_{\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{o}}_{\Lambda^{0}}/\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda^{0}}}\otimes\sigma_{\gamma}^{*}T_{\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{o}}_{\Lambda^{1}}/\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda^{1}}}\Bigr)/\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma},

where
\mathbb{P}_{>0}
of a vector bundle was defined in Equation (
3.6
). This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.27
.

For any factorization
\Gamma^{-}\xrightarrow{\Lambda^{0}}\Gamma_{0}\xrightarrow{\Lambda^{1}}\Gamma_{1}\xrightarrow{\Lambda^{2}}\Gamma^{+}
of
\Lambda
, the square

(4.35)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{0}}\underset{\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma_{0}}}{\times}\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{1}}\underset{\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma_{1}}}{\times}\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{0}}\underset{\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma_{0}}}{\times}\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{21}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{10}}\underset{\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma_{10}}}{\times}\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi\times\text{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{id}\times\Psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi}

commutes and is a pullback square.

Proof.

The argument is analogous to the proof of
[
BX22
, Proposition 5.22]
.
∎

Given a sequence
\Lambda^{*}=\{\Lambda_{i}\}_{i=0}^{k}
of partitions composing to
\Lambda\colon\Gamma^{-}\to\Gamma^{+}
, define

(4.36)

\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{*}}\,\coloneqq\,G_{\Lambda}\times_{G_{\Lambda^{*}}}\text{im}(\Psi_{\Lambda^{*}}),

where
G_{\Lambda^{*}}=\prod_{j=0}^{k}G_{\Lambda^{j}}
. For the next result, we have to recall some definitions from
[
BX22
, §5.2.5]
. For all
d\geq 0
, set
{\bm{Q}}_{d}\coloneqq\tilde{\bm{Q}}_{d}/\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, where

\tilde{\bm{Q}}_{d}:=\left\{\tilde{h}\in\mathbb{C}^{(d+1)\times(d+1)}\ |\ \tilde{h}^{\ast}=\tilde{h},\ \tilde{h}_{00}\neq 0\right\}.

We use the convention that the indices of the Hermitian matrix
\tilde{h}\in\tilde{\bm{Q}}_{d}
range from
0
to
d
. The multiplicative group
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
acts on
\tilde{\bm{Q}}_{d}
by scalar multiplication on each entry. The
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-orbit of
\tilde{h}\in\tilde{\bm{Q}}_{d}
is denoted by
[\tilde{h}]
. We identify
{\bm{Q}}_{d}
with

{\bm{Q}}_{d}^{*}:=\Big\{h\in\mathbb{C}^{(d+1)\times(d+1)}\ |\ h^{\ast}=h,\ h_{00}=0\Big\}

in the way that a Hermitian matrix
h
with
h_{00}=0
is identified with the
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
-orbit of
\tilde{h}=I_{d+1}+h
. Then
{\bm{Q}}_{d}
is a real vector space with dimension equal to
d^{2}+2d
.

Lemma 4.28
.

There exists a smooth
G_{\Lambda}
-vector bundle

(4.37)

Q_{\Lambda^{*}}\to\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{*}}

so that the total space admits an equivariant diffeomorphism
Q_{\Lambda^{*}}\to\partial_{\Lambda^{*}}\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}
to the corner stratum of
\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}
extending the embedding (
4.39
),∎

Proof.

This is similar to
[
BX22
, Proposition 5.24]
but since we have several punctures and asymptotic markers at outgoing as well as incoming punctures, we describe how the proof has to be adapted. We make a few simplifying assumptions to keep the exposition clear while still highlighting the essential modifications needed. Thus we will deal with the case of
\Lambda^{*}=\{\Lambda^{0},\Lambda^{1}\}
, hence
\Lambda=\Lambda^{1}\circ\Lambda^{0}
. We further assume
\Gamma^{+}=\{\gamma^{+}\}
. The general result will follow from a similar but combinatorially more involved argument. Write
\Gamma=(\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{m}).

Define the bundle
Q_{\Lambda^{*}}
to be the
G_{\Lambda}
-equivariantization of the
G_{\Lambda^{*}}
bundle
\bm{Q}_{\Lambda^{*}}\times\text{im}(\Psi_{\Lambda^{*}})
where
\bm{Q}_{\Lambda^{*}}
is a distinguished subspace of
\bm{Q}_{{1+d_{0}+d_{10}\dots+d_{1m}}}
. The required equivariant diffeomorphism
Q_{\Lambda^{*}}\to\partial_{\Lambda^{*}}\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}
is determined uniquely by the map

\rho\colon\bm{Q}_{\Lambda^{*}}\times\text{im}(\Psi_{\Lambda^{*}})\to\partial_{\Lambda^{*}}\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}

such that

\rho(h,\varphi)=(Id+\rho_{h})\varphi,

where
\rho_{h}
is the upper diagonal matrix corresponding to
h
. The proof of the map
Q_{\Lambda^{*}}\to\partial_{\Lambda^{*}}\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}
being injective follows exactly by the same arguments as in
[
BX22
, Proposition 5.24]
.

In order to prove surjectivity, recall that a rational stable map
\varphi
of degree
\diamond\leq d
curve in
\mathbb{P}^{d}
lies in a unique minimal linear
\mathbb{P}(W)\subset\mathbb{P}^{d}
where
\dim W=\diamond+1
. We call
W
the
linear span
of
\varphi

5
5

5

We are slightly abusing terminology since classically the linear span is defined as the projectivization of the vector space we consider here
. Thus, any curve
\varphi\in\partial_{\Lambda^{*}}\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}
, determines a system of linear projective spaces
\mathbb{P}(W_{0}),\mathbb{P}(W_{10}),\,\dots,\,\mathbb{P}(W_{1m})
where
W_{*}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{1+d_{0}+d_{10}\dots+d_{1m}}
is a subspace so that

•

W_{0}
is the linear span of
\varphi_{\gamma^{+}}
and
W_{1i}
is the linear span of
\varphi_{\gamma_{i}}
,

•

\dim(W_{0}\cap W_{1i})=1
,

•

W_{1i}\cap W_{1j}=\emptyset
unless
i=j
,

•

W_{0}+W_{10}\dots+W_{1m}=\mathbb{C}^{1+d_{0}+d_{10}\dots+d_{1m}}.

The above system of spaces can be viewed as a ‘tree’ generalization of the fans defined in
[
BX22
, §5.2.5]
. Thus, we call such a system a
t-fan
. Set
L_{i}\coloneqq W_{0}\cap W_{1i}
. We say a t-fan is
normal
if
W_{0}
is orthogonal to the orthogonal complement of
L_{i}\hookrightarrow W_{1j}
for all
i
. A similar argument as in
[
BX22
, Proposition 5.21]
proves that
\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{*}}
is exactly the set of curves with normal t-fans.

The
t-flag
corresponding to the t-fan
W_{*}
is defined as

•

V_{0}=W_{0}
,

•

V_{1i}=W_{0}+W_{1i}.

By using the Gram–Schmidt process, any t-flag can be mapped to a standard t-flag under an action of
g\in G_{\Lambda}
where the standard t-flag is defined as

•

V_{0}=\mathbb{C}^{1+d_{0}}
,

•

V_{1j}=\mathbb{C}^{1+d_{0}}\times\{0\}^{d_{11}+\dots d_{1j-1}}\times\mathbb{C}^{d_{1j}}\times\{0\}^{d_{1j+1}+\dots d_{1m}}.

Thus, given some
\varphi\in\partial_{\Lambda^{*}}\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}
, we can assume that the t-flag corresponding to it is the standard one. In particular we can assume that that linear span of
\varphi_{\gamma^{+}}
is
\mathbb{C}^{1+d_{0}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{1+d_{0}+d_{10}\dots+d_{1m}}
, i.e., is given by the first
1+d_{0}
coordinates. Let
y_{i}=\widetilde{ev}_{\gamma_{i}}(\varphi_{\gamma_{+}})\in\mathbb{C}^{1+d_{0}}
and choose vectors
w^{1i}_{1},w^{1i}_{2},\dots,w^{1i}_{d_{i}}
such that
W_{1i}
has a basis
(y_{i},w^{1i}_{1},w^{1i}_{2},\dots,w^{1i}_{d_{i}}).

Let
\bm{Q}_{\Lambda^{*}}
denote the elements of
\bm{Q}_{1+d_{0}+\dots}
which are of the form
Id+A+A^{*}
where
A
is of the following block matrix form,

(4.38)

A=\begin{bmatrix}0&A^{0}_{1}&A^{0}_{2}&\cdots&A^{0}_{m}\\
0&0&0&\cdots&0\\
0&0&0&0&\cdots\\
\vdots&&&\ddots&\\
\end{bmatrix}.

We will construct an element
h\in\bm{Q}^{*}_{1+d_{0}+\dots}
such that
I+h^{u}
takes the t-fan
W_{*}
to a normal t-fan where
h^{u}
is the upper triangular matrix corresponding to
h
. Denote the orthogonal complement of
W_{0}\cap W_{1i}
in
W_{1i}
as
W_{i}^{o}
. Note that
W_{i}^{o}
can be viewed as a graph of a linear function
T^{o}_{i}:\mathbb{C}^{d_{i}}\to\mathbb{C}^{1+d_{0}}
. Thus we can find a matrix
A
of the form as described in (
4.38
) such that
I+A+A^{*}
takes the t-fan
W_{*}
to the normal t-fan

•

\mathscr{W}_{0}=\mathbb{C}^{1+d_{0}}\times\{0\}^{d_{11}+d_{12}\dots}
,

•

\mathscr{W}_{i}=\mathbb{C}\cdot\langle y_{i}\rangle\oplus\{0\}^{1+d_{0}+d_{11}\dots d_{1i-1}}\times\mathbb{C}^{d_{1i}}\times\{0\}^{d_{1i+1}+\dots}
.

This shows that
\varphi
lies in the image of the map
Q_{\Lambda^{*}}\to\partial_{\Lambda^{*}}\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}
.
∎

4.2.2.
Embeddings of families of buildings

We first discuss the inductive construction when
\left\lVert(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-})\right\rVert=1
. Let
\Lambda\colon\Gamma^{-}\to\Gamma^{+}
be a partition and
\Gamma^{-}\xrightarrow{\Lambda^{0}}\Gamma\xrightarrow{\Lambda^{1}}\Gamma^{+}
be a factorization. We will do the construction for the moduli space associated to
\Lambda
and then endow any other moduli space in the orbit of the symmetric action with the same global Kuranishi chart after permuting the labels of the punctures.

By the inductive hypothesis, we have constructed global Kuranishi charts
\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda^{0}}
and
\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda^{1}}
with base spaces
\mathscr{B}^{P}_{\Lambda^{0}}
, respectively
\mathscr{B}^{P}_{\Lambda^{1}}
and by Proposition
4.26
, there exists an embedding

(4.39)

\Psi\colon\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{0}}\times_{S^{1}}\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{1}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}

whose image is contained in a boundary stratum
\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{01}}
of
\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}
. Define the ‘restricted family’

\mathscr{Z}_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}\times_{\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\Lambda}}

of buildings, where
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{B}^{P}_{\Lambda}}
was defined in Definition
3.20
. To lift the embeddings (
4.39
) to maps between these families, we need the following definition.

Definition 4.29

(Orbifold associated to Reeb orbit)
.

Given an unparametrized Reeb orbit
\gamma
, let

(4.40)

E\gamma\coloneqq\left\{\sigma\in C^{\infty}(S^{1},Y)\mid\dot{\sigma}=\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}(\gamma)\,R(\sigma),\,\text{im}(\sigma)=\text{im}(\gamma)\right\}

be the space of parametrized Reeb orbits lying over
\gamma
. Then,
S^{1}
acts transitively on
E\gamma
with isotropy
\mathbb{Z}/m_{\gamma}
and we define

(4.41)

B\gamma:=[E\gamma/S^{1}].

Note that we have an equivalence
B\mathbb{Z}/{m_{\gamma}}\to B\gamma
, where
m_{\gamma}
is the multiplicity of
\gamma
.

The asymptotic markers yield rel–
C^{1}
evaluation maps

(4.42)

p_{\gamma}\colon\overline{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}\coloneqq[\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}/\widehat{G}_{\Lambda}]\to B\gamma

induced by

(\varphi,u,w)\mapsto[\theta\mapsto(\widehat{u})_{z_{\gamma}}(\theta\cdot b_{\gamma})],

where
\theta\in S^{1}
and for each
\gamma
labeling an exterior edge
b_{\gamma}
denotes the asymptotic marker associated to
\varphi
and
\gamma
. Given a sequence
\Gamma=(\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{k})
of Reeb orbits, we set

B\Gamma\coloneqq\prod_{i=1}^{k}B{\gamma}_{i}.

Then, the (smooth local) embeddings (
4.39
) lift to (continuous) embeddings

(4.43)

\Psi_{\Lambda^{01}}\colon\mathscr{Z}_{\Lambda^{0}}\overline{\times}_{B\Gamma}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\Lambda^{1}}\coloneqq\Bigl(\mathscr{Z}_{\Lambda^{0}}\times_{B\Gamma}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\Lambda^{1}}\Bigr)/S^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{Z}_{\Lambda},

where
S^{1}
acts on the fiber product via the diagonal embedding
S^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma}\times\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma}
. The lift uses the fact that the map (
4.39
) is covered by a canonical isomorphism

(4.44)

\text{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathscr{C}_{\Gamma_{i},\Gamma^{+}}^{\mathrm{o}}\sqcup\text{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathscr{C}_{\Gamma^{-},\Gamma_{i}}^{\mathrm{o}}\cong\Psi^{*}\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{o}}_{\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-}}.

Note that we have already fixed a pre-perturbation datum
\mathfrak{D}
for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\text{SFT}}^{J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-})_{\Lambda}
. Suppose
\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda}
is a good covering as in Definition
3.24
and
\zeta
a smooth map as in (
3.23
) yielding a
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}
-equivariant map

(4.45)

\zeta_{\Lambda}\colon\mathcal{Z}_{\Lambda}\to\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\bigoplus\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\text{Lie}(\text{PU}({d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}+1}))},

due to the isomorphism (
4.29
). The pullback of (
4.45
) to
\mathscr{Z}_{\Lambda}
yields a
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}
-equivariant map
\zeta_{\Lambda}\colon\mathscr{Z}_{\Lambda}\to\mathfrak{g}_{\Lambda}
. Thus, we may demand that
\zeta_{\Lambda}
restricts to
\zeta_{\Lambda^{1}}\times\zeta_{\Lambda^{0}}
over the respective boundary stratum. Assume also that we have found a perturbation space
(E_{\Lambda},\mu)
so that
(E_{\Lambda^{1}},\mu_{\Lambda^{1}})\times(E_{\Lambda^{1}},\mu_{\Lambda^{1}})
admits a linear equivariant embedding into the pullback of
(E,\mu)
along (
4.44
). Recall that the perturbation space depends on the choice of
\zeta_{\Lambda}
, since the regularity condition has to be satisfied over the locus
\{(\varphi,u)\mid\bar{\partial}_{J}u=0,\,\zeta_{\Lambda}(\varphi,u)=0\}
. Then,
\alpha_{\Lambda}=(\mathfrak{D},\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda},\zeta,E_{\Lambda},\mu_{\Lambda})
is a well-defined perturbation datum. Thus, Proposition
3.48
yields a global Kuranishi chart

\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}=\Bigl(\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{+}}\times\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{-}}\times G^{\prime}_{\Lambda},\mathcal{T}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda},\mathcal{E}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda},\mathfrak{s}_{\Lambda}\Bigr)

for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}_{\text{SFT}}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-})_{\Lambda}
equipped with a rel–
C^{1}
map
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}\to\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}
. We let
G_{\Lambda}\subset G^{\prime}_{\Lambda}
be the product of stabilizers of
[1:0:\dots:0]
and set
\widehat{G}_{\Lambda}:=\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{+}}\times\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{-}}\times G_{\Lambda}
. Define the
\widehat{G}_{\Lambda}
-invariant subspaces

(4.46)

\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}}\,\mathcal{T}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}\qquad\qquad\mathscr{E}_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}\times_{\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}}\,\mathcal{E}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}

and denote the pullback of
\mathfrak{s}_{\Lambda}
to
\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}
by the same symbol. By construction, the embeddings (
4.43
) induce rel–
C^{1}
embeddings

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}\colon\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda^{0}}\overline{\times}_{B\Gamma}\,\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda^{1}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}

(4.47)

\displaystyle((\varphi_{0},u_{0},w_{0}),(\varphi_{1},u_{1},w_{1}))\mapsto(\Psi((\varphi_{0},u_{0}),\varphi_{1},u_{1})),w_{0}\oplus w_{1}).

They are covered by embeddings of obstruction bundles, defined as follows. The original obstruction bundle is the direct sum
\mathcal{E}_{\Lambda}=E_{\Lambda}\oplus\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}(d_{\Lambda}+1)
. The embeddings
E_{\Lambda^{1}_{i}}\oplus E_{\Lambda^{0}_{i}}\hookrightarrow E_{\Lambda}
exist by assumption, while the embeddings of Lie algebras are by the inclusion (
4.31
) of covering groups. We define

(4.48)

\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda^{0}}\overline{\times}_{B\Gamma}\,\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda^{1}}\coloneqq\Bigl(\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{-}}\times G_{\Lambda^{0}}\times G_{\Lambda^{1}}\times\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{+}},\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda^{0}}\overline{\times}_{B\Gamma}\,\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda^{1}},\mathscr{E}_{\Lambda^{0}}\boxplus\mathscr{E}_{\Lambda^{1}},\mathfrak{s}_{\Lambda^{0}}\boxplus\mathfrak{s}_{\Lambda^{1}}\Bigr)

and summarize the construction in the following lemma.

Lemma 4.30
.

There exists a global Kuranishi chart

(4.49)

\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda}\;\coloneqq\;(\widehat{G}_{\Lambda},\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda},\mathscr{E}_{\Lambda},\mathfrak{s}_{\Lambda})

for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\text{SFT}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-})_{\Lambda}
admitting strong equivalences

(4.50)

\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda^{0}}\overline{\times}_{B\Gamma}\,\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda^{1}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda}

onto a boundary strata of
\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda}
for any factorization
\Gamma^{-}\xrightarrow{\Lambda^{0}}\Gamma\xrightarrow{\Lambda^{1}}\Gamma^{+}
of
\Lambda
.

Proof.

The discussion above shows that it remains to construct a suitable good covering and a suitable perturbation space. To keep the notation tractable, we assume that
\Gamma^{+}=\{\gamma\}
and
\Lambda^{1}=\{\gamma^{\prime}\}
. The general case is a straightforward generalization.

Step 1:
The usual clutching maps induce embeddings

(4.51)

\psi\colon\mathscr{B}^{\text{st}}_{1+\#\Lambda^{1}+3d^{\prime}_{\Lambda^{1}}}(d_{\Lambda^{1}})\times\mathscr{B}^{\text{st}}_{1+\#\Lambda^{0}+3d^{\prime}_{\Lambda^{0}}}(d_{\Lambda^{0}})\hookrightarrow\mathscr{B}^{\text{st}}_{1+\#\Lambda+3d^{\prime}_{\Lambda}}

where the superscript indicates that we only consider stable maps. The maps
\psi
are equivariant with respect to the inclusions
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda^{1}}\times\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda^{1}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}
. By
[
AMS24
, Lemma 4.13]
taking
X=\{\text{pt}\}
in Definition 4.6 op. cit., the
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}
-action on
\mathcal{B}^{\,\text{st}}_{1+\#\Lambda+3d^{\prime}_{\Lambda}}
is Palais proper. Thus,
[
Pal61
, Theorem 4.3.1]
asserts that
\mathscr{B}^{\text{st}}_{\Lambda,+3d^{\prime}_{\Lambda}}
admits a
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}
-invariant Riemannian metric as well as
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}
-invariant bump functions. First, we can extend the product of the
\zeta_{i}:=\zeta_{\mathcal{U}^{i}}
to the canonical
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}
-equivariant function

\zeta_{01}\colon\text{im}(\psi)^{\sim}\coloneqq\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}\times_{\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda^{1}}\times\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda^{0}}}\text{im}(\psi)\,\to\,\mathfrak{g}_{\Lambda}.

Then, choosing a
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}
-equivariant tubular neighborhood of
\text{im}(\psi)^{\sim}
, we can extend
\zeta_{01}
to a
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}
-equivariant function on an open neighborhood of
\text{im}(\psi)^{\sim}
. If we have several embeddings of the form (
4.51
), their images are disjoint, even under the
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}
-action, due to our assumption on
\Gamma^{+}
and
\Gamma^{-}
. Thus we can choose the tubular neighborhoods to be disjoint. Then, using
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}
-invariant cut-off functions, we can extend the thus obtained functions to a smooth
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}
-equivariant function

(4.52)

\zeta\colon\mathscr{B}^{\text{st}}_{\Lambda,+3d^{\prime}_{\Lambda}}\to\mathfrak{g}_{\Lambda}.

It remains to extend the good coverings
\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda^{1}}
and
\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda^{0}}
. Recall that such a good covering consists of a finite collection
\{(U_{i},\sigma_{i,j},D_{i,j},\chi_{i})\}_{i,j}
of

i)

open
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
-invariant subsets
U_{i}\subset\mathscr{Z}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
that cover
\mathscr{Z}_{\bar{\partial}}

ii)

a smooth
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
-equivariant section
\sigma_{i,j}\colon U_{i}\to\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{o}}|_{U_{i}}
for
1\leq j\leq 3d_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
and divisors
D_{i,j}\subset Y
so that for any
(\varphi,u)\in U_{i}
, we have
u\,\pitchfork\,D_{i}
and
u(\sigma_{i,j}(\varphi,u))\in D_{i,j}
and

\#\,C_{v}\cap\{\sigma_{i,j}(\varphi,u)\}_{j}=\frac{3}{p}\deg(L_{u,v})

for any irreducible components
C_{v}\subset C
, allowing for the stabilisation map to lift to

(4.53)

\text{st}_{U_{i}}\colon U_{i}\to\mathcal{B}_{1+\#\Lambda+3d^{\prime}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}}(d_{\Lambda^{\prime}})

iii)

\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
-invariant functions
\chi_{i}\colon Z_{i}\to[0,1]
with support contained in
U_{i}

so that
\mathscr{Z}_{\Lambda^{\prime},\bar{\partial}}
is contained in the support of
\sum\limits{\chi_{i}}
.

Write
\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda^{r}}=\{(D_{i,j}^{r},U_{i}^{r},\chi_{i}^{r})\}_{i\in I^{r},j}
and choose for
i\in I^{0}\times I^{r}
an open
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}
-invariant subset
U_{i}
of
\mathscr{Z}_{\Lambda}
so that
U_{i}\cap\text{im}(\Psi)=\Psi(U_{i^{1}}\times U_{i^{0}})
and
U_{i}
does not intersect any other other boundary stratum. Shrinking
U_{i}
if neccessary, we may ensure that for any
1\leq j\leq 3d^{\prime}_{\Lambda^{r}}
the section
\sigma_{i^{r},j}^{r}
extends to a
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}
-invariant section
U_{i}\to\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{o}}|_{U_{i}}
with
u(\sigma_{i,j}^{r}(\varphi,u))\in D^{r}_{i^{r},j}
and
u\,\pitchfork\,D_{i,j}
near
\sigma_{i^{r},j}(\varphi,u)
for any
(\varphi,u)\in U_{i}
. This requires the divisor
D_{i^{r},j}
and the fact that transversality is an open condition. Note that we do not need the whole map
u
to intersect
D^{r}_{i^{r},j}
transversely.
In particular, this construction yields a
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}
-equivariant map
\,\text{st}_{U_{i}}\colon U_{i}\to\mathcal{B}^{\,\text{st}}_{1+\#\Lambda+3d^{\prime}_{\Lambda}}(d_{\Lambda})
so that

(4.54)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{i^{1}}^{1}\times_{S^{1}}U_{i^{0}}^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{B}^{\,\text{st}}_{1+\#\Lambda^{1}+3d^{\prime}_{\Lambda^{1}}}\times\mathscr{B}^{\text{st}}_{1+\#\Lambda^{0}+3d^{\prime}_{\Lambda^{0}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{B}^{\text{st}}_{1+\#\Lambda+3d^{\prime}_{\Lambda}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,\text{st}_{U_{i^{1}}^{1}}\times\,\text{st}_{U_{i^{0}}^{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\,\text{st}_{U_{i}}}

commutes. Now, we can use the Tietze extension theorem, applied to
\mathcal{Z}_{\Lambda}/\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}
, to extend the cut-off functions
\chi_{i^{1}}^{1}\times\chi^{0}_{i^{0}}
on
\Psi(U_{i^{1}}^{1}\times U_{i^{0}}^{0})^{\sim}
equivariantly to obtain invariant continuous functions
\chi^{\prime}_{i}\colon U_{i}\to[0,1]
. Since
A_{i}:=\mathcal{G}\cdot\text{supp}(\chi_{i^{1}}^{1}\times\chi^{0}_{i^{0}})
is closed in
\mathscr{Z}_{\Lambda}
and contained in
U_{i}
, we can use the Tietze extension theorem again to find a
\mathcal{G}
-invariant function
\rho_{i}\colon\mathscr{Z}_{\Lambda}\to[0,1]
which is identically
1
on
A_{i}
and supported in
U_{i}
. Thus, we can extend
\rho_{i}\chi^{\prime}_{i}
to a
\mathcal{G}
-invariant function
\chi_{i}
on all of
\mathcal{Z}_{\Lambda}
, extending the cut-off function on the boundary. Now, we can complete
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
to an invariant open cover
\mathcal{U}
of
\mathscr{Z}_{\Lambda,\bar{\partial}}
so that any
U\in\mathcal{U}\setminus\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
does not meet the images of the embeddings
\Psi_{i}
. This yields the desired good covering
\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda}
, so that
\zeta_{\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda}}
is an extension of the functions
\zeta_{01}
.

Step 2:
First, note that we can rephrase a perturbation space
(E,\mu)
as the trivial
G
-vector bundle
E\to\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{o}}\times\widehat{Y}
equipped with a
\mathbb{R}\times G
-equivariant vector bundle morphism
E\to\Lambda^{0,1*}_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{o}}/\mathcal{B}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}T\widehat{Y}
. Thus, we can extend the trivial
G_{\Lambda_{i}^{1}}\times G_{\Lambda^{0}_{i}}
-vector bundle
E_{\Lambda^{1}}\times_{S^{1}}E_{\Lambda^{0}}\to\mathscr{B}_{0}\times_{S^{1}}\mathscr{B}_{1}
to a
G_{\Lambda}
-vector bundle

E^{\prime}_{01}:=G_{\Lambda}\times_{G_{\Lambda_{i}^{1}}\times G_{\Lambda^{0}_{i}}}(E_{\Lambda^{1}}\times_{S^{1}}E_{\Lambda^{0}})

and we can extend
\mu_{\Lambda^{1}}\times\mu_{\Lambda^{0}}
uniquely to
G_{\Lambda}
-equivariant map. By
[
Las79
, Proposition 1.1]
, we can take the direct sum with a
G_{\Lambda}
-vector bundle
W\to\text{im}(\Psi)^{\sim}
, equipped with the zero map to
\Lambda^{0,1*}_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{o}}/\mathcal{B}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}T\widehat{Y}
, to obtain a
G_{\Lambda}
-perturbation space
(E_{01},\mu_{01})
for
\text{im}(\Psi)^{\sim}
that extends the perturbation space of the boundary stratum. Using the map of Lemma
4.28
and multiplying
\mu_{01}
with a suitable cut-off function, we can pull back this perturbation space to a perturbation space on the whole boundary stratum. Using a (sufficiently small) collar of
\text{im}(\Psi_{\Lambda^{0},\Lambda^{1}})^{\sim}
and a bump function, we can extend
(E_{01},\mu_{01})
to a perturbation space
(E_{01},\mu_{\Lambda,01})
on all of
\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}
. Since these perturbation spaces are sufficient to achieve transversality for maps in

\mathfrak{s}_{\text{pre}}^{-1}(0)=\{(\varphi,u)\in\mathcal{Z}_{\Lambda}\mid\zeta_{\mathcal{U}}(\varphi,u)=0\}

that lie near some boundary stratum, we can extend the direct sum
\bigoplus\limits_{\Gamma_{i}}(E_{01},\mu_{\Lambda,01})
to a perturbation space
(E_{\Lambda},\mu_{\Lambda})
for
\mathfrak{s}_{\text{pre}}^{-1}(0)
so that over a boundary stratum
(E_{\Lambda},\mu_{\Lambda})
is of the form
(E_{01},\mu_{01})\oplus(W,0)
. This yields the desired perturbation datum
\alpha=(\mathfrak{D},\mathcal{U},\zeta,E,\mu)
.

By definition (and
[
AMS24
, Lemma 3.5]
) the global chart
\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda}
is equivalent to
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}
. In particular, it is a global chart for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\text{SFT}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-})_{\Lambda}
. The embeddings (
4.2.2
) exist by our choice of perturbation space
E_{\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-}}
and they fit into a commutative square

(4.55)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{E}_{\Lambda^{0}}\overline{\times}_{B\Gamma}\,\mathscr{E}_{\Lambda^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{E}_{\Lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda^{0}}\overline{\times}_{B\Gamma}\,\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda^{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}}

This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.31
.

In the construction of the perturbation space, we have used a strategy of
[
Rez22
]
, instead of the construction in
[
BX22
]
. This allows us to see the embeddings of boundary strata immediately as strong equivalences, while
[
BX22
]
has to use an outer-collaring as well as some gluing results (
[
BX22
, Proposition 5.64]
).

It remains to prove the inductive step.

Proposition 4.32
.

There exists a system of global Kuranishi charts
\{\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda}\}_{\Lambda\colon\Gamma^{-}\to\Gamma^{+}}
for the moduli spaces
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\text{SFT}}^{J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-})_{\Lambda}
admitting strong equivalences

(4.56)

\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda^{0}}\overline{\times}_{B\Gamma}\,\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda^{1}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda}

onto the codimension-
1
boundary strata of
\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda}
for any factorization
\Lambda=\Lambda^{1}\circ\Lambda^{0}
so that any boundary stratum of
\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda}
is the image of (
4.50
) up to stabilization for some factorization and the squares

(4.57)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda^{0}}\overline{\times}_{B\Gamma_{0}}\,\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda^{1}}\overline{\times}_{B\Gamma_{1}}\,\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda^{0}}\overline{\times}_{B\Gamma_{0}}\,\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda^{21}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda^{10}}\overline{\times}_{B\Gamma_{1}}\,\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda}}

are pullback squares.

Proof.

The construction of the embeddings
\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda^{1}}\overline{\times}_{B\Gamma}\,\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda^{0}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}
of thickenings for respective partitions of Reeb orbits follows by induction, using the arguments of §
4.2.2
and Lemma
4.30
. The same holds for the embeddings of obstruction bundles. The additional difficulty in the general case is to ensure that our extensions of good coverings and perturbation spaces can be done compatibly over the corner strata. We first extend the maps of (
4.52
) from the boundary strata to the interior. Extending
\zeta_{\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{\prime}}
and
\zeta_{\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{+}}
and extending to
\mathcal{G}
-equivariant maps, we obtain
\mathcal{G}
-equivariant functions

\widetilde{\zeta}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\colon\partial_{\Lambda^{01}}{\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}}^{\,\text{st}}_{\Lambda}\,\longrightarrow\,\mathfrak{g}_{\Lambda}

that agree over the corner strata. By Lemma
4.33
, we may extend them to a
\mathcal{G}
-equivariant smooth function
\zeta^{\prime}\colon{\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}}^{\,\text{st}}_{\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-}}\to\mathfrak{g}
. By the inductive hypothesis, we assume that the good coverings of the codimension-
1
strata over their respective boundary strata are obtained from good coverings of the moduli spaces forming the codimension-
2
strata. Thus, we may use the same argument as in the discussion before the square (
4.54
) to extend the good coverings of the boundary strata to a good covering of a neighborhood of the boundary. In particular, the maps
\,\text{st}_{U_{i}}
and
\,\text{st}_{U_{j}}
agree over the intersection of the boundary with
U_{i}\cap U_{j}
. Thus, we may choose arbitrary extensions of
\mathcal{G}
-invariant bump functions and extend these data to a good covering as at the end of the proof of Lemma
4.30
.

To construct the perturbation space
(E_{\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-}},\mu_{\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-}})
, we observe that the beginning of the construction in the proof of Lemma
4.30
applied to the codimension-
1
boundary strata of
\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-}}
yields a
G
-representation
E^{\prime}_{\Lambda}
, equipped with a a map

\mu^{\partial}_{\Lambda}\colon C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{o}}|_{\partial\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-}}}\times\widehat{Y},\Lambda^{0,1*}_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{o}}/\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}T\widehat{Y})^{\mathbb{R}}.

whose restrictions to the closures of the codimension-
1
strata agree over the codimension-
2
strata. Thus, we can extend
\mu^{\partial}_{\Lambda}
to a
G_{\Lambda}
-equivariant map
\mu^{\prime}_{\Lambda}\colon E^{\prime}_{\Lambda}\to C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{o}}\times\widehat{Y},\Lambda^{0,1*}_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{o}}/\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}T\widehat{Y})^{\mathbb{R}}
by
[
Kot
]
. As this is sufficient to achieve transversality for curves with domain near the boundary, we may extend
(E^{\prime}_{\Lambda},\mu^{\prime}_{\Lambda})
to a perturbation datum, where
(E_{\Lambda},\mu_{\Lambda})=(E^{\prime}_{\Lambda},\mu^{\prime}_{\Lambda})\oplus(E^{\mathrm{o}},\mu^{\mathrm{o}})
, where
\mu^{\mathrm{o}}(e)
is supported away from
\mathcal{C}|_{\partial\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}}\times\widehat{Y}
.
This proves the first claim. Any boundary stratum is covered by such an embedding since any boundary stratum of
\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}
is a vector bundle over the image of some embedding
\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda^{1}}\times\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda^{0}}\to\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}
. The last assertion follows from Lemma
4.27
and the construction of the perturbation spaces.
∎

Lemma 4.33
.

Suppose
\mathcal{G}
acts properly on a smooth manifold
M
with corners and for each boundary stratum
S\subset\partial M
there exists a
\mathcal{G}
-equivariant function
f_{S}\colon S\to V
to a common finite-dimensional
\mathcal{G}
-representation so that the restrictions agree over the codimension-
2
corner strata. Then, there exists a
\mathcal{G}
-equivariant smooth function
f\colon M\to V
that extends the functions
f_{S}
.

Proof.

We first apply
[
Kot
]
to the functions
\{f_{S}\}
to obtain a smooth extension
\widetilde{f}\colon M\to V
. Averaging over
G
, we may assume
\widetilde{f}
to be
G
-invariant. By
[
Pal61
]
, we can find a locally finite open cover
\mathcal{U}
of
M
so that for each
U\in\mathcal{U}
there exists a
G
-invariant subset
S\subset U
with
U\cong\mathcal{G}\times_{G}S
. Moreover, we can find a
\mathcal{G}
-invariant partition of unity
\{\varsigma_{U}\}_{U}
subordinate to
\mathcal{U}
. Define
f_{U}\colon U\to V
by
f_{U}(g\cdot s)=g\cdot\widetilde{f}(s)
for
(g,s)\in\mathcal{G}\times S
and set
f:=\sum\limits_{U}{\varsigma_{U}\,f_{U}}
to obtain the desired extension.
∎

This completes the proof of Theorem
4.20
.

4.3.
Flow bimodules from symplectic cobordisms

In this subsection we show that exact symplectic cobordisms induce flow bimodules between the flow categories constructed in §
4.2
. Let
(\widehat{X},\omega)
be an exact symplectic cobordism from
(Y^{+},\lambda^{+})
to
(Y^{-},\lambda^{-})
, equipped with an
\omega
-adapted almost complex structure
J
. Suppose we are given action bounds
L^{\pm}\geq 0
and pre-perturbation data
\mathfrak{D}^{\pm}=(\widetilde{\lambda}^{\pm},\nabla^{\pm},p^{\pm})
for
\mathcal{P}_{L^{\pm}}
, where

•

\widetilde{\lambda}^{\pm}
is a
\mathcal{P}_{L^{\pm}}(Y^{\pm})
-integral approximations of
\lambda^{\pm}
,

•

\widehat{\nabla}^{\pm}
are complex linear connections on
\widehat{X}
and
\widehat{Y}^{\pm}
, respectively, such that
\nabla_{o}
restricts to
\widehat{\nabla}^{\pm}
on the corresponding end and the connections on
Y^{\pm}
are translation invariant;

•

prime numbers
p^{+},p^{-}
satisfying (
3.54
).

Theorem 4.34
.

Given a pre-perturbation datum
\mathfrak{D}
extending
\mathfrak{D}^{\pm}
, there exists a flow bimodule
\mathscr{N}^{\widehat{X}}
from the flow category
\mathscr{M}_{\leq L^{-}}^{Y^{-}}
to the flow category
\mathscr{M}_{\leq L^{+}}^{Y^{+}}
. The morphism space from an object
\Gamma^{-}\in\mathcal{P}_{L^{-}}(Y^{-})
to
\Gamma^{+}\in\mathcal{P}_{L^{+}}(Y^{+})
is

\mathscr{N}^{\widehat{X}}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})=\bigsqcup\limits_{\Lambda}\,\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\text{SFT}}^{\widehat{X},\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)_{\Lambda}

respectively, a global Kuranishi chart of said moduli space.

Proof.

We only sketch the construction of
\mathscr{N}^{\widehat{X}}
because of its similarity to the construction of the flow category in
4.2
. There is a natural extension of the definition of the ‘norm’ of
\|(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-})\|
for
\Gamma^{\pm}\in\mathcal{P}_{L^{\pm}}(Y^{\pm})
given by the height of the tallest building in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\text{SFT}}^{\widehat{X},\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)_{\Lambda}
(if it were unobstructed). The inductive construction of perturbation data for the moduli spaces begins similarly as before. By the choice of perturbation datum
\mathfrak{D}^{\pm}
and the constructions of §
4.2
, we are given global Kuranishi charts for the morphism spaces between objects in the same symplectization. Hence, we only need to construct charts for the moduli of buildings from an object of
\mathscr{M}_{\leq L^{-}}^{Y^{-}}
to an object of
\mathscr{M}_{\leq L^{+}}^{Y^{+}}
so that its boundary strata are compatible with the already chosen charts in the construction of
\mathscr{M}_{\leq L^{\pm}}^{Y^{\pm},\lambda^{\pm}}.

We do this inductively as well. However, both due to the formalism and due to the geometry of the moduli spaces, we have to make an artificial choice: we let

\mathscr{N}^{\widehat{X}}(\emptyset_{Y^{+}},\emptyset_{Y_{-}})\coloneqq*

be the trivial global Kuranishi chart for a point. This choice is forced on us due to the following phenomenon in cobordisms: there can be a family of holomorphic planes in
\widehat{X}
that escape off to
\widehat{Y}^{+}
, resulting in a two-leveled building, which has a holomorphic plane in
\widehat{Y}^{+}
and an empty level in the symplectization. This phenomenon can occur whenever curves have no negative punctures.

Given a partition
\Lambda\colon\Gamma^{+}\to\Gamma^{-}
of norm
0
, extend
\mathfrak{D}
to an arbitrary perturbation datum
\alpha_{\Gamma^{\pm}}
for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\widehat{X},\,J}_{\text{SFT}}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-})_{\Lambda}
as in Definition
3.28
. Let

\mathscr{K}_{\Lambda}^{c}=\mathscr{K}^{c}_{\alpha_{\Lambda}}=(\widehat{G}_{\Lambda},\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda},\mathscr{E}_{\Lambda},\mathfrak{s}_{\Lambda})

be the associated global Kuranishi chart for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\text{SFT}}^{\widehat{X},\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)_{\Lambda}
as in Lemma
4.30
, obtained from the chart
\mathcal{K}_{\alpha_{\Lambda}}
of Proposition
3.65
. The proofs of the counterparts of Propositions
4.26
and
4.27
are the same except for the added notational complexity required to keep track of the targets.

An important observation is that the category of trees that stratifies
\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{c}
naturally carries the information of an order
\mathrm{d}_{e}
for every edge
e
, obtained from Definitions
3.49
and
3.1
. The other change one has to make is that the concatenation
T_{e}\#T_{c}
, of a leveled forest
T_{e}
labeling a stratum in
\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}
with a leveled forest
T_{c}
labeling a stratum in
\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{c}
, is a leveled forest, which labels a stratum in
\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{c}
. For the cobordism counterparts of Lemma
4.30
and Proposition
4.32
, the only difference lies in the construction of the embedding maps between thickenings; see §
4.2.2
. While choosing finite-dimensional approximation scheme
E_{*}
, we use Lemma
3.64
to obtain a joint finite-dimensional approximation scheme. The rest of the construction follows similarly.
∎

An important bimodule from a flow category
\mathscr{X}
to itself is the
diagonal bimodule

\Delta_{\mathscr{X}}
, which can be thought of as the identity morphism. It has objects given by two copies
\mathcal{P}^{\pm}
of the symmetric sets
\mathcal{P}
of objects of
\mathscr{X}
and morphisms given by

(4.58)

\Delta_{\mathscr{X}}(x,y)=\begin{cases}\mathcal{D}\mathscr{X}(x,y)\quad&x\neq y\\
*\quad&x=y,\end{cases}

where
\mathcal{D}X
is the conic degeneration of an orbifold,
[
AB24
, §6.1]
. It comes with a natural map
d\colon\mathcal{D}X\to X
, and we write
\mathcal{D}\mathcal{K}=(\mathcal{D}\mathcal{T},d^{*}\mathcal{E},d^{*}\mathfrak{s})
for the conic degeneration of a derived orbifold. In particular,
\Delta_{\mathscr{X}}(x,y)
defines an object of the category
\text{dOrb}_{/\cdot}
defined in Definition
4.7

Lemma 4.35
.

If
(\widehat{X},\omega)=(\widehat{Y},d(e^{s}\lambda))
is the trivial cobordism equipped with the almost complex structure
J
, then
\mathscr{N}_{\leq L}^{\widehat{X}}
of Theorem
4.34
is equivalent to the diagonal bimodule.

Proof.

We will use slightly different perturbation data to construct the flow bimodule in this case. The proof of equivalence of global Kuranishi charts in
[
HS24b
, Proposition 6.1]
then shows that the associated flow bimodule is equivalent to the one constructed in Proposition
4.34
. Here we say two flow bimodules are equivalent if they have the same objects and their morphism spaces are equivalent compatibly with structure maps.

Let
\mathfrak{D}
be the pre-perturbation datum chosen for the construction of
\mathscr{M}_{\leq L}^{Y}
and let
\{\alpha_{\Lambda}\}_{\Lambda}
be the collection of perturbation data constructed inductively in §
4.2
. Then,
\mathfrak{D}
is also a pre-perturbation datum for the symplectic cobordism
\widehat{X}
and
\widetilde{\alpha}_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\alpha_{\Lambda}
defines a perturbation datum for the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{J}_{\text{SFT}}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-})
of buildings in
\widehat{X}
, denoted by
\overline{\mathcal{N}}
instead of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}
in order to distinguish it from moduli spaces of buildings in the symplectisation. By definition,
\mathscr{N}^{\widehat{X}}(\emptyset,\emptyset)
is a point, while for a nonempty sequence
\Gamma
, the moduli space of trivial cylinders in
\widehat{X}
is regular and a point, whence
\mathscr{N}^{\widehat{X}}(\Gamma,\Gamma)=*
as well. Suppose
\Gamma^{-}\neq\Gamma^{+}
and let
\Lambda\colon\Gamma^{-}\to\Gamma^{+}
be a partition. By Lemma
4.41
, the thickening
\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda}
of the global Kuranishi chart
\mathscr{K}^{c}_{\Lambda}
for
\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})_{\Lambda}
admits a canonical equivariant rel–
C^{1}
map
q\colon\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda}\to\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}
, which is a fiber bundle of intervals. Moreover,
\mathscr{E}^{c}_{\Lambda}=q^{*}\mathscr{E}_{\Lambda}
and the obstruction section is pulled back as well. Since
\text{Homeo}_{+}([0,1])
is contractible, one can lift
q
to a homeomorphism
\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda}\to\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}\times[0,1]
. Using the explicit description of the composition maps, this shows that the forgetful map
\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda}\to\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}
factors through the conic degeneration
\mathcal{D}\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}
of
\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}
and that
\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda}=\mathcal{D}\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}\times_{\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}}\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}
. Since the map
\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda}\to\mathcal{D}\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}
is compatible with the bimodule structure maps, the claim follows.
∎

4.4.
Stable complex structures

In this subsection we show that the flow categories of Theorem
B
admit stable complex structures. Recall that we fixed a nondegenerate contact manifold
(Y,\lambda)
, a
\lambda
-adapted almost complex structure
J
, a real number
L>0
. Given this, let
\mathscr{M}^{Y}_{\leq L}
the flow category of Theorem
B
.

Theorem 4.36
.

The symmetric flow category
\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{\leq L}
admits a lift to a stably complex symmetric flow category.

The proof is similar to
[
AB21
, §11.3]
and
[
AB24
, §B]
. We abbreviate
\mathscr{M}\coloneqq\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{\leq L}
. The morphism space is the disjoint union

\mathscr{M}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})\;=\;\bigsqcup\limits_{\Lambda}\,\mathscr{M}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})_{\Lambda}

over all functions
\Lambda\colon\Gamma^{-}\to\Gamma^{+}
. We will phrase every statement in terms of these partitions as we have done in §
4.2
. The tangent bundle of the global Kuranishi chart satisfies

(4.59)

T^{+}\mathscr{M}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\Lambda}=\;T\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,T^{v}\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}

while the obstruction bundle is given by

(4.60)

T^{-}\mathscr{M}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})_{\Lambda}\,=\;E_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda}\,

where
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\Lambda}
is the Lie algebra of the covering group
\widehat{G}_{\Lambda}
,
\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda}
is the Lie algebra of the product
\prod\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\text{PU}(d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}+1)}
of projective unitary groups, and
E_{\Lambda}
is a finite-rank
G
-vector bundle.

In §
4.4.1
, we define the lift of the objects
\Gamma^{+}
to objects of a stably complex flow category. Subsequently, we construct the stable complex structures on the morphism spaces in §
4.4.2
and §
4.4.3
, summarizing the results in Proposition
4.45
.

4.4.1.
Lift of the objects

Recall that the objects of a lift of
\mathscr{M}
to a stably complex flow category
\mathscr{M}^{U}
consist of a finite sequence
\Gamma
of Reeb orbits of action at most
L
and a virtual vector space
V_{\Gamma}=(V^{+}_{\Gamma},V^{-}_{\Gamma})
. We will construct for each Reeb orbit
\gamma
of
\lambda
a virtual
S^{1}
-vector bundle
V_{\gamma}
over the
S^{1}
-manifold
E\gamma
defined in (
4.40
).Then, we define

V_{\Gamma}\coloneqq\prod\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma}{V_{\gamma}}\to B\Gamma

to be the product vector bundle.

Remark 4.37
.

If
\gamma
is a good Reeb orbit, this virtual vector bundle is orientable. It is non-orientable otherwise.

Recall that the pre-perturbation datum
\mathfrak{D}
we chose for the construction of
\mathscr{M}
includes a choice of
J
-linear connection
\nabla^{Y}
on
T\widehat{Y}=\xi\oplus\mathbb{C}
. While the construction of global Kuranishi charts does not require any properties of
\nabla^{Y}
except linearity with respect to
J
, for the following constructions it will be useful to assume that
\nabla^{Y}
has trivial monodromy around any simple Reeb orbit of action at most
L
.
Moreover, fix a smooth cutoff function
\chi
on
\mathbb{R}
with

(4.61)

\chi(s)=\begin{cases}1\quad&s\ll 0\\
0\quad&s\gg 0.\end{cases}

Given a Reeb orbit
\gamma
and a parametrization
\widetilde{\gamma}\in E\gamma
, let
c_{\widetilde{\gamma}}\colon\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}\to\widehat{Y}
be the trivial cylinder over
\widetilde{\gamma}
. Then, the pullback
c_{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{*}T\widehat{Y}=c_{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{*}\xi\oplus\mathbb{C}
, equipped with the chosen almost complex structure
J
, is a complex vector bundle over
\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}
. It carries two canonical connections: the pullback of
\nabla^{Y}
, which is complex linear, and the connection
\nabla^{\prime}
induced by the pullback of
\mathcal{L}_{R_{\lambda}}
on
\xi
and the trivial connection on
\mathbb{C}
. We define the connection

(4.62)

\nabla^{\widetilde{\gamma}}\coloneqq\nabla^{Y}+\chi(s)(\nabla^{\prime}-\nabla^{Y})

on
\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}
and let
\bar{\partial}^{\widetilde{\gamma}}=(\nabla^{\widetilde{\gamma}})^{0,1}
be the associated real Cauchy–Riemann operator. Since
c_{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{*}\xi\oplus\mathbb{C}
is trivializable,
(c_{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{*}\xi\oplus\mathbb{C},\bar{\partial}^{\widetilde{\gamma}})
extends uniquely to a Cauchy–Riemann problem
(\mathcal{V}_{\widetilde{\gamma}},\bar{\partial}^{\widetilde{\gamma}})
on the capping off of
\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}
at the
positive
end, using the trivialization induced by the connection
\nabla^{Y}
.
6
6

6

We cap off at the positive instead of the negative end in order to obtain formulas compatible with the conventions in
[
AB24
]
. This is for the same reason that we define
\mathscr{M}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})
to be
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J}_{\text{SFT}}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-})
.
As all data that depend on the parametrization
\widetilde{\gamma}
depend smoothly on it, we obtain a smooth
S^{1}
-vector bundle

\mathcal{V}\to E\gamma

with an
S^{1}
-invariant family
\bar{\partial}^{\gamma}=\{\bar{\partial}^{\widetilde{\gamma}}\}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}
of Cauchy–Riemann operators. Fix an element
\widetilde{\gamma}\in E\gamma
and choose a finite-dimensional complex representation

(4.63)

\nu_{0}\colon W^{\prime}\to\Omega^{0,1}_{c}(\mathbb{R}\times S^{1},c_{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{*}T\widehat{Y})

that is invariant under the isotropy of
\widetilde{\gamma}
and is sufficiently large so that
\bar{\partial}^{\widetilde{\gamma}}\,\oplus\,\nu_{0}
is surjective. We require that

(4.64)

\text{supp}(\nu_{0}(w))\subset[-1,1]\times S^{1}

for each
w\in W^{\prime}
. Using the
S^{1}
-equivariance of
\bar{\partial}^{\gamma}
and the transitivity of the
S^{1}
-action on
E\gamma
, we obtain a finite-rank
S^{1}
-vector bundle
W^{\prime}_{\gamma}\to E\gamma
with fiber-wise trivial
S^{1}
-action and a linear
S^{1}
-equivariant map
\nu
from
W^{\prime}_{\gamma}
to the bundle with fibers
\Omega^{0,1}(\mathbb{R}\times S^{1},c_{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{*}\xi)
which surjects onto the fiber-wise cokernel of
\bar{\partial}^{\gamma}
. Then, we define the vector bundles

(4.65)

V_{\gamma}^{+}=\ker(\bar{\partial}^{\gamma}+\nu)\qquad\qquad V^{-}_{\gamma}=W^{\prime}_{\gamma}.

4.4.2.
Stable complex structures on base spaces

This is essentially a more complicated version of
[
AB24
]
since our base spaces go through an additional generalized blow-up compared to those in
[
AB24
]
.

Lemma 4.38
.

The generalized blow-up of an almost complex manifold, equipped with the canonical smooth structure, admits a canonical almost complex structure up to contractible choice.

Proof.

We will show that the generalized blow-up
\widetilde{M}\xrightarrow{\beta}M
of a smooth manifold
M
with corners comes equipped with a bundle isomorphism
T\widetilde{M}\to\beta^{*}TM
. The choice of this isomorphism is canonical up to a contractible set. In particular, if
M
is almost complex, we can lift its almost complex structure to the generalized blow-up uniquely up to a contractible choice. Moreover, it follows from the argument in
[
AB24
, §B.3.1]
and Remark
3.8
that once the bundle isomorphism is determined on the exceptional boundary locus, then it can be extended up to a contractible choice using bump functions. Hence, it suffices to construct such an isomorphism over the blow-up locus.
For simplicity, we assume that
\widetilde{M}
is generalized blow-up of the corner

C=\bigcap\limits_{i=1}^{\ell+1}B_{i},

where
B_{1},\dots,B_{\ell+1}
are boundary faces of
M
. Denote the exceptional boundary face
\beta^{-1}(C)
by
E
. The construction begins with choosing any two metrics
\widetilde{g},g
on
\widetilde{M}
and
M
respectively. From hereon in the proof, we use canonical to mean
canonical for a fixed pair
\widetilde{g},g
.
A metric on
M
determines a trivialization of the normal bundles
N_{i}\to B_{i}
. This in turn yields a canonical splitting

(4.66)

TM|_{C}\cong TC\oplus\bigoplus N_{i}

and we write
n_{i}
for the inward-pointing unit normal vector in
N_{i}
.
By construction of the generalized blow-up, we have a canonical isomorphism
E\,\cong\,\mathbb{P}_{+}(\bigoplus_{i}N_{i})
to the positive part of the spherical projectivisation of the normal bundle at
C
, defined in Equation
3.6
. Using the canonical trivialization of
N_{i}
, this shows that
E\cong C\times\Delta^{\ell}
where
\Delta^{\ell}
is identified with the intersection of
S^{\ell}\cap[0,\infty)^{\ell+1}
. Thus, the pair
(g,\widetilde{g})
yields a canonical splitting

T\widetilde{M}|_{E}\;\cong\;N_{E/\widetilde{M}}\,\oplus\,\text{pr}_{C}^{*}TC\,\oplus\,\text{pr}_{\Delta}^{*}T\Delta^{\ell},

with
\ker(d\beta)=\text{pr}_{\Delta}^{*}T\Delta^{\ell}
. In particular,
d\beta
restricts to an isomorphism

(\text{pr}_{\Delta}^{*}T\Delta^{\ell}|_{C\times\star})^{\perp}\,\xlongrightarrow{\simeq}\,\beta^{*}(TC\oplus N_{1})|_{C\times\star},

where
\star=(1,0,\dots 0)\in\Delta^{\ell}
. The tangent space
T_{\star}\Delta^{\ell}
is canonically identified with
\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
in
\mathbb{R}^{\ell+1}
and we write
e_{1},\dots,e_{\ell}
for the standard basis of
\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
.
Fix an isomorphism

\Phi\colon\text{pr}_{\Delta}^{*}T\Delta^{\ell}|_{C\times\star}\to\text{im}(d\beta|_{E})^{\perp}

such that
\Phi(c,\star,e_{i})=n_{i+1}(c)
for
i=1,\dots,\ell
under the isomorphism (
4.66
). The space of such isomorphisms is a contractible space. Therefore, the bundle isomorphism

(4.67)

T\widetilde{M}|_{E}\;\xrightarrow{\simeq}\;N_{\widetilde{M}/E}\oplus\text{pr}_{C}^{*}TC\oplus\text{pr}_{\Delta}^{*}T\Delta^{\ell}\;\xlongrightarrow{\beta_{*}\oplus\beta_{*}\oplus\Phi}\;\beta^{*}TM

is canonical up to contractible choice.
∎

Remark 4.39
.

In the case of a real-oriented blow-up
\text{Bl}_{D}(X)
, we do not require the choice of a metric on
X
to be able to pull back an almost complex structure. Then, the preimage
E
of
D
is canonically isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}_{>0}(N_{D/X})
, whence we have a free
S^{1}
-action on
E
. Thus, a metric on
\text{Bl}_{D}(X)
gives us a decomposition

T\text{Bl}_{D}(X)|_{E}\cong\beta^{*}TD\oplus\mathbb{R}\oplus L,

where
L
is the canonical line of
\mathbb{P}_{>0}(N_{D/X})
. On
\beta^{*}TD
we have a canonical complex structure
J_{D}
and we extend it to
J
on
T\text{Bl}_{D}(X)|_{E}
by mapping the unit section of
L
to the unit vector of
\mathbb{R}
(corresponding to the canonical vector field of the action).

We are now going to apply this to the base space of the topological flow category
\mathscr{M}
. Given a smooth manifold
M
with the action of a compact Lie group
G
, it will be useful to write
T\overline{M}
for the virtual vector bundle
TM-\mathfrak{g}
, where
\mathfrak{g}
is the Lie algebra of
M
. In the result below we will not explicitly indicate the quotient by the group action by any tangent bundle should be considered in that sense.

Lemma 4.40
.

For any
\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+}\in\mathcal{P}_{\leq L}
and any partition
\Lambda\colon\Gamma^{-}\to\Gamma^{+}
of
\Gamma^{-}
, there exists

1)

a complex
\widehat{G}_{\Lambda}
-vector bundle
I^{b}_{\Lambda}\to\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}

2)

an equivalence

(4.68)

T\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,\mathbb{R}\;\simeq\;I^{b}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,\mathbb{R}^{\Gamma^{+}}

of
\widehat{G}_{\Lambda}
-equivariant virtual vector bundles on
\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}
.

3)

for any factorization
\Gamma^{-}\xrightarrow{\Lambda^{0}}\Gamma\xrightarrow{\Lambda^{1}}\Gamma^{+}
of
\Lambda
a split equivariant embedding

(4.69)

I^{b}_{\Lambda^{0}}\,\oplus I^{b}_{\Lambda^{1}}\,\to\,I^{b}_{\Lambda}

of complex equivariant vector bundles over
\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{0}}\underset{\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma}}{\times}\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{1}}
.

They satisfy the following compatibility conditions.

•

The diagram

(4.70)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{b}_{\Lambda^{0}}\,\oplus\mathbb{R}^{\Gamma}\,\oplus I^{b}_{\Lambda^{1}}\,\oplus\mathbb{R}^{\Gamma^{+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{b}_{\Lambda}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{\Gamma}\,\oplus\mathbb{R}^{\Gamma^{+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{0}}\,\oplus\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda^{0}}\,\oplus\mathbb{R}_{\Lambda^{0}}\,\oplus T\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{1}}\,\oplus\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda^{1}}\,\oplus\mathbb{R}_{\Lambda^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{t}_{\Gamma}\,\oplus T\overline{\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{0}}\underset{\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma}}{\times}\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{1}}}\,\oplus\mathbb{R}_{\Lambda^{0}}\,\oplus\mathbb{R}_{\Lambda^{1}}\,\oplus\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda^{0}}\,\oplus\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{t}_{\Gamma}\,\oplus T\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda}\oplus\mathbb{R}_{\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

commutes, where
\mathfrak{t}_{\Gamma}
is identified canonically with
\mathbb{R}^{\Gamma}
,
\mathbb{R}_{\Lambda^{1}}
is identified with
\mathbb{R}_{\Lambda}
, and
