\mathbb{R}_{\Lambda^{0}}
is identified with the normal vector of the boundary stratum of
\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}
. Moreover, we identify
\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda}
with
\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda^{0}}\oplus\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda^{1}}\oplus\frac{\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda}}{\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda^{0}}\oplus\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda^{1}}}
and the latter summand with the normal bundle of the image of
\overline{\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{0}}\underset{\,\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma}}{\times}\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{1}}}
in
\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}
using Lemma
4.28
.

•

For any factorization
\Lambda=\Lambda^{2}\circ\Lambda^{1}\circ\Lambda^{0}
the square

(4.71)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{b}_{\Lambda^{0}}\,\oplus I^{b}_{\Lambda^{1}}\,\oplus I^{b}_{\Lambda^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{b}_{\Lambda_{0}}\,\oplus I^{b}_{\Lambda^{12}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{b}_{\Lambda^{01}}\,\oplus I^{b}_{\Lambda^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{b}_{\Lambda}}

over
\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{0}}\underset{\;\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma}}{\times}\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{1}}\underset{\;\;\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}}{\times}\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{2}}
commutes.

Proof.

Recall that the base space of
\mathscr{M}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})_{\Lambda}
is the manifold
\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}
equipped with an action of
\widehat{G}_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{-}}\times\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{-}}\times G_{\Lambda}
, where
\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma}:=(S^{1})^{\Gamma}
and
G_{\Lambda}=\prod\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{U(d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}})}
. In particular, it carries the structure of a principal
\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{-}}\times\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma^{-}}
-bundle
\pi_{\Lambda}\colon\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}\to\underline{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}.
The map
\pi_{\Lambda}
is given by forgetting the asymptotic markers. The space
\underline{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}
is a stratum of the (corner) blow-up of
\underline{\mathscr{B}}^{P}_{\Lambda}\times[0,1)^{\Gamma^{+}}
, where
\underline{\mathscr{B}}^{P}_{\Lambda}
is a real oriented blow-up of the complex manifold

\mathscr{B}(\Lambda)\subset\prod\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,\gamma\sqcup\Lambda_{\gamma}}(\mathbb{P}^{d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}},d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}})_{\varphi(z_{\gamma})=[1:0:\dots:0]}}.

Given any factorization
\Lambda=\Lambda^{1}\circ\Lambda^{0}
, we have an embedding

(4.72)

\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{0}}\underset{\;\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma}}{\times}\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda^{1}}\to\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}

covering

(4.73)

\mathscr{B}^{P}_{\Lambda^{0}}\underset{\;\mathbb{T}_{\Gamma}}{\times}\mathscr{B}^{P}_{\Lambda^{1}}\to\mathscr{B}^{P}_{\Lambda},

both constructed in §
4.2.1
. Using
[
Kot
]
, we can construct systems
\{\widetilde{g}_{\Lambda}\}_{\Lambda}
and
\{g_{\Lambda}\}_{\Lambda}
of invariant Riemannian metrics on
\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}
and
\mathscr{B}^{P}_{\Lambda}
, respectively, so that the embeddings (
4.72
) and (
4.73
) are isometric. By Remark
4.39
, the metric
g_{\Lambda}
induces a complex structure on
T\underline{\mathscr{B}}^{P}_{\Lambda}
. Using
\widetilde{g}_{\Lambda}
and
g_{\Lambda}
in Lemma
4.38
, we get a decomposition

T\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}\;\cong\;T\underline{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}\oplus\mathfrak{t}_{\Gamma^{+}}\,\oplus\,\mathfrak{t}_{\Gamma^{-}}\;\cong\;T\Delta^{|\Gamma^{+}|-1}\,\oplus\,\beta^{*}T\underline{\mathscr{B}}^{P}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,\mathfrak{t}_{\Gamma^{+}}\,\oplus\,\mathfrak{t}_{\Gamma^{-}}

Identifying
T\Delta^{k-1}
with
\mathbb{R}^{k}/\mathbb{R}
, this shows that

(4.74)

T\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,\mathbb{R}\;\cong\;T\mathbb{R}^{\Gamma^{+}}\,\oplus\,\beta^{*}T\underline{\mathscr{B}}^{P}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,\mathfrak{t}_{\Gamma^{+}}\,\oplus\,\mathfrak{t}_{\Gamma^{-}}

canonically. Thus, we can set
I^{b,+}_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\beta^{*}T\underline{\mathscr{B}}^{P}_{\Lambda}
. The decomposition (
4.74
) can be further transformed to

(4.75)

T\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,\mathfrak{g}\mathfrak{l}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,\mathbb{R}\;\cong\;T\mathbb{R}^{\Gamma^{+}}\,\oplus\,\beta^{*}T\underline{\mathscr{B}}^{P}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda}

where
\mathfrak{g}\mathfrak{l}_{\Lambda}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:comparing-group-quotients}}}{{=}}\mathbf{i}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda}\oplus\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda}
is the Lie algebra of the complex Lie group
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}
. Therefore, we can set

I^{b,-}_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\mathfrak{g}\mathfrak{l}_{\Lambda}.

The maps
I^{b,-}_{\Lambda^{0}}\,\oplus I^{b,-}_{\Lambda^{1}}\to I^{b,-}_{\Lambda}
are induced by the block matrix inclusions
\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda^{0}}\times\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda^{1}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{\Lambda}
, whence their compatibility in the sense of the square (
4.71
) is immediate. Meanwhile, the embeddings
I^{b,+}_{\Lambda^{0}}\,\oplus I^{b,+}_{\Lambda^{1}}\to I^{b,+}_{\Lambda}
come from the embeddings (
4.30
) of base spaces. The commutativity of (
4.71
) follows in their case from the inductive choice of Riemannian metrics. Since the vertical maps in Diagram (
4.70
) are defined using these compatible metrics, the commutativity of this diagram follows as well.
∎

4.4.3.
Stable complex structures on vertical tangent bundle

We will first prove the existence of stable complex structures on each morphism space
\mathscr{M}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})
separately before considering their compatibilities. Our strategy is similar to the one of
[
AB21
, §11.3.4]
. The idea is to construct a homotopy equivalence
\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}\to\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}
with two sections
a
and
b
and a family of Cauchy–Riemann operators over
\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}
whose restrictions to
\text{im}(a)
and
\text{im}(b)
are given by

\bar{\partial}^{\gamma}\,\#\,D\bar{\partial}_{J}\qquad\text{and}\qquad D^{\mathbb{C}}\,\#\,(\#_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\gamma}}\bar{\partial}^{\gamma^{\prime}}),

respectively, where
D^{\mathbb{C}}
is a (non-canonical) complex-linear Cauchy–Riemann operator.

Notation
.

Given a partition
\Lambda\colon\Gamma^{-}\to\Gamma^{+}
, we write
S=S_{\Lambda}
for the set of Reeb orbits
\gamma
(counted with multiplicities) so that
\gamma\in\Gamma\setminus\text{im}(\Lambda^{0})
for some factorization
\Gamma^{-}\xrightarrow{\Lambda^{0}}\Gamma\xrightarrow{\Lambda^{1}}\to\Gamma^{+}
of
\Lambda
. Note that we allow
\Lambda^{1}=\text{id}_{\Gamma^{+}}
.

Lemma 4.41
.

There exists for each function
\Lambda\colon\Gamma^{-}\to\Gamma^{+}
, there exists a
G_{\Lambda}
-equivariant fiber bundle

q\colon\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}^{c}\to\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}

with fibers given by
[0,1]
. It admits two equivariant sections
a_{\infty}
and
a_{0}
.

Proof.

Recall that we chose a pre-perturbation datum
\mathfrak{D}=(\widetilde{\lambda},\nabla,p)
for the construction of
\mathscr{M}
. We observe first that we can construct the global Kuranishi chart for buildings
\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})_{\Lambda}
in the trivial symplectic cobordism using the
\mathfrak{D}
. In this case, the base space agrees with the base space
\mathscr{B}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}
of the global Kuranishi chart for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,J}_{\text{SFT}}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})_{\Lambda}
, but the thickening is defined using the family
\mathcal{Z}^{c}_{\lambda}
in Definition
3.53
instead of Definition
3.20
. There exists a canonical rel–
C^{1}
submersion

(4.76)

\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda}\;\to\;\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda},

which is the identity on the level of base spaces. Its fibers are canonically identified with
[0,1]
. The map
\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda}\to\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}
has two canonical sections

a_{0}\colon\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{-})\overline{\times}_{B\Gamma^{-}}\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda}\qquad\quad a_{\infty}\colon\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}\overline{\times}_{B\Gamma^{+}}\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{+})\times\hookrightarrow\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda},

given by adding trivial cylinders (in the cobordism) at the positive puncture or at the negative one. If the compactified domain is given by the sphere in the middle of Figure
7
, then the section
a_{\infty}
is given by the configuration on the left, while
a_{0}
is given by the right configuration on the right hand side:

Figure 7.

Here the black spheres are those components that are mapped to the symplectic cobordism (being trivial cylinders in the left and right configuration), while the gray components are mapped to the symplectisation. The point labeled by
\infty
is the positive puncture, while all other marked points correspond to negative punctures.
∎

Fix a Riemannian metric
h
on
\widehat{Y}
and let
B_{r}(\gamma)
be the ball of radius
r>0
around a Reeb orbit
\gamma
. Fix some number
\delta>0
that is less than the injectivity radius of
h
. Let
d
be the associated distance and
\Phi
be the parallel transport of
h
and write
\Phi_{x\to y}
for parallel transport along the unique geodesic connecting
x
to
y
, whenever
d(x,y)
is sufficiently small.

Proposition 4.42
.

There exists a principal
G^{\prime}_{\Lambda}
-bundle
\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}\to\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda}
, which admits a
G^{\prime}_{\Lambda}\times\widehat{G}_{\Lambda}
-vector bundle
\mathcal{W}\to\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}
, a vector bundle
W^{v}_{\Lambda}
, and a complex virtual vector bundle
I^{v}_{\Lambda}
so that

(4.77)

a_{\infty}^{*}\mathcal{W}\,\cong\,T^{v}\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,\mathbb{R}^{\Gamma^{+}}\,\oplus\,V^{+}_{\Gamma^{+}}\,\oplus\,V^{-}_{\Gamma^{-}}\,\oplus I^{v,-}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,W^{v}_{\Lambda}

and

(4.78)

a_{0}^{*}\mathcal{W}\,\cong\,E_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,I^{v,+}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,V_{\Gamma^{-}}^{+}\,\oplus\,V_{\Gamma^{+}}^{-}\,\oplus\,W^{v}_{\Lambda},

where
a_{\infty}
and
a_{0}
are the sections of Lemma
4.41
.

Proof.

Let
p^{\prime}\gg p
be a large prime number, and let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{D}}=(\widetilde{\lambda},\nabla,p^{\prime},p)
be the pre-perturbation datum for the moduli spaces of buildings in the trivial symplectic cobordism. Then, we can construct the global Kuranishi chart for
\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})
using the base space
\mathscr{B}^{c}_{\Lambda}
defined in §
3.5.1
. Let

\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Lambda}\subset\prod\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,\gamma\sqcup\Lambda_{\gamma}}(\mathbb{P}^{d^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}}\times\mathbb{P}^{d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}},(d^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{\gamma}},d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}))_{z_{\gamma}\mapsto(e_{0},e_{0})}}

be the preimage of
\mathscr{B}^{c}_{\Lambda}\times\mathscr{B}_{\Lambda}
. The argument of
[
HS24b
, Lemma 7.3]
shows that
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Lambda}
is unobstructed, thus a complex manifold with a complex
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\Lambda}
-action. We can thus define

(4.79)

\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{\Lambda}=\mathscr{B}^{c}_{\Lambda}\times_{\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}}\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\Lambda}.

The only difference between
\mathscr{B}^{c}_{\Lambda}
and
\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{\Lambda}
is that framings in the former are constant on the domains of trivial cylinders, while those in
\mathscr{B}^{c}_{\Lambda}
are not (they have degree
(p^{\prime}-p)\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}(\gamma)
). Thus, the universal family
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda}\to\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{\Lambda}
allows us to access these domains. In order to lift this to the thickenings, we fix a map
\zeta^{\prime}_{\mathcal{U}}
as in (
4.45
) that determines the unitary framings in
\mathscr{B}^{c}_{\Lambda}
and set

(4.80)

\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\left\{(\widetilde{\varphi},\varphi,u,w)\in\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{\Lambda}\times_{\mathscr{B}^{c}_{\Lambda}}\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda}\mid T_{\widetilde{\varphi}}=T_{u}\,\zeta^{\prime}_{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{\varphi},u)=0\right\}.

The conditions we impose are open in the fiber product, so this is a rel–
C^{1}
manifold with corners. Moreover, the canonical forgetful map
\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}\to\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda}
is a principal
G^{\prime}_{\Lambda}
-bundle, where
G^{\prime}_{\Lambda}
is a product of unitary groups. Let
\mathcal{C}^{sc}\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{o}}
be the locus of points that are mapped to the symplectic cobordism. Then, the height functions of the respective map give us a rel–
C^{1}
function

(4.81)

\text{ev}_{\mathbb{R}}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{sc}\to\mathbb{R},

which extends to a continuous function
\overline{\text{ev}}_{\mathbb{R}}\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda}\to[-\infty,\infty]
.
Using
\text{ev}_{\mathbb{R}}
, we can extend
\chi
to a rel–
C^{1}
- map
\widetilde{\chi}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda}\to[0,1]
. Note that
\widetilde{\chi}
is invariant under the covering group action.

Let
\mathcal{F}\to\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}
and
\mathcal{E}^{0,1}\to\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}
be the s bundles with

\mathcal{F}_{(\widetilde{\varphi},u,w)}=W^{\ell,2,\delta}\Bigl(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{o}}_{\widetilde{\varphi}},u^{*}T\widetilde{Y}\Bigr)\qquad\qquad\mathcal{E}^{0,1}_{(\varphi,u,w)}=W^{\ell,2,\delta}\Bigl(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{o}}_{\widetilde{\varphi}},\Lambda^{0,1*}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{o}}_{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\otimes u^{*}T\widetilde{Y}\Bigr),

where
\ell\geq 6
and
\delta>0
is a sufficiently small exponential weight.
Let
B_{u}\in\Omega^{0,1}(\dot{C},\text{End}(u^{*}T\widetilde{Y}))
be the difference
D^{\nabla^{Y}}_{u}-(\nabla^{Y}\cdot)^{0,1}
. We define the rel–
C^{1}
family
\widetilde{D}_{(\widetilde{\varphi},u,w)}
of Cauchy–Riemann operators by

(4.82)

\widetilde{D}_{(\widetilde{\varphi},\varphi,u,w)}(\xi)=D^{\nabla^{Y}}_{u}(\xi)-\widetilde{\chi}(\widetilde{\varphi},\varphi,u,w,\cdot)B_{u}(\xi).

Writing the domain of
a_{\infty}(\varphi,u,w)
as
\bigsqcup\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}C^{\infty}_{\gamma}\vee C_{\gamma}
, where
C^{\infty}_{\gamma}
is the ‘cylinder on top’ (see Figure
7
), this operator is the restriction of

(4.83)

\widetilde{D}_{a(\varphi,u,w)}=\begin{cases}D^{\nabla^{Y}}_{u}(\xi)\quad&\text{on }C^{\infty}(C_{\gamma},u^{*}T\widetilde{Y})\\
\bar{\partial}^{\widetilde{\gamma}}\quad&\text{on }C^{\infty}(C_{\gamma}^{\infty},c_{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{*}T\widetilde{Y}),\end{cases}

where
c_{\widetilde{\gamma}}
is the trivial cylinder a parametrisation
\widetilde{\gamma}
of
\gamma
, induced by
u
and the asymptotic marker at
z_{\gamma}
. On the other hand, writing the domain of
a_{0}(\varphi,u,w)
as
\bigsqcup\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}C_{\gamma}\vee\bigvee\limits_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\gamma}}C^{0}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
, where
C^{0}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
are the ‘additional cylinders at the bottom’,
\widetilde{D}_{b(\varphi,u,w)}
is the restriction of

(4.84)

\widetilde{D}_{b(\varphi,u,w)}=\begin{cases}(\nabla^{Y}\xi)^{0,1}\quad&\text{on }C^{\infty}(C_{\gamma},u^{*}T\widetilde{Y})\\
\bar{\partial}^{\widetilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}\quad&\text{on }(C_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{0},c_{\widetilde{\gamma}^{\prime}}^{*}T\widetilde{Y}),\end{cases}

where
\widetilde{\gamma}^{\prime}
is the parametrization of
\gamma^{\prime}
determined by
u
and the asymptotic marker at
z_{\gamma^{\prime}}
.

We use finite-dimensional complex vector spaces of perturbations to achieve surjectivity of
\widetilde{D}
. However, first we extend the maps
\nu_{\gamma}\colon V^{-}_{\gamma}\to\Omega^{0,1}_{c}(\mathbb{R}\times S^{1},u_{\gamma}^{*}T\widetilde{Y})^{\mathbb{R}}
of §
4.4.1
to maps
\nu_{\gamma}\colon p_{\gamma}^{*}V^{-}_{\gamma}\to\mathcal{E}^{0,1}
for
\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}\sqcup\Gamma^{-}
, where
p_{\gamma}
is the evaluation map (
4.42
). We now explain how we can construct extensions of the perturbations (
4.63
).

Construction 4.43
.

We have the evaluation map

(4.85)

\text{ev}_{Y}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{o}}_{\Lambda}\to Y:(\widetilde{\varphi},u,w,z)\mapsto\text{pr}_{Y}(u(z)),

Choose now for
\gamma\in\Gamma^{\pm}
a neighborhood
U_{\gamma}^{\pm}
of the canonical section
\sigma_{\gamma}\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda}
given by the respective marked point so that

•

\text{ev}_{Y}({U^{\pm}_{\gamma}})\subset B_{\delta}(\gamma)
,

•

the closures are pairwise disjoint, and

•

the closures do not meet the critical points of
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda}\to\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}
.

Fix for each
\gamma
a rel–
C^{1}
invariant cut-off function
j_{\gamma}\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda}\to[0,1]
which is identically
1
near
\sigma_{\gamma}
and supported in
U_{\gamma}
. Recall that we have a canonical retraction
r\colon B_{\delta}(\gamma)\to\text{im}(\gamma)
. Let
\Phi_{x}
be the parallel transport along the normal geodesic from
r(x)
to
x
(using the Riemannian metric
h
on
Y
chosen above). Then, the vector bundle map

(4.86)

\widetilde{\nu}_{\gamma}\colon\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}\times_{E\gamma}V_{\gamma}^{-}\,\to\,\mathcal{E}^{0,1}

at
y=(\widetilde{\varphi},\varphi,u,w)
is given by

(4.87)

\widetilde{\nu}_{\gamma}(y,v)\coloneqq\begin{cases}j_{\gamma}(y,\cdot)\widetilde{\chi}(\text{ev}_{\mathbb{R}}(y,\cdot))\,\Phi_{u_{Y}(\cdot)}[\nu_{\gamma}(p_{\gamma}(y),v)(r_{\gamma}(u_{Y}(\cdot)))]\qquad&\text{on }\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{y}\cap U^{\pm}_{\gamma},\\
0\quad&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

where the superscript of
U^{\pm}_{\gamma}
is determined by whether
\gamma
labels a positive or negative puncture. This is well-defined even if
\gamma
is multiply-covered by the definition of the map
\nu_{\gamma}
in §
4.4.1
.

By
[
Kot
]
, we can arrange for the choices in Construction
4.43
to be compatible across boundary strata. We now do a similar extension for
\nu_{\gamma}
whenever
\gamma
is a Reeb orbit at which a building in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\text{SFT}}^{\,J}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-})
breaks. Since any element of
\Gamma^{+}
has action
\leq L
and each component has a unique positive puncture, we have
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}(\gamma)\leq L
. By the construction in Theorem
4.34
, each boundary stratum of
\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}
admits a map

(4.88)

\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda^{01}}\to\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda^{01}}\to G_{\Lambda}\times_{G_{\Lambda^{0}}\times G_{\Lambda^{1}}}\text{im}\Bigl(\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda^{0}}\overline{\times}_{B\Gamma}\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda^{1}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}\Bigr),

respectively,

(4.89)

\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda^{10}}\to\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda^{10}}\to G_{\Lambda}\times_{G_{\Lambda^{0}}\times G_{\Lambda^{1}}}\text{im}\Bigl(\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda^{0}}\overline{\times}_{B\Gamma}\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda^{1}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}\Bigr)

for some
\Gamma
and partitions
\Lambda^{1}\colon\Gamma\to\Gamma^{+}
and
\Lambda^{0}\colon\Gamma^{-}\to\Gamma
with
\Lambda^{1}\circ\Lambda^{0}=\Lambda
. The first map in each case is induced by the forgetful map
\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}\to\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda}
, while the second map is a vector bundle map (cf. Lemma
4.28
). The evaluation map
p_{\gamma}\colon\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda^{1}}\to E\gamma
for
\gamma\in\Gamma
is invariant under the
G_{\Lambda^{1}}
-action, so it induces a rel–
C^{1}
map

(4.90)

\widetilde{p}_{\gamma}\colon\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda^{01}}\to E\gamma

that is equivariant with respect to the action of
G_{\Lambda}
.

Construction 4.44
.

Let
U_{ij}\subset\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda^{ij}}
be an open subset of the boundary stratum so that for any curve in
U_{ij}
the component that is mapped to the symplectization is either directly above the Reeb orbits
\gamma\in\Gamma
or below it. In other words,
U_{ij}
is the complement of a union of some of the boundary strata. We can use the same definition as in Construction
4.43
once we have constructed for each such
\gamma
an equivariant extension of the map
\widetilde{p}_{\gamma}|_{U^{\gamma}_{ij}}
. We discuss the case of the embedding (
4.89
), and will just write
U
. Let
\sigma_{\gamma}\colon\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda^{ij}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda}
be the section given by the nodal point labeled by
\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}
. For each such
\gamma
, let
U^{\prime\prime}_{\gamma}
be a neighborhood of
\sigma_{\gamma}(U)
so that

•

\text{ev}_{Y}(U^{\prime\prime}_{\gamma}\setminus\text{im}(\sigma_{\gamma}))\subset B_{\delta}(\gamma)
,

•

\text{ev}_{\mathbb{R}}|_{U^{\prime\prime}_{\gamma}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{o}}_{\Lambda}}
is a fiber-wise submersion

•

\overline{U^{\prime\prime}_{\gamma}}
does not intersect
\overline{U_{\gamma^{\prime}}}
for any
\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{+}\sqcup\Gamma^{\prime}

•

\overline{U^{\prime\prime}_{\gamma}}\cap\overline{U^{\prime\prime}_{\gamma^{\prime}}}=\emptyset
if
\gamma\neq\gamma^{\prime}
are Reeb orbits at which buildings in
\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}
break,
7
7

7

Note that
\gamma
might appear twice as a Reeb orbit at which a building breaks. These two occurrences are distinct, although we omit this from the notation.

•

\overline{U^{\prime\prime}_{\gamma}}
only meets the critical points of
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda}\to\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}
in
\text{im}(\sigma_{\gamma})
.

In particular, we may assume that the intersection
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{y}\cap U^{\prime\prime}_{\gamma}
with a fiber is contained in the unique irreducible component
C^{\bullet}_{y}
of
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{y}
that is mapped to the symplectic cobordism. Set
U^{\prime}_{\gamma}:=U^{\prime\prime}_{\gamma}\setminus\text{im}(\sigma_{\gamma})
. The asymptotic marker at the positive puncture
\Lambda^{1}(\gamma)
and the matching isomorphism on nodes induce for each
y\in U
a path
L^{\gamma}_{y}\subset C_{y}^{\bullet}
from the unique positive ‘puncture’ (which is a point in
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda}
) of
C_{y}^{\bullet}
to
\sigma_{\gamma}(y)
. Since
\pi|_{U_{\gamma}}\colon U^{\prime}_{\gamma}\to\pi(U^{\prime}_{\gamma})
is a rel–
C^{1}
submersion, we can use Ehresmann’s Lemma and the connection induced by the chosen Riemannian metric and the Fubini-Study metric on complex projective space to obtain for each
y\in\pi(U^{\prime}_{\gamma})
an interval
L_{y}\subset C^{\bullet}_{y}
, possibly replacing the whole thickening by an invariant neighborhood of the zero locus
\mathfrak{s}^{-1}(0)
.
Writing
d
for the Gromov–Hausdorff metric on
\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}
, we define

(4.91)

R_{\gamma}\colon\pi(U^{\prime}_{\gamma})\to[0,\infty):y\mapsto d(y,\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda})

be the distance from the boundary stratum, where
\pi\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda}\to\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}
is the universal family. Then, we can extend
\sigma_{\gamma}
to a rel–
C^{1}
map
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\gamma}\colon\pi(U^{\prime}_{\gamma})\to U^{\prime\prime}_{\gamma}
by letting
\widetilde{\sigma}_{\gamma}(y)=:z
be the unique point in the fiber so that

\text{ev}_{\mathbb{R}}(y,z)=R_{\gamma}(y)\qquad\qquad z\in L_{y}.

Such a point exists and is unique due to the asymptotic behavior of the curves near nodes. Finally, we can define
\widetilde{p}_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}}\colon\pi(U^{\prime}_{\gamma})\to E\widetilde{\gamma}
via the composition

\pi(U^{\prime}_{\gamma})\,\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\sigma}_{\gamma}}\,U^{\prime\prime}_{\gamma}\,\xrightarrow{(y,z)\,\mapsto r(\text{ev}_{Y}(u,z))}\,\text{im}(\gamma)\,\xrightarrow{\simeq}\,E\gamma,

where the first map associates to
y
the point
r(\text{ev}_{Y}(\sigma_{\gamma^{\prime}}(y))
and the second map is the inverse of
\widetilde{\gamma}\mapsto\widetilde{\gamma}(1)
. Now we may define the extension by the term (
4.87
) with
p_{\gamma}
replaced by
\widetilde{p}_{\gamma}
.

We define

(4.92)

W^{v}_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\bigoplus\limits_{\Gamma^{-}\prec\Gamma\prec\Gamma^{+}}\bigoplus\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma}V_{\gamma}^{-}

equipped with the induced map
\widetilde{\nu}_{\Lambda}\colon W^{v}_{\Lambda}\to\mathcal{E}^{0,1}
.
Since
q\colon\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}\to\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}
is proper, the preimage
q^{-1}(\mathfrak{s}^{-1}(0))
is compact. Thus, we may find a finite-rank complex
G
-vector bundle
I^{v,-}_{\Lambda}\to\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}
over the ‘original’ thickening and an equivariant map
\kappa_{\Lambda}\colon I^{v,-}_{\Lambda}\to\mathcal{E}^{0,1}
so that the operator

(4.93)

\widetilde{D}_{(\widetilde{\varphi},u,w)}+\nu_{\Gamma^{+}}+\nu_{\Gamma^{-}}+\widetilde{\nu}_{\Lambda}+\kappa_{\Lambda}\colon\mathcal{F}_{(\widetilde{\varphi},u,w)}\,\oplus\,V_{\Gamma^{+}}^{-}\oplus\,V_{\Gamma^{-}}^{-}\oplus\,W^{v}_{\Lambda}\oplus{I^{v,-}_{\Lambda}}_{{}_{(\widetilde{\varphi},u,w)}}\to\,\mathcal{E}^{0,1}_{(\widetilde{\varphi},u,w)}

is surjective for any
{(\widetilde{\varphi},\varphi,u)}\in q^{-1}(\mathfrak{s}^{-1}(0))
. Shrinking
\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}
, we may assume (
4.93
) is surjective on all of
\widetilde{\mathscr{T}}_{\Lambda}
. We define

(4.94)

\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}\coloneqq\ker\Bigl(\widetilde{D}+\mu_{\Lambda}+\nu_{\Gamma^{+}}+\nu_{\Gamma^{-}}+\widetilde{\nu}_{\Lambda}+\kappa_{\Lambda}\Bigr)

where
\mu_{\Lambda}\colon E_{\Lambda}\to\mathcal{E}^{0,1}
is the perturbation space chosen in the construction of the global Kuranishi chart. Since
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
is invariant under the free
G^{\prime}_{\Lambda}
action, the quotient
\mathcal{W}\coloneqq\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}/G^{\prime}_{\Lambda}
admits a vector bundle map
\mathcal{W}\to\mathscr{T}^{c}_{\Lambda}
,
Since
\widetilde{\nu}_{\Lambda}
vanishes near
a_{\infty}
and
a_{0}
, we have a canonical isomorphism

a_{\infty}^{*}\mathcal{W}\;\cong\;q^{*}T^{v}\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,\mathbb{R}^{\Gamma^{+}}\,\oplus\,V_{\Gamma^{+}}^{+}\,\oplus\,V_{\Gamma^{-}}^{-}\,\oplus\,I^{v,-}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,W^{v}_{\Lambda},

where
\ker(D_{y}+\mu_{\Lambda}(y))=T^{v}_{y}\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{\Gamma^{+}}
because we quotient by
\mathbb{R}
-translations in the target. Meanwhile,

a_{0}^{*}\mathcal{W}\;\cong\;E_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,I^{v,+}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,V_{\Gamma^{-}}^{+}\,\oplus\,V_{\Gamma^{+}}^{-}\,\oplus\,W^{v}_{\Lambda},

whose first part
I^{v,+}_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\ker((\nabla^{Y}\cdot)^{0,1}+\mu_{\Lambda}+\kappa_{\Lambda})
is the kernel of a complex-linear surjective Cauchy–Riemann operator and thus carries a canonical complex structure.
∎

4.4.4.
Compatibilities of stable complex structures

We can now make Theorem
4.36
precise, phrasing the statement in terms of the partitions
\Lambda
to keep the notation consistent.

Proposition 4.45
.

Possibly after shrinking the thickenings of the morphism spaces of
\mathscr{M}
, there exists for any pair of objects
\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+}
and any partition
\Lambda\colon\Gamma^{-}\to\Gamma^{+}
an equivalence

(4.95)

T\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,V_{\Gamma^{+}}\,\oplus\,W_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\mathbb{R}\;\simeq\;I_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,V_{\Gamma^{-}}\,\oplus\,W_{\Lambda}

of virtual vector bundles that is compatible with the symmetric actions. For any factorization
\Gamma^{-}\xrightarrow{\Lambda^{0}}\Gamma\xrightarrow{\Lambda^{1}}\Gamma^{+}
of
\Lambda
we have an equivariant split embeddings

(4.96)

I_{\Lambda^{0}}\,\oplus\,I_{\Lambda^{1}}\;\to\;I_{\Lambda}

(4.97)

W_{\Lambda^{0}}\,\oplus\,W_{\Lambda^{1}}\;\to\;W_{\Lambda}

over
\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda^{0}}\overline{\times}_{B\Gamma}\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda^{1}}
so that the square of a

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda^{0}}\,\oplus\,V_{\Gamma^{-}}\,\oplus\,W_{\Lambda^{0}}\,\oplus\,\mathbb{R}_{\Lambda^{0}}\,\oplus T\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda^{1}}\,\oplus\,V_{\Gamma}\,\oplus\,W_{\Lambda^{1}}\,\oplus\,\mathbb{R}_{\Lambda^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus V_{\Gamma^{+}}\,\oplus\,W_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,\mathbb{R}_{\Lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\Lambda^{0}}\,\oplus\,V_{\Gamma^{-}}\,\oplus\,W_{\Lambda^{0}}\,\oplus I_{\Lambda^{1}}\,\oplus\,V_{\Gamma}\,\oplus\,W_{\Lambda^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,V_{\Gamma^{-}}\,\oplus\,W_{\Lambda}}

commutes, where
\mathbb{R}_{\Lambda^{1}}
is mapped to
\mathbb{R}_{\Lambda}
and
\mathbb{R}_{\Lambda^{1}}
to the normal bundle of the boundary stratum. Moreover, the restrictions are compatible across the boundary strata of codimension
2
.

Proof of Proposition
4.45
.

Recall that

T\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}=(T\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,T^{v}\mathscr{T}_{\Lambda},E_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda}),

where
\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{u}_{\Lambda}
is the Lie algebra of
\prod\limits_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{+}}{\text{PU}_{d_{\Lambda_{\gamma}}+1}(\mathbb{C})}
.
We define

(4.98)

I_{\Lambda}\coloneqq I^{b}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,I^{v}_{\Lambda}\quad\qquad U_{\Lambda}\coloneqq(0,\mathbb{R})\qquad\quad W_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\mathbb{R}^{\Gamma^{+}}\oplus W^{v}_{\Lambda}.

By Lemma
4.46
and Lemma
4.40
, it suffices to show that we can choose the vector bundles of Proposition
4.42
compatibly over boundary strata.
Due to the decompositions (
4.59
) and (
4.60
), we can discuss the compatibility of the stable complex structures on the tangent bundle of the base spaces and the vertical tangent bundles separately. The compatibility for the base spaces was shown in §
4.4.2
.
The existence of the split embeddings (
4.96
) follows from Lemma
4.40
and by constructing the bundles
I^{v,-}_{\Lambda}
in the proof of Proposition
4.42
inductively as in Step 2 of the proof of Lemma
4.30
, respectively, of Proposition
4.32
. Meanwhile, the existence of the split embeddings
I^{v,+}_{\Lambda^{0}}\,\oplus\,I^{v,+}_{\Lambda^{1}}\to I^{v,+}_{\Lambda}
follows immediately from the inductive construction of the perturbation spaces. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.46
.

Suppose
\mathscr{X}
is a symmetric flow category and
\mathscr{Y}
a symmetric flow bimodule from
\mathscr{X}
to itself, admitting for each
x,y\in\mathscr{X}
a fibration
q_{xy}\colon\mathscr{Y}(x,y)\to\mathscr{X}(x,y)
with fibers given by the interval
[0,1]
, so that the system
\{q_{xy}\}
is compatible with the structural maps of the flow bimodule, including the symmetric actions. Suppose also that
q_{x,y}
admits two boundary sections
a_{+},a_{-}
so that the squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}(x,y)\,\overline{\times}_{y}\,\mathscr{X}(y,z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}(x,z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{Y}(x,y)\,\overline{\times}_{y}\,\mathscr{X}(y,z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{Y}(x,z)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{-}\times\text{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{-}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}(x,y)\,\overline{\times}_{y}\,\mathscr{X}(y,z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}(x,z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}(x,y)\,\overline{\times}_{y}\,\mathscr{Y}(y,z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{Y}(x,z)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{id}\times a_{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{+}}

commute. Then, for any vector bundle
\mathscr{W}\to\mathscr{Y}
that is compatibly a flow bimodule itself, there exists a contractible space of systems
\{\Phi_{x,y}\}_{x,y}
of compatible isomorphisms

(4.99)

\Phi_{xy}\colon a_{+}^{*}\mathscr{W}(x,y)\to a_{-}^{*}\mathscr{W}(x,y)

Proof.

Using
[
Kot
]
, we inductively choose a system of invariant Riemannian metrics so that the composition maps are isometric. Then, we use the Riemannian metrics to inductively construct countable locally finite open covers
\mathcal{U}^{xy}=\{B_{r_{i}}(x_{i})\}_{i}
so that
\mathcal{U}^{xz}\cap(\mathscr{X}(x,y)\overline{\times}-y\mathscr{X}(y,z))=\mathcal{U}^{xy}\,\overline{\times}_{y}\,\mathcal{U}^{yz}
. Inductively, choose trivializations

\widetilde{U}_{i}:=q^{-1}(U_{i})\xrightarrow{\psi_{i}}U_{i}\times[0,1],

so that
\psi_{i}(a_{-}(b))=(b,0)
and
\psi_{i}(a_{+}(b))=(b,1)
, and

W|_{\widetilde{U}_{i}}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\psi}_{i}}U_{i}\times[0,1]\times W_{a_{+}(x_{i})},

covering
\psi_{i}
and which are compatible across boundary strata. Inductively, define orderings of the open covers
\mathcal{U}^{xy}
so that the inclusions
\mathcal{U}^{xy}\,\overline{\times}_{y}\,\mathcal{U}^{yz}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{U}^{xz}
are order-preserving. Then, the construction in the proof of
[
tD08
, Theorem (14.3.1)]
carries over to yield the desired compatible isomorphisms.
∎

4.5.
A colimit and a cylindrical contact flow category

Our construction of global Kuranishi charts for the moduli spaces
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J}_{\text{SFT}}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)_{\Lambda}
requires us to approximate the contact form
\lambda
by a
1
-form with integral action on the Reeb orbits in order to construct framings. This forces our construction of the flow category
\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{\leq L}
to depend on the action bound
L
. To obtain the “full flow category” of
(Y,\lambda)
we take a colimit of the induced diagram of flow categories. Since we do not prove that the composition of our flow bimodules is (homotopic to) the flow bimodule of the ‘larger’ cobordism, the full flow category will be a telescope of the flow categories of Theorem
B
.

Proposition 4.47
.

There is a directed system

\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{1}\rightarrow\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{2}\rightarrow\dots

of the symmetric stably complex flow categories constructed in Theorem
4.20
.

Definition 4.48
.

We call the colimit

\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}\coloneqq\colim\limits\,\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{n}

in
\text{Flow}^{\Sigma,U}
“the”
contact flow category
of the contact manifold
(Y,\lambda).

While we expect
\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}
to be independent of the required auxiliary choices, we do not show this invariance here.

Proof.

From the discreteness of
\spec(\lambda)\subset\mathbb{R}
, we can find an order-preserving enumeration
a\colon\mathbb{N}\to\spec(\lambda)
. We choose
\mathcal{P}_{L}
-integral approximations by taking any
\mathcal{P}_{a(1)}
-integral approximation
\widetilde{\lambda}_{1}
and selecting the
\mathcal{P}_{a(n+1)}
-integral approximation
\widetilde{\lambda}_{n}
such that the action
\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{\lambda}_{n}}(\gamma)
divides
\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{\lambda}_{n+1}}(\gamma)
whenever
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}(\gamma)\leq a(n)
.
Choose prime integers
p_{n}
so that for any
n\geq 1
, the pair
(p^{+},p^{-})=(p_{n+1},p_{n})
satisfies (
3.54
). Let
\widehat{X}=\widehat{Y}
be the trivial cobordism equipped with the chosen almost complex structure
J
and let
\mathscr{N}^{n}_{\widehat{Y}}
be the bimodule associated to
(\widehat{X},J)
and the following pre-perturbation datum
\mathfrak{D}^{\pm}_{n}
given by

•

L^{+}=a(n+1),L^{-}=a(n)
,

•

\mathcal{P}^{-}_{n}=\mathcal{P}_{a(n)}
and
\mathcal{P}^{+}_{n}=\mathcal{P}_{a(n+1)}
,

•

p^{+}=p_{n+1}
,
p^{-}=p_{n}
.

The bimodules
\mathscr{N}^{n}_{\widehat{Y}}
induce the directed system
\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{1}\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathscr{N}^{1}_{\widehat{Y}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\;\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{2}\;\xrightarrow{\mathscr{N}^{2}_{\widehat{Y}}}\;\dots
of the claim.

It remains to show that they admit stable complex structures compatible with those of
\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{i}
from Theorem
4.36
.
Fix thus
i\in\mathbb{N}
. By
[
AB24
, Definition 4.17]
, respectively its adaption to our setting, we have to construct for any function
\Lambda\colon\Gamma^{-}\to\Gamma^{+}
between a sequence of Reeb orbits considered as objects of
\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{i}
and
\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{i+1}
, respectively, the data of

(1)

a complex virtual vector bundle
\widetilde{I}_{\Lambda}

(2)

a vector bundle
\widetilde{W}_{\Lambda}

(3)

the vector space
\widetilde{U}_{\Lambda}=(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R}^{\{\Gamma^{+}\}})

on
\mathscr{N}^{i}_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\mathscr{N}^{i}_{Y}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})_{\Lambda}
together with an equivalence

(4.100)

T\mathscr{N}^{i}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,V^{i}_{\Gamma^{+}}\,\oplus\,\widetilde{W}_{\Lambda}\ \cong\ \widetilde{I}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,\widetilde{U}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,\widetilde{W}_{\Lambda}\,\oplus\,V^{i+1}_{\Gamma^{+}}

where
V^{j}_{\Gamma}
is the vector bundle constructed in §
4.4.1
with the perturbation data used for
\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{j}
. These are required to satisfy analogues of the compatibility conditions described in Definition
4.13
. As in §
4.4
, the construction of such a stably complex lift of
\mathscr{N}^{\widehat{Y}}_{i}
can be split up into the construction of stable complex structures on the base spaces, cf. Lemma
4.40
, and those on the vertical tangent bundle. The generalization of Lemma
4.40
to the cobordism base spaces is straightforward, because the key input, Lemma
4.38
, is a general statement that also holds for cobordism base spaces. The construction of the stable complex structures on the vertical tangent bundles is exactly the same as in §
4.4.3
. The only difference is that we have
T^{v}\mathscr{N}^{i}_{\Lambda}=\ker(D(\bar{\partial}_{J})+\mu_{\Lambda})
, while
T^{v}\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{i}\oplus\mathbb{R}=\ker(D(\bar{\partial}_{J})+\mu_{\Lambda})
due to the fact that we quotient by translations in the target for curves in the symplectization. This accounts for the difference between
U_{\Lambda}
and
\widetilde{U}_{\Lambda}
. Choosing the auxiliary data needed for the construction of the stable complex structures inductively, their compatibility with the composition maps in the sense of Proposition
4.45
follows.
∎

In the construction of the full contact flow category above, we were forced to take the colimit approach due to a lack of an integral approximation
\widetilde{\lambda}
without first filtering through the action. However, we can construct a flow category for cylindrical contact homology directly. We collect the necessary modifications to obtain a cylindrical contact flow category.

Theorem 4.49
.

Let
(Y,\lambda)
be a hyper-tight contact manifold with a given compatible almost complex structure
J
interpolating between cylindrical almost complex structures
J^{\pm}
. Then, there exists a flow category
\mathscr{M}_{cyl}
of class rel–
C^{1}
whose objects are the Reeb orbits of
\lambda
and whose morphism spaces are

\mathscr{M}_{cyl}^{Y,\lambda}(\gamma^{-},\gamma^{+})=\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\text{SFT}}^{\,J}(\gamma^{+},\gamma^{-})

for any pair
(\gamma^{+},\gamma^{-})
of Reeb orbits.

Proof.

Recall that the construction of the global Kuranishi charts in §
3.3
started off with a choice of a pre-perturbation datum
\mathfrak{D}=(\widetilde{\lambda},\nabla,p)
as in Definition
3.27
. The integral approximation
\widetilde{\lambda}
was only used to frame holomorphic buildings; cf. §
3.2.1
. We can instead use the following scheme to frame cylindrical holomorphic buildings. Let

\mathcal{A}_{e}\colon\spec(\lambda)\to\mathbb{N}

denote the ordered enumeration function on the spectrum of the contact form
\lambda
. Given a cylindrical holomorphic building
u
, we replace the complex line bundle in (
3.17
) with the following cylindrical version

(4.101)

L^{c}_{u_{v}}\coloneqq\mathcal{O}_{C_{v}}\Bigl({\mathcal{A}_{e}(\gamma_{e^{+}})\;z_{v,e^{+}}}-{\mathcal{A}_{e}(\gamma_{e^{-}})\;z_{v,e^{-}}}\Bigr)^{\otimes p}.

We use sections of such line bundles to frame cylindrical buildings. The map

(4.102)

\displaystyle E\colon\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\text{SFT}}^{J}(\gamma^{+},\gamma^{-})^{cyl}\to\mathbb{N}

(4.103)

\displaystyle[u]\mapsto\mathcal{A}_{e}(\gamma^{+})-\mathcal{A}_{e}(\gamma^{-})

that sends a cylindrical holomorphic building to the difference of
\mathcal{A}_{e}
-action of the incoming and outgoing Reeb orbit, satisfies the following properties:

•

E
is additive under cylindrical breaking, i.e.,
E([u_{1}]\#[u_{2}])=E([u_{1}])+E([u_{2}]),

•

E\geq 0
and
E([u])=0
if and only if
[u]
is a trivial cylinder.

The proof of Theorem
4.20
now carries over verbatim except for replacing every mention of
\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{\lambda}}
with
\mathcal{A}_{e}
, using the line bundles of (
4.101
), and assuming that each
\Gamma
is a singleton set.
∎

4.6.
Recovering contact homology

We sketch that our flow category recovers contact homology, a classical invariant of closed contact manifolds, following
[
Par19
]
. Let
(Y^{2n-1},\xi)
be a closed contact manifold equipped with a non-degenerate contact form
\lambda
as before. Fix a choice of
\lambda
-adapted almost complex structure
J
and action bound
L
as well as the perturbation data required for the construction of the stably complex flow category
\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{\leq L}
. We will outline how this data yields a
\mathbb{Z}/2
-graded chain complex
(CC_{*}(Y,\xi)_{\lambda}^{\leq L},\partial)
, where

(4.104)

CC^{\leq L}_{*}(Y,\xi)_{\lambda}\coloneqq\bigoplus\limits_{k\geq 0}\text{Sym}^{k}_{\mathbb{Q}}\Bigl(\bigoplus\limits_{\gamma\in(\mathcal{P}_{\leq L})_{\text{good}}}\mathfrak{o}_{\gamma}\otimes\mathbb{Q}\Bigr),

and the differential on the space generated by
\mathfrak{o}_{\Gamma^{+}}=\mathfrak{o}_{\gamma_{1}}\dots\mathfrak{o}_{\gamma_{k}}
is induced by the map

(4.105)

\partial_{\Gamma}\;\coloneqq\;\sum\limits_{\Gamma^{-}}{\;[\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{\leq L}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma)_{(0)}]^{\text{vir}}}

where
\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{\leq L}(\Gamma^{-},\Gamma^{+})_{(0)}
is the part of the morphism space of virtual dimension
0
and
[\cdot]^{\text{vir}}
is any choice of virtual count as in
[
Par19
, §4]
or
[
BH23
, §9]
. The fact that these virtual counts define well-defined maps between orientation lines follows from the existence of stable complex structures, Theorem
4.36
on the flow category
\mathscr{M}^{Y,\lambda}_{\leq L}
.

For the sketch, recall that the orientation line
\mathfrak{o}_{\gamma}
defined in §
3.6
and has parity

|\mathfrak{o}_{\gamma}|=|\gamma|=\text{sign}(\det(\text{id}-A_{\gamma})),

where
A_{\gamma}
is the asymptotic operator associated to
\gamma
. We set
|\Gamma|=\sum_{i=1}^{k}|\gamma_{i}|
for
\Gamma=(\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{k})
and use this to grade the chain complex (
4.104
). The key properties of the maps
\partial_{\Gamma}
are

(1)

\partial_{\Gamma}
has odd degree for each
\Gamma

(2)

the maps satisfy the Leibniz rule

\partial_{(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})}=\partial_{(\gamma_{1})}\otimes\text{id}+(-1)^{|\gamma_{1}|}\text{id}\otimes\partial_{(\gamma_{2})}.

Both follow from studying the relevant moduli spaces of buildings. Then, the maps
\partial_{\Gamma}
descend to a map
\partial
on
CC_{*}(Y,\xi)_{\lambda}^{\leq L}
and it remains to show that
\partial^{2}=0
. Once this is proven, the
contact homology of action at most
L
of
(Y,\lambda)
will be

HC^{\leq L}_{*}(Y,\xi)_{\lambda}:=H_{*}(CC^{\leq L}_{*}(Y,\xi)_{\lambda},\partial).

Appendix A
Gluing Results

This section develops the tools required for showing that the thickening
\mathcal{T}
is a topological manifold over the base-space
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}
where the fibers carry a smooth structure. Our presentation of the gluing results is similar to
[
AMS24
, Appendix A]
and
[
Par19
, §5]
.

Let
(\widehat{Y},J)
be the symplectization of a contact manifold
(Y,\lambda)
equipped with an
\mathbb{R}
-invariant almost complex structure
J
, which satisfies
J(\partial_{s})=R
where
\partial_{s}
is the infinitesimal vector field generated by the translation
\mathbb{R}
action and
R
is the Reeb vector field. Let
\overline{\mathcal{M}}\to\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}
be a smooth map and define
\mathcal{C}
via the pull-back square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{B}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}.}

Recall that
\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}
is the base space, which was obtained from a real-oriented blow-up of certain divisors of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{*}(\mathbb{P}^{N}).
In particular, each fiber in the universal bundle consists of the data of

•

a curve
C

•

matching conditions at certain nodes of
C

Let
\overline{\mathcal{C}}^{0}
denote the complement of the nodal points in
\overline{\mathcal{C}}
and
\mathfrak{Y}_{\overline{\mathcal{C}}}:=\Omega^{0,1}_{\overline{\mathcal{C}}/\overline{\mathcal{M}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}T\widehat{Y}

be the bundle over
\overline{\mathcal{C}}^{0}\times\widehat{Y}
which consists of
T\widehat{Y}
-valued anti-holomorphic forms on the vertical bundle of
\overline{\mathcal{C}}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}.
Let
W
be a real vector space with a linear map

A\colon W\to\Gamma^{\infty}_{c}(\mathfrak{Y}_{\overline{\mathcal{C}}}).

Given a decorated corolla
T
, we define the regular locus of moduli of buildings
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{T}^{\,\text{reg}}(\widehat{Y})
to be

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{T}^{\,\text{reg}}(\widehat{Y})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l|l}\nu\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}&u\ \textnormal{is smooth and is of type }T\\
u\colon\overline{\mathcal{C}}|_{\nu}\rightarrow{}\widehat{Y}&\bar{\partial}_{J}u+A(w)(\cdot,u(\cdot))=0\\
w\in W&u\ \textnormal{is regular}\\
\end{array}\right\}.

We denote the fiber over a point
\nu\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}
by
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\text{reg}}_{T}(\widehat{Y})|_{\nu}
. The regularity assumption in (
A
) implies that each fiber
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\text{reg}}_{T}(\widehat{Y})|_{\nu}
carries a unique smooth structure. However, this smooth structure does not necessarily extend to a smooth structure on all of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\text{reg}}_{T}(\widehat{Y})
due to the resolution of nodes. The next gluing statement shows a somewhat weaker assertion. It is the main result of this section.

Theorem A.1
.

For any
(\nu,u,w)\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{T}^{\,\text{reg}}(\widehat{Y})
, there exists a neighborhood
N
of
(\nu,u,w)
in the fiber
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\text{reg}}_{T}(\widehat{Y})|_{\nu}
and a neighborhood
B
of
\nu
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}
admitting an embedding
g\colon B\times N\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\text{reg}}_{T}(\widehat{Y})
that fits in the commuting square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B\times N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\,\text{reg}}_{T}(\widehat{Y})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{\mathcal{M}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\proj}

such that the restriction of
g
to each fiber of the trivial fibration
B\times N\to B
is smooth.

We first prove a special case of Theorem
A.1
, assuming that
\overline{\mathcal{M}}\to\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}
is an open embedding. Then, we use an exponentiation-along-pullback-of-the-tangent-bundle trick to extend it to any map
\overline{\mathcal{M}}\to\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}
.

Proposition A.2
.

For any two gluing maps
g_{1},g_{2}
as constructed in Theorem
A.1
the restrictions to the fibers over
B_{1}\cap B_{2}

(A.4)

g_{21,b}\coloneqq g_{2}^{-1}\circ g_{1}|_{b}\colon(\{b\}\times N_{1})\cap g_{1}^{-1}(g_{2}(B_{2}\times N_{2}))\to\{b\}\times N_{2}

vary continuously in the
C^{1}_{loc}-
topology.

Corollary A.3
.

There exists a well-defined
vertical tangent bundle

(A.5)

T^{v}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{T}^{\text{reg}}(\widehat{Y})\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{T}^{\text{reg}}(\widehat{Y})

with fibers given by

(A.6)

T^{v}_{(b,u)}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{T}^{\text{reg}}(\widehat{Y})=\ker(D_{\varphi}+P).

A.1.
Setup for Gluing

We will present the gluing treatment as done in
[
Par19
, §5]
for the sake of completeness. The main gluing theorem as stated in op. cit. is a local homeomorphism result, but actually the gluing map is smooth for a fixed gluing parameter. In our discussion we will point out how to extract that result from
[
Par19
, §5]
and use it to prove Theorem
A.1
.
To be consistent with notations in
[
Par19
]
we recall some relevant concepts here.

A.1.1.
Preliminaries on §2.2 and 2.5 of
[
Par19
]

In §
2.2
, we defined the categories
\mathscr{S}
stratifying the moduli spaces
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{J}_{P}(\Gamma^{+},\Gamma^{-};\beta)
of Pardon buildings. The space of gluing parameters of decorated tree
T
as in Definition
2.1
is defined to be

G_{T/}\coloneqq(0,\infty]^{E^{int}(T)}.

Given a gluing parameter
\mathbf{\ell}=\{\ell_{e}\}_{e}
, the tree type
T_{\ell}
is obtained by contracting all the edges for which
\ell_{e}<\infty.
In certain setups, it is conceptually beneficial to reparametrize
G_{T/}\cong[0,1)^{E^{int}(T)}
by applying the function
x\to e^{-x}
to each factor.

Convention A.4
.

In this section we will implicitly assume that the gluing parameter space
\mathbb{C}^{N_{0}}
actually denotes a small open neighborhood of
0
in
\mathbb{C}^{N_{0}}
.

A.1.2.
Target Gluing

Fix a point
(\nu,[u],w)\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{T}^{\text{reg}}(\widehat{Y})
in the fiber over
\nu\in\mathcal{B}^{P}
. For a gluing parameter
\ell\in G_{T_{\nu}/}
, we define the
glued target

\widehat{Y}_{\ell}
as follows. For each positive (resp. negative) end we truncate
[0,\infty)\times Y
(resp.
(-\infty,0]\times Y
) to
[0,\ell_{e}]
(resp.
[-\ell_{e},0]
)and identify the truncated ends corresponding to the edge
e\in E^{int}(T)
by translation by
\ell_{e}
. If
\ell_{e}=\infty
for some edge
e
, we do nothing for that edge. The target constructed by these gluing operations is denoted as
\widehat{Y}_{\ell}
.

We also select local sections
q_{i}^{\prime}
of the universal curve over
\mathcal{B}^{P}
near
\nu
for every curve corresponding to a vertex of
T_{\nu}
, such that
\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(u(q_{i}^{\prime}(\nu))=0.
In our gluing construction, we will send these marked points to their corresponding
0-
levels in the glued target
\widehat{Y}_{\textbf{g}}
. This choice of sections is intuitively a gauge-fixing for the
\mathbb{R}
action on the target. In particular, it allows us to pick a map
u\colon\mathcal{C}^{0}_{\nu}\to\mathbb{R}\times Y
in the equivalence class of maps
[u]
.

A.1.3.
Gluing in the base

Before elaborating on the gluing chart in the base space, we show how we can reduce to the case of the real-oriented blow-up of the moduli space of stable curves.

Lemma A.5
.

For any
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}^{P}(d)
there exist
d^{\prime}=d(d+2)
hyperplanes
D_{i}
in
\mathbb{P}^{d}
such that
\widehat{\varphi}
intersects
D_{i}
transversely away from the marked points and there is an open embedding

\displaystyle U

\displaystyle\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{0,d^{\prime}+E^{\text{ext}}}

\displaystyle[\check{\phi},C,j,x,m]

\displaystyle\mapsto[C,j,x\cup\check{\phi}^{-1}(D_{1})\cup\dots\cup\check{\phi}^{-1}(D_{d^{\prime}}),x]

of a neighborhood of
\varphi_{0}
into real-oriented blow-up
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{0,d^{\prime}+E^{\text{ext}}(T)}
of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,d^{\prime}+E^{\text{ext}}(T)}
at the boundary divisors.

Proof.

This is a generalization of
[
BX22
, Lemma 5.15(3)]
, which can be shown in the same way, using
[
AMS21
, Proposition 6.5]
.
∎

For the element
\nu=(\varphi_{0},C_{0},j_{0},m)
of
\mathcal{B}^{P}
, we will construct a neighborhood in
\mathcal{B}^{P}
based on the asymptotics of the map
u
. Recall that
(C_{0},j_{0})
is a marked closed Riemann surface,
\varphi_{0}\colon C_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N}
is a holomorphic embedding into
\mathbb{P}^{N}
and
m
is the additional decoration of asymptotic markers and matching conditions at certain nodes. Pick cylindrical charts of the form
[M,\infty)\times S^{1}
or
(-\infty,M]\times S^{1}
near each puncture of
C_{0}
such that the map
u
satisfies

u(s,t)=(Ls,\gamma(t))+O(e^{-ks}).

Note that such a choice of cylindrical end is equivalent to the choice of a tangent vector
v\in T_{p}C_{v}
at the puncture
p
.

To get a gluing chart near
\nu\in\mathcal{B}^{P}
, we use the identification from Lemma
A.5
. We will use the local model of the moduli space of genus
0
curves with asymptotic markers and matching conditions as
[
Par19
, §2.6 - 2.7, 5.1.3]
. We denote the component of the curve
C_{0}
corresponding to the vertex
v\in V(T)
as
C_{0}^{v}
. Assume that
C_{0}^{v}
has
N_{v}
nodes. Recall that nodes do not carry matching conditions.
Pick a chart

\mathscr{J}_{v}:=\mathbb{C}^{2\#p_{v}+\#q_{v}-N_{v}-3}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,q_{v,i},{p_{v,i}}_{(2)}}^{\#\text{nodes}=\#N_{v}}

which sends
0
to the curve
(C_{0}^{v},j_{0}^{v})
and
z\in\mathscr{J}_{v}
to
(C_{0}^{v},j_{z}^{v})
, where
j_{z}^{v}
is a complex structure agreeing with
j_{0}^{v}
near the special points.
The subscript
(2)
denotes that the points
p_{v,i}
are doubly-marked, i.e., the tangent space
T_{p_{v,i}}C_{0}^{v}\setminus\{0\}
carries a marking (equivalently, there is an associated complex isomorphism
\mathbb{C}\to T_{p_{v,i}}C_{0}^{v}
).
Moreover, we can identify
\mathscr{J}_{v}
with an open neighborhood of
0
in the cokernel of the map

\displaystyle\left\{X\in C^{\infty}(C^{v}_{0},TC^{v}_{0})\;\middle|\;\begin{array}[]{l}X\text{ is holomorphic near }p,q,\\
X(p_{v,i})=X(q_{v,i})=0,\\
dX(p_{i})=0\end{array}\right\}\,\to\,C^{\infty}_{c}(C^{v}_{0}\setminus\cup\{p_{v,i}\}\cup\{q_{v,i}\},TC^{v}_{0}\otimes\Omega^{0,1}_{C^{v}_{0}})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad X\mapsto\mathscr{L}_{X}{j_{0}}

We can then obtain a local diffeomorphism

{\raisebox{1.99997pt}{$\Bigl(\prod_{v\in V(T)}\mathscr{J}_{v}\times\mathbb{C}^{\#N_{v}}\Bigr)$}\left/\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\sim$}\right.}\;\;\to f\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,d^{\prime}+E^{\text{ext}}(T)}|_{T_{\nu}}\to\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\nu}}.

where the quotient
\sim
is taken over relations induced from the
\mathbb{R}_{>}0
action on the tensor at the doubly marked points. In particular, for every edge
e=(v,v^{\prime})
, we quotient by the
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
action on
T_{p_{v}}C^{v}_{0}\otimes T_{p_{v^{\prime}}}C^{v^{\prime}}_{0}
. Here,
\mathcal{B}^{P}|^{\text{nodes fixed}}_{T_{\nu}}
is the locus of all framed curves of tree type
T_{\nu}
with a fixed number of nodes.
Abusing notation, we denote the image of this diffeomorphism by
\mathcal{B}^{P}|^{\text{nodes fixed}}_{T_{\nu}}
.
We construct a local chart near
\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\nu}}
of the form,

(A.7)

\text{Glue}_{\mathcal{B}^{P}}:\mathcal{B}^{P}|^{\text{nodes fixed}}_{T_{\nu}}\times G_{T_{\nu}/}\times\mathbb{C}^{N^{{}^{\mathrm{o}}}_{C_{0}}}\to\mathcal{B}^{P},

where the map
\Glue_{\mathcal{B}}^{P}
is defined by a slight variation of the usual gluing in moduli of genus
0
curves.
In particular, over the nodes in
\mathcal{N}_{C}^{\bullet}
we restrict it to the angle
\theta=(\theta_{e})_{e}
determined by the matching isomorphisms in
\nu
, while the gluing parameter
\ell=(\ell_{e})_{e}\in G_{T_{\nu}/}
determines the radial component of each coordinate
\textbf{y}=(y_{e})_{e}\in\mathbb{C}^{E^{int}(T_{\nu})}.

More precisely, suppose
(C_{0},j_{0},m)\in\mathcal{B}^{P}
is a curve with matching conditions
m
, which is over the fiber
(C_{0},j_{0})\in\mathcal{B}
under the blow-down map
\mathcal{B}^{P}\to\mathcal{B}
.
Then,

(A.8)

\text{Glue}_{\mathcal{B}^{P}}((\varphi,C,j_{0},m),\ell,\textbf{z})=(\varphi_{\textbf{g}},C_{\textbf{g}},j_{\textbf{g}},m_{\textbf{g}}),

where
\textbf{g}=((y_{e})_{e},\textbf{z}),\;y_{e}=(\ell_{e}e^{i\theta_{e}})
and
(C_{\textbf{g}},j_{\textbf{g}})
is obtained by the usual truncation-followed-by-gluing construction. The decoration
m_{\textbf{g}}
for the glued curve
(C_{\textbf{g}},j_{\textbf{g}})
is induced from
(C_{0},j_{0},m)
by keeping the asymptotic markers fixed and remembering the matching conditions at the edges
e
for which
\ell_{e}\neq 0.

Notation
.

From now on, we will write
\textbf{g}=(\ell,z)\in G_{T_{\nu}/}\times\mathbb{C}^{N^{{}^{\mathrm{o}}}_{C_{0}}}
to denote the ‘total’ gluing parameter, which accounts for gluing along both Reeb punctures and nodes. We also abbreviate
\text{Glue}_{\mathcal{B}^{P}}(\varphi,C,j,m),\textbf{g})
as simply
\nu_{\textbf{g}}=(\varphi_{\textbf{g}},C_{\textbf{g}}).

We can rewrite the gluing map in (
A.9
) in a conciser form by replacing
\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\nu}}\times\mathbb{C}^{N^{o}_{C_{0}}}
with the locus of
\mathcal{B}^{P}
corresponding to the tree type
T_{\nu}
,
\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\nu}}
.

(A.9)

\text{Glue}_{\mathcal{B}^{P}}:\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\nu}}\times G_{T_{\nu}/}\to\mathcal{B}^{P},

A.1.4.
Linearization with respect to varying domain complex structures

Recall that the linearization of the section
\bar{\partial}(\cdot)+A(\cdot)(\cdot,\cdot)
at the point
(\nu,u,w)\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}
is given as

(A.10)

D^{v}_{0}\colon W^{k,p,\delta}(C_{\nu},u^{*}T\widehat{Y})\oplus W\to W^{k-1,p,\delta}(C_{\nu},u^{*}T\widehat{Y}\otimes\Omega^{0,1}TC_{\nu}),

where the superscript
v
denotes that this is the ‘vertical’ part of the total linearization with respect to the fibration
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{reg}_{T}(\widehat{Y})\to\mathcal{B}^{P}.

If
(\nu,u,w)
is in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{reg}_{T}(\widehat{Y})
, then the vertical linearization
D^{v}_{0}
is surjective. We also have the linearization
D_{0}

D_{0}\colon W^{k,p,\delta}(C_{\nu},u^{*}T\widehat{Y})\oplus W\oplus\mathcal{J}_{T_{v}}\to W^{k-1,p,\delta}(C_{\nu},u^{*}T\widehat{Y}\otimes\Omega^{0,1}TC_{\nu})

given by

D_{0}(\eta,w,\xi)=D_{0}^{v}(\eta,w)+J\circ du\circ\xi.

Due to the regularity assumption, we have the local diffeomorphisms

(A.11)

\displaystyle\ker D^{v}_{0}\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{reg}_{T}(\widehat{Y})_{\nu}

(A.12)

\displaystyle\ker D_{0}\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{reg}_{T}(\widehat{Y})_{T_{\nu}},

near the origin, where
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{reg}_{T}(\widehat{Y})_{\nu}
is the restriction to fiber over
\nu\in\mathcal{B}^{P}
and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{reg}_{T}(\widehat{Y})_{T_{\nu}}
is the restriction to strata
\mathcal{B}^{P}_{T_{\nu}}
corresponding to the tree type
T_{\nu}
.

A.1.5.
Pre-Glued maps

For a gluing parameter,
\textbf{g}\in G_{T_{\nu}/}\times\mathbb{C}^{N^{{}^{\mathrm{o}}}_{C_{0}}}
. Recall that
g
defines a positive real parameter
\ell_{e}
for each internal edge
e
and that there is a Reeb orbit
\widetilde{\gamma}_{e}
for each edge
e
such that
u_{0}
is asymptotic to the trivial cylinder
(Ls,\widetilde{\gamma}_{e}(t))
where
L
is the
\lambda
-action of
\widetilde{\gamma}_{e}.
We define the
flattened
building
u_{0}|_{\textbf{g}}
as follows: for every internal edge
e,
in the chosen cylindrical coordinates
(s,t)
near the positive puncture corresponding to the edge
e
, we define

u_{0}|_{\textbf{g}}(s,t):=\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{cc}u_{0}(s,t)&s<\frac{1}{6}\ell_{e}\\
\exp_{(Ls,\widetilde{\gamma}_{e}(t))}[\chi(s-\frac{1}{6}\ell_{e}).\exp_{(Ls,\widetilde{\gamma}_{e}(t))}^{-1}u_{0}(s,t)]&\frac{1}{6}\ell_{e}\leq s\leq\frac{1}{6}S+1\\
(Ls,\widetilde{\gamma}_{e}(t))\quad\frac{1}{6}&\ell_{e}+1<s.\par\end{array}\end{cases}

Near the positive end of a nodal point
n
of
(C_{0},u_{0})
, we define the flattening as

u_{0}|_{\textbf{g}}(s,t):=\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{cc}u_{0}(s,t)&s<\frac{1}{6}S_{\mathsf{n}}\\
\exp_{u_{0}(\mathsf{n})}[\chi(s-\frac{1}{6}S).\exp_{u_{0}(\mathsf{n})}^{-1}u_{0}(s,t)]&\frac{1}{6}S_{\mathsf{n}}\leq s\leq\frac{1}{6}S_{\mathsf{n}}+1\\
u_{0}(\mathsf{n})&\frac{1}{6}S_{\mathsf{n}}+1<s.\par\end{array}\par\par\end{cases}

where
S_{\mathsf{n}}=-\log|z_{\mathsf{n}}|
for the gluing parameter
z_{\mathsf{n}}
corresponding to the node
\mathsf{n}
.
We define the flattening analogously in the negative ends of the punctures and nodes. Here the exponential maps are taken with respect to a fixed
\mathbb{R}-
invariant metric on
\widehat{Y}
.

Definition A.6
.

We define the
pre-glued
map
u_{\textbf{g}}\colon C_{\textbf{g}}\to\widehat{Y}_{\textbf{g}}
to be the natural descent of the flattened map
u_{0}|_{\textbf{g}}.

A.2.
Gluing Estimates

We will now cover the required Fredholm setup and compare the linearization of the usual ‘Floer-function’ on the pre-glued map with the linearization before gluing. We will also prove a ‘kernel-gluing’ which is an isomorphism between kernels of linearization before and after pre-gluing. In this section our analysis slightly deviates from Pardon’s setup since we consider the gluing map without variation of the domain curve.

Fredholm Setup

We recall the relevant Sobolev spaces required for gluing. We start off with selecting metrics and connections.

Convention A.7
.

We fix an
\mathbb{R}-
invariant metric on the target
\widehat{Y}
and a
J-
linear connection
on
\widehat{Y}
that is induced from the pullback of the natural map
\widehat{Y}\to\{0\}\times Y
. On the domain
C
, we fix a metric that is equal to
ds^{2}+dt^{2}
in the cylindrical coordinates near each puncture
p_{e}
. We also equip the tangent bundle
TC
with a connection for which
\partial_{s}
is parallel in the cylindrical coordinates near each puncture.

Different choices of metrics or connections are commensurable, so these choices do not affect the topology.

A.2.1.
Weighted Sobolev norms.

Recall that the weighted Sobolev space
W^{k,p,\delta}(E)
of a bundle
E\to C_{\circ}
consists of those sections that decay at the rate
O(e^{\delta s})
near each cylindrical end of
C_{\circ}
. In particular, the weighted Sobolev norm
\|\cdot\|_{k,p,\delta}
has the usual
W^{k,p}
norm contribution away from the ends and near each cylindrical end of
C_{\circ}
the contribution is

\int(\lvert\xi\rvert^{p}+\lvert\nabla\xi\rvert^{p}+\cdots+\lvert\nabla^{k}\xi\rvert^{p})e^{p\delta s}ds\;dt

for a section
\xi
supported in the cylindrical end. The norm is finally constructed by the usual bump function trick.

A.2.2.
Floer Function

Given a point
(\nu,u_{0},w_{0})\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{T}^{\,\text{reg}}(\widehat{Y}),
and a gluing parameter,
\textbf{g},
we know that the pre-glued map,
u_{\textbf{g}}
, does not satisfy the equation
\bar{\partial}u_{\textbf{g}}+A(w_{0})(\cdot,u_{\textbf{g}}(\cdot))=0.
But the ‘defect’ of being a true solution can be explicitly measured by the following function in a neighborhood of
(u_{\textbf{g}},w_{\textbf{g}})
in
\Maps(C_{\textbf{g}},\widehat{Y}).

(A.13)

\mathscr{F}_{\textbf{g}}\colon W^{k,p,\delta}(C_{\textbf{g}},u_{\textbf{g}}^{*}T\widehat{Y}_{\textbf{g}})\oplus\mathscr{J}_{T_{v}}\oplus W\to W^{k-1,p,\delta}(C_{\textbf{g}},u_{\textbf{g}}^{*}T\widehat{Y}_{\textbf{g}}\otimes\Omega^{0,1}TC_{\textbf{g}})\oplus\mathbb{R}^{V(T_{\textbf{g}})}

\mathscr{F}_{\textbf{g}}(\xi,y,w):=(\text{P{T}}_{\exp_{u_{\textbf{g}}}\xi\to u_{\textbf{g}}}\otimes I_{y})\left(d(\exp_{u_{\textbf{g}}}\xi)+A(w+w_{0})(\cdot,\exp_{u_{\textbf{g}}}\xi(\cdot))\right)^{0,1}_{j_{y}}\oplus\bigoplus_{v\in V(T_{\textbf{g}})}\pi_{\mathbb{R}}(\exp_{u_{\textbf{g}}}\xi)(q^{\prime}(v)).

In the above equation, the exponential maps are taken with respect to an
\mathbb{R}-
equivariant metric on
\widehat{Y}
,
I_{y}
is the composition of the natural maps

\Omega^{0,1}_{C_{\textbf{g}},j_{y}}\to\Omega^{0,1}_{C_{\textbf{g}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{C}\to\Omega^{0,1}_{C_{\textbf{g}},j_{0}},

and the parallel transport
PT
is taken with respect to a fixed
\widehat{J}_{0}
linear connection which is
\mathbb{R}
equivariant.
We recall some estimates about the Floer function
\mathscr{F}_{\textbf{g}}
from
[
Par19
, §5]
.

Lemma A.8

(
[
Par19
, Lemma 5.5]
)
.

We have
\|\mathscr{F}_{\textbf{g}}(0)\|_{k-1,2,\delta}\to 0
as
\textbf{g}\to 0
for all
k\geq 1.

Lemma A.9

(
[
Par19
, Proposition 5.6]
)
.

For
\left\|\zeta\right\|_{k,2,\delta},\left\|\eta\right\|_{k,2,\delta}\leq c_{k,\delta}^{\prime}
, we have

(A.14)

\left\|\mathscr{F}_{\textbf{g}}^{\prime}(0,\eta)-\mathscr{F}_{\textbf{g}}^{\prime}(\zeta,\eta)\right\|_{k-1,2,\delta}\leq c_{k,\delta}\left\|\zeta\right\|_{k,2,\delta}\left\|\eta\right\|_{k,2,\delta}

for constants
0<c_{k,\delta}^{\prime},c_{k,\delta}<\infty
which are bounded uniformly in
g
near
0
, for all
k\geq 4
.

We denote the linearization of
\mathscr{F}_{\textbf{g}}
at
0
by
D_{\textbf{g}}
. Recall that, by assumption, the restriction of
D_{0}
to
W^{k,p,\delta}(u_{0}^{*}T\widehat{Y})\oplus W
is a surjective Fredholm operator. Thus, there is a right inverse
Q_{0}
. We now state the main kernel gluing and existence of right inverses.

Proposition A.10
.

There is a right inverse
Q_{\textbf{g}}
of
D_{\textbf{g}}
whose norm is bounded uniformly in
g
and, for sufficiently small
g
, an isomorphism of vector spaces

\Glue_{\ker}\colon\ker D_{0}\to\ker D_{\textbf{g}}.

Proof.

We omit the proof and refer the reader to
[
Par19
, §5.2.8]
for the construction of the right inverse
Q_{\textbf{g}}
. The kernel gluing isomorphism is constructed in Equation (5.40) in op. cit.
∎

By Equation (
A.14
), the derivative
\mathscr{F}_{\textbf{g}}^{\prime}(v,\cdot)
is surjective for
\|v\|_{k,p,\delta}<c_{k,\delta}
. Thus
\mathscr{F}_{\textbf{g}}^{-1}(0)
is a
C^{k-2}-
manifold, which is transverse to
\text{im}\;Q_{\textbf{g}}
.

Proposition A.11
.

The projection map
\proj:\mathscr{F}_{\textbf{g}}^{-1}(0)\to\ker D_{\textbf{g}}
along
\text{im}\;Q_{\textbf{g}}
is a local diffeomorphism whose image contains
0\in\ker D_{\textbf{g}}.

Proof.

This is shown in
[
Par19
, §5.3.1]
.
∎

A.3.
Gluing map

We can now define a gluing map
\Glue(\textbf{g},\cdot)\colon
The main gluing theorem in
[
Par19
]
proves the following result about local homeomorphism.

Proposition A.12
.

Fix a point
\nu
in the base-space
\mathcal{B}^{P}.

For a given
\textbf{g}\in G_{T_{\nu}/}\times\mathbb{C}^{N^{{}^{\mathrm{o}}}_{C_{0}}}
, the restriction of the gluing map

\Glue({\textbf{g}},\;\underline{\;\;\;}\;)\colon\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{T}^{\,\text{reg}}(\widehat{Y})|_{T_{\nu}}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{T}^{\,\text{reg}}(\widehat{Y})|_{T_{\Glue_{\mathcal{B}^{P}}(\nu,\textbf{g})}}

given by the equation

(A.15)

\Glue(\textbf{g},\;\underline{\;\;\;}\;)=\exp_{u_{\textbf{g}}}\circ\proj_{\ker D_{\textbf{g}}}^{-1}\circ\Glue_{\ker}\circ\proj_{\ker D_{0}}

is a local diffeomorphism.

Proof.

Fix a point
(u_{0},w_{0})\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{T}^{\,\text{reg}}(\widehat{Y})|_{\nu}
. For a fixed
g
, the gluing map is the composition of local diffeomorphisms and thus itself is a local diffeomorphism. The map
\proj_{\ker D_{0}}\colon\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{T}^{\,\text{reg}}(\widehat{Y})|_{\nu}\to\ker D_{0}
is the inverse of the local diffeomorphism
\ker D_{0}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{T}^{\,\text{reg}}(\widehat{Y})|_{\nu}
defined on a small neighborhood of the origin. The middle map
\Glue_{\ker}
is the kernel gluing map

\Glue_{\ker}\colon\ker D_{0}\to\ker D_{\textbf{g}},

and
\proj_{\ker D_{\textbf{g}}}
is the restriction of the projection map in Proposition
A.11
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
A.1
.

We will only prove the case of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}\to\mathcal{B}^{\mathbb{R}}
being an open embedding since the general result then follows from a similar argument as
[
AMS24
, Corollary A.2]
. Due to the strong regularity of
(\nu,u,w)
, there exists a neighborhood
B_{T_{\nu}}
of
\nu
in the submanifold
\mathcal{B}^{P}|_{T_{\nu}}
of domains of tree type
T_{\nu}
and a neighborhood
N
of
(\nu,u,w)
in the fiber
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{reg}_{T}(\widehat{Y})|_{(\nu,u,w)}
such that there is an embedding
B_{T_{\nu}}\times N\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{reg}_{T}(\widehat{Y})|_{T_{\nu}}
. Fix a chart in a neighborhood of
B_{T_{\nu}}
given by the map

\text{Glue}_{\mathcal{B}^{P}}:B|_{T_{\nu}}\times G_{T_{\nu}/}\to B\underset{open}{\subset}\mathcal{B}^{P},

as described in (
A.9
). Under the identification of a neighborhood of
(\nu,u,w)
in the fiber
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{reg}_{T}(\widehat{Y})|_{T_{\nu}}
with
B|_{T_{\nu}}\times N
, we have that the gluing map,
\Glue
can be written as

\Glue\colon B|_{T_{\nu}}\times N\times G_{T_{\nu}/}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{reg}_{T}(\widehat{Y}).

Now the result is a direct consequence of Proposition
A.12
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
A.2
.

We rephrase the proposition using the language developed in this appendix. Let
(\nu_{i},u_{i},w_{i})\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{reg}_{T}(\hat{Y}),\;i\in\{1,2\}
be a pair of points and let
\Glue^{i}
be gluing maps constructed as above. We also assume that these gluing neighborhoods intersect non-trivially. In particular, let
N_{i}\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{reg}_{T}(\hat{Y})|_{\nu_{i}}
be neighborhoods of
(\nu_{i},u_{i},w_{i})
in the fiber and suppose
B_{i}
are neighborhoods of
\nu_{i}
in the
\mathcal{B}^{P}
such that
B_{1}\cap B_{2}\neq\emptyset
and
\Glue^{1}(B_{1}\cap B_{2},N_{1})\cap\Glue^{2}(B_{1}\cap B_{2},N_{2})\neq\emptyset.
We can further assume that
N_{i}^{\prime}s
are open neighborhoods of
0
in the kernel of the respective linearization,
\ker D_{0}^{i}
. Thus, we can identify
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
with open neighborhoods of the origin in finite-dimensional Euclidean space. After potentially replacing
N_{1}
with a subset, we have a map

G:=(\Glue^{2})^{-1}\circ\Glue^{1}\colon(B_{1}\cap B_{2})\times N_{1}\to(B_{1}\cap B_{2})\times N_{2}.

Now it is enough to check that the map
G(g,\;\underline{\;\;}\;)\colon N_{1}\to N_{2}
depends continuously on
g
in the
C^{1}_{loc}
topology. By viewing the map
G
using the definition of gluing map as defined in Equation (
A.15
), we see that it is enough to check continuity of derivative of

\Glue_{\ker D^{2}_{0}}^{-1}\circ\proj_{\ker D^{2}_{g}}^{-1}\circ\exp_{u_{2},\textbf{g}}^{-1}\circ\exp_{u_{1},\textbf{g}}\circ\proj_{\ker D^{1}_{g}}^{-1}\circ\Glue_{\ker D^{1}_{0}}\colon N_{1}\to N_{2}

where
D^{i}_{\textbf{g}}
is defined similarly to
D_{\textbf{g}}
above. Now the result follows from the fact that the derivative of
\exp_{u_{2},\textbf{g}}^{-1}\circ\exp_{u_{1},\textbf{g}}
depends continuously on
g
and the construction of the right inverse
Q_{\textbf{g}}
depends continuously on
g
, see
[
Par19
, §5.2.7-5.2.8]
.
∎
